# Arcanum — API Reference

This document is the source of truth for Arcanum's native HTTP API, OpenAI-compatible API, wire formats, authentication behavior, request and response contracts, status mapping, and public error codes. It covers both general `/api` calls and the OpenAI-compatible `/v1` surface.

Architecture and design decisions belong in [`Arcanum.DESIGN.md`](Arcanum.DESIGN.md). The readable architecture guide is [`Arcanum.Design.Human.md`](Arcanum.Design.Human.md). The complete public configuration model belongs in [`Compendium.README.md`](Compendium.README.md#complete-configuration-reference). Complete CLI syntax and option behavior belong in [`Arcanum.Command.Reference.md`](Arcanum.Command.Reference.md).

The established §8 contract numbers are retained in this extracted reference so existing issue, test, and documentation citations remain stable.

## 1. Complete API surface

**API surface (`MapArcanumEndpoints`):**

| Method | Path | Contract/purpose |
|--------|------|-----------------|
| GET | `/metrics` | Prometheus text-format metrics. |
| GET | `/api/presence` | Anonymous, bodyless local-server challenge accepted only when both the raw TCP peer and the post-forwarding effective peer are loopback. The client supplies one 32-byte random nonce in `X-Arcanum-Presence-Nonce`; a ready host returns **204** with protocol version, canonical loopback authority, a nonce/authority-bound HMAC proof, and an encrypted short-lived process capability. The authority is derived from the server-side listener's actual TLS state and local port with the client-facing host fixed to `localhost`; caller-controlled `Host`, request scheme, and forwarded metadata cannot select it. It exposes no health, configuration, reusable credential, or installation data, accepts no API key, and returns **400** for malformed challenges, **404** for a non-loopback peer, or **503** until both the process-held proof key and a trustworthy listener identity are available. |
| GET | `/api/health` | Authenticated `ApiResponse<HealthReportDto>` readiness snapshot. Healthy/Degraded reports return **200**; a valid Unhealthy report remains a success envelope with its component detail and returns **503**. Components include **`Conclave`**, whose detail leads with `state=disabled\|configured\|degraded\|healthy` (DESIGN §5.7.1); only `degraded` affects readiness. |
| GET | `/api/meta` | Instance metadata and feature flags for sidecar discovery (`ApiResponse<InstanceMetadataDto>`). Carries `conclaveEnabled`, `a2AServerEnabled`, `a2AClientEnabled`, `conclaveA2AState`, `a2AServerPath`, `a2AAgentCardPath`, and `a2AAllowedRemoteAgentCount`. Every flag is read from the resolved configuration: `archiveSearchEnabled` follows `Arcanum:Features:ArchiveSearch`. A flag no setting can move is not reported: `loreSystemEnabled` (a constant `false` for a system that no longer exists) and `contextCompressionEnabled` / `tokenTrackingEnabled` (code-owned `true`, with no key that changes them) were removed from the wire. |
| POST | `/api/server/quit` | Accept an authenticated operator shutdown request (`202` + `ApiResponse<bool>`), then stop the host after the acknowledgement completes. |
| GET | `/api/budget` | Daily budget snapshot (`ApiResponse<BudgetSummaryDto>`: enabled, enforced, daily limit, today's spend, remaining, spent percent, alert threshold; DESIGN §22.2). `enforced` is true only when `enabled` is true and `dailyLimitUsd` is positive. Recorded spend is reported in every state; `remainingUsd` and `spentPercent` are zero placeholders when `enforced` is false, not an exhausted cap. Spend is split into `localSpendUsd` (this instance's own inference — committed billable operations for the UTC day plus outstanding reservations, the same authority the budget gate enforces on) and `externalSpendUsd` (delegated A2A work, as reported by peers), with `todaySpendUsd` their sum. `unpricedDelegatedSendings` counts Sendings that settled today whose peer reported **no** cost — counted, never costed, so a non-zero value means the spend figures are a floor rather than the whole bill. CLI `arcanum budget`. |
| GET | `/api/grimoire/stats` | Grimoire database statistics (`ApiResponse<GrimoireStatsDto>`; database + WAL byte sizes and per-table row counts via `GrimoireStatsService`). |
| GET | `/api/data/status` | Unified retained-data inventory (`ApiResponse<DataRetentionStatus>`): typed rows/files/estimated bytes, effective policy, physical store, safe provenance, aggregate totals, and categories preserved outside the selected root (§8.20). |
| GET | `/api/data/retention` | Read the effective `Arcanum:Retention` policy (`ApiResponse<RetentionSettings>`; §8.20). |
| PUT | `/api/data/retention` | Update one typed rule (`RetentionRuleUpdateRequest` → `ApiResponse<RetentionSettings>`); values are normalized and clamped by the server (§8.20). |
| POST | `/api/data/prune/plan` | Build the complete non-mutating selected plan (`DataRetentionRequest` → `ApiResponse<DataRetentionPlan>`), represented through internal checkpoint pages rather than a total candidate ceiling (§8.20). |
| POST | `/api/data/prune` | Re-plan and apply through a durable operation (`DataRetentionApplyRequest` → `ApiResponse<DataRetentionApplyResult>`; optional stale-plan guard; §8.20). A candidate that carries a live Covenant label is skipped at apply and left where it is, so a sweep still removes every other candidate; the plan is built before that check, so a dry run may name a candidate the apply then leaves. |
| DELETE | `/api/data/sessions/{id}` | Explicit dependency-aware session deletion (`ApiResponse<DataRetentionApplyResult>`; pins, holds, active work, and a Covenant-labelled assistant Entry all block; §8.20). A labelled Entry refuses the whole deletion with **403** `Covenant.ForbiddenAuthority`, because a Session delete is all or nothing and a Session removed around Entries that are still there is worse than one that stayed. A sensitivity label that cannot be read refuses it with **503** `Covenant.Unavailable` instead, since nothing was found to be protected and the protection could not be checked. The check is made inside the transaction that deletes, so a refusal leaves everything in place. |
| DELETE | `/api/data/attachments/{id}` | Explicit attachment-version deletion including owned bytes and derived indexes (`ApiResponse<DataRetentionApplyResult>`; §8.20). |
| POST | `/api/data/memory/reset/plan` | Build the non-mutating plan for exactly one `MemoryResetScope` from `MemoryResetRequest` (`ApiResponse<DataRetentionPlan>`). Optional `campaignId` previews only that Campaign's rows of a `Saga`, `Lexicon`, or `CampaignSummary` reset. Covenant returns five content-free aggregates under a protected planning lease (§8.20). |
| POST | `/api/data/memory/reset` | Reset exactly one `MemoryResetScope` from `MemoryResetRequest` (`ApiResponse<DataRetentionApplyResult>`). Optional `campaignId` clears only that Campaign's `Saga`, `Lexicon`, or `CampaignSummary` memories and leaves every other Campaign's and every installation-scoped one in place; naming it for any other scope is refused **400** `Data.InvalidRequest`. Covenant enters the durable protected-erasure coordinator directly under its V4 launch checkpoint and is never the nested arm of a broader workflow (§8.20). The untargeted `Saga` and `Lexicon` arms refuse with **403** `Covenant.ForbiddenAuthority` while the kind still holds any labelled member, because the statement behind them examines no identity and cannot leave one row out, and with **503** `Covenant.Unavailable` when the labels cannot be read. A Campaign-targeted `Saga` or `Lexicon` reset refuses the same way when any memory its Campaign predicate selects carries a live label: the question is asked inside the delete's transaction about exactly those memories, not about the label's recorded Campaign, and a refusal removes nothing. A selected memory whose identity is not a Guid cannot be asked about, so the reset refuses with **503** `Covenant.Unavailable` instead and removes nothing. A reset of any scope but `Covenant` whose preview selected nothing applies as a successful, reconciled no-op with zero counts, and the bundled CLI exits `0`; rows that appear after that empty preview are refused as **409** `Data.PlanChanged`, and a fresh preview is needed. |
| POST | `/api/data/factory-reset` | Reset managed data beneath the selected root from `FactoryResetRequest`; requires exact `confirmation: "factory-reset"`, composes healthy-catalog Covenant erasure with ordinary factory cleanup under its V2 launch checkpoint, and preserves backups/configuration/security material/out-of-root data (`ApiResponse<DataRetentionApplyResult>`; §8.20). An offline installation reset launches this same erasure as its nested arm under a parent-receipt binding; a direct call to this route is standalone work and binds nothing. |
| POST | `/api/data/factory-reset/plan` | Loopback-only data-phase planning for an installation reset. Accepts `Global` with no workspace binding or `Workspace` with an exact registered Campaign binding (`ApiResponse<DataRetentionPlan>`; §8.20). |
| GET | `/api/config` | Read the latest successfully persisted `ArcanumSettings`; provider endpoints remain redacted, while secret environment-variable references are returned without resolving their values (`ApiResponse<ArcanumSettings>`; §8.12). |
| PUT | `/api/config` | Validate and write a full settings snapshot to `arcanum.json` (`ApiResponse<bool>`; §8.12). |
| POST | `/api/config/validate` | Validate settings without writing (`ApiResponse<bool>`; §8.12). |
| GET | `/api/models` | Flatten models from the latest successfully persisted provider snapshot (`ApiResponse<ModelInfoDto[]>`; endpoint redacted as `"***"`; effective `supportsTools` is false only for an explicit `providers.models.supportsTools: false`; models on a Familiar provider's `hiddenModels` list are omitted; read-only, no connectivity checks; §8.12). |
| GET | `/api/providers` | List the latest successfully persisted providers with `apiKey`/`endpoint` redacted (`ApiResponse<ProviderInfoDto[]>`; `models` excludes the hide list and `hiddenModels` reports it; a Familiar reports an empty `endpoint` and `credentialEnvironmentVariable`; read-only; §8.12). |
| GET | `/api/providers/{name}/familiar-probe` | Familiar readiness for one `ClaudeCodeCli`/`CodexCli` provider (`ApiResponse<FamiliarProbeResult>`; asks the CLI for its own status, never for a completion, so it is non-billable; never reads the CLI's credential store and carries no account material; **404** `Provider.NotFound`, **400** `Validation.InvalidProviderType` for an OpenAI-compatible row; §8.12.1). |
| GET | `/api/perception/look` | Eye of the World snapshot (optional `directory` query; requires `Arcanum:Security:PerceptionWorkspaceRoots`; **403** when unset). |
| POST | `/api/perception/chronosync` | Analyze a `PatternSnapshot` body and persist the workspace-context baseline it implies (`ApiResponse<ChronosyncReport>` through `IChronosyncEngine.AnalyzeAndSyncAsync`). The body is validated before any analysis and a refusal carries the mapper's status for its code. Honors `Idempotency-Key` through the bound-argument filter (§11.17), so a second post under the same key with a different snapshot is **409** `Security.IdempotencyConflict`. |
| POST | `/api/intelligence/ping` | Buffered inference. |
| POST | `/api/intelligence/memory-delegation` | Buffered native parent inference with one bounded exact-version child memory grant (`MemoryDelegationRequest` → `ApiResponse<PromptResponseDto>`; §8.10.4). |
| POST | `/api/intelligence/ping-stream` | NDJSON streaming inference (same `PingRequest` extensions as buffered ping). |
| POST | `/api/intelligence/human-response` | Submit human-in-the-loop answer. |
| POST | `/api/intelligence/arsenal` | Spell names, metadata-only `SpellSummary[]`, native tools, and MCP server status. |
| POST | `/api/intelligence/mana` | Read-only diagnostic Mana (token) counter (`ApiResponse<ManaCountResult>`; body `ManaCountRequest` { `messages`, `prompt`, `model`, `tools` }). |
| POST | `/api/intelligence/context/inspect` | Effective-turn preview that never infers the main response (`ContextPreviewRequest` → `ApiResponse<ContextPreviewResult>`); with retrieval enabled it makes real auxiliary provider calls whose embeddings open their own `embedding` runs and ledger rows (§8.10.2), and `noRetrieval` makes it spend-free; accepts optional forced-Spell, preview-only text/image context, tool/system-prompt shaping, and inference-option fields while reusing production assembly components without main inference, tool invocation, attachment persistence, or assistant-entry persistence. |
| POST | `/api/web/search` | First-class bounded web search (`WebSearchWorkflowRequest` → `ApiResponse<WebSearchWorkflowResult>`; citations and provider usage; DESIGN §11.27). Optional `attachToSessionId` stores the result Markdown; the provider call is already billed by then, so a failed attachment answers **200** with `attachmentId: null` and a non-null `attachmentError` rather than discarding the answer. |
| POST | `/api/web/browse` | First-class bounded static page read (`WebBrowseWorkflowRequest` → `ApiResponse<WebBrowseWorkflowResult>`; JavaScript mode degrades explicitly when no renderer is configured; DESIGN §11.27). `attachToSessionId` behaves as on `/api/web/search`: a failed attachment reports `attachmentError` and still returns the page. |
| POST | `/api/web/research` | Server-owned progress-driven research as NDJSON `WebResearchStreamFrame` lines (`limits`, `progress`, `result`, `error`); the request can carry effective Campaign/Workspace/Session/Model context, current-turn text/image context, and synthesis sampling controls (DESIGN §11.27). |
| GET | `/api/mcp` | List managed MCP servers (`ApiResponse<McpServerInfo[]>`; DESIGN §5.6). |
| GET | `/api/mcp/{name}` | One managed MCP server (`ApiResponse<McpServerInfo>`); optional `workingDirectory` query for disambiguation. |
| POST | `/api/mcp/{name}/start` | Start one MCP server (`ApiResponse<bool>`); optional `workingDirectory` query. |
| POST | `/api/mcp/{name}/stop` | Stop one MCP server (`ApiResponse<bool>`); optional `workingDirectory` query. A stop is committed once the client is gone, so a caller that disconnects mid-stop does not turn it into a failure; 400 `Mcp.ClientDisposalIncomplete` means the old client is still shutting down. |
| POST | `/api/mcp/{name}/restart` | Restart one MCP server (`ApiResponse<bool>`); optional `workingDirectory` query. A restart cancelled after the old server stopped answers 400 `Mcp.RestartCanceled`, and the message says what the server was left in (`Stopped`, or `Error` with the half-started client disposed); a restart that finds the old client still shutting down answers 400 `Mcp.ClientDisposalIncomplete`. |
| POST | `/api/mcp/trust-workspace/preview` | What trusting a workspace-local `mcp.json` would allow, read by the host from its own copy of the file (`ApiResponse<McpWorkspaceTrustPreview>`; body `{ "workingDirectory": "..." }`; DESIGN §5.6). `McpWorkspaceTrustPreview` is `{ workspace, lines, truncated, configDigest }`: `workspace` is the normalized root the host would trust, `lines` list each server with the transport the host will actually use (the inference the connection manager applies: an explicit `stdio`, `http` or `sse` type wins, otherwise a `url` means `http`) and the URL that transport dials or the command line it runs, then any authored command or URL that transport ignores (marked `not run` / `not used`) and an unrecognised `type`, working directory and environment variable names (never values), already made safe to print (whitespace runs collapse to one space, an argument with a space or quote is double-quoted, trailing whitespace in an argument stays as one space inside the quotes, and a control, direction-changing, zero-width or blank-looking character is written as `<U+XXXX>`), `truncated` is true when a field was longer than the 4096 characters one field shows (so the preview cannot be the basis for approval), and `configDigest` is the upper-case SHA-256 hex of the exact bytes the preview describes. Failures: `Mcp.MissingWorkspace`, `Mcp.InvalidWorkspace`, `Mcp.MissingConfig` (no file), `Mcp.InvalidConfig` (not JSON the host can describe, including a null server entry or a null `args` / `inheritEnv` element), `Mcp.TrustFailed` (too large or not a safe regular file); each is **400**. |
| POST | `/api/mcp/trust-workspace` | Approve a workspace-local `mcp.json` for auto-start (`ApiResponse<bool>`; body `{ "workingDirectory": "...", "expectedConfigDigest": "..." }`; DESIGN §5.6). `expectedConfigDigest` is optional and is the `configDigest` of the preview the operator approved: when present the host trusts the file only if the bytes it reads now have that digest, and otherwise answers **400** `Mcp.ConfigChanged` and records nothing; when absent it trusts whatever the file holds when it reads it. The CLI always sends it. |
| POST | `/api/mcp/reload` | Reload MCP connections (global nuclear reload — DESIGN §5.6). |
| POST | `/api/mcp/tools/invoke` | **Diagnostic MCP Invocation** — policy-constrained direct invoke of an **external** MCP tool by an operator (`ApiResponse<McpToolInvokeResponse>`). Development edition only; otherwise **404** `Mcp.DiagnosticDisabled`. |
| GET | `/api/sessions` | Search/list Grimoire sessions (`ApiResponse<SessionQueryResult>`; DESIGN §11.16). `status` accepts trimmed, case-insensitive `active`, `archived`, or `all`; absent/blank defaults to `active`. An unknown status is **400** `Session.InvalidStatus`. The Campaign sessions route uses the same validation and canonical native filtering. |
| POST | `/api/sessions` | Create a Session and its immutable canonical Campaign binding atomically (`ApiResponse<SessionDetailDto>`; **201**; a missing Campaign is **404** `Campaign.NotFound`, including a Campaign removed before commit). Optional `title` is nullable and trimmed; blank becomes null, with no generated-title 200-character cap. Creation does not adopt a legacy unbound Session (DESIGN §11.16). |
| GET | `/api/sessions/analytics` | Session analytics (`ApiResponse<SessionAnalytics>`; DESIGN §11.16). |
| GET | `/api/sessions/{id}` | Session metadata (`ApiResponse<SessionDetailDto>`; **404** when missing). |
| GET | `/api/sessions/{id}/entries` | Entry history (`ApiResponse<EntryDto[]>`; optional `offset`, `limit`, and keyset cursor parameters `beforeCreatedAt` / `beforeId`, which must be supplied together or not at all: either one alone is **400** `Validation.InvalidQuery` rather than being ignored). `?countOnly=true` returns `ApiResponse<SessionEntryCountDto>` instead of entry rows; it reads no page, but a half cursor sent with it is still the same **400**, checked before the count. Both forms acquire conditional Covenant read authority before the Session read. When Covenant is enabled, the original generation and operator authority are revalidated before the first response byte and retained through complete serialization; responses carry `Cache-Control: no-store, private`, `Pragma: no-cache`, `Expires: 0` and no `ETag` or `Last-Modified`. With Covenant disabled, a Session retaining protected artifacts is refused with **403** `Covenant.ForbiddenAuthority` before its transcript is read; clean Sessions remain readable. |
| POST | `/api/sessions/{id}/entries` | Append entry manually (**404** / **400**; publishes live SSE only after a successful append). `role` must be supplied as `system`, `user`, `assistant`, or `tool` (defined numeric enum values 0–3 remain accepted); omitted, null, or undefined role is **400** `Validation.InvalidBody` with role guidance. An explicit `system` is valid. Valid-role empty content remains **400** `Session.EmptyContent`. A refusal writes no Entry. |
| PATCH | `/api/sessions/{id}` | Update title or status (**404** `Session.NotFound`; **400** `Session.InvalidStatus`). Only the fields the body supplies are written, in one statement: a title-only patch never rewrites the status, so it cannot overwrite a status another request changed after this one read the Session. A supplied blank title clears it. |
| DELETE | `/api/sessions/{id}` | Archive session (**204**; soft delete). |
| GET | `/api/sessions/{id}/export` | Export JSON or Markdown (`ApiResponse<SessionExportResult>`). Required `format` is `json` or `markdown`, matched without regard to case; anything else is **400** `Session.InvalidFormat`. A Session whose ledger tables cannot be read (one is missing) is **503** `Covenant.ManualRecoveryRequired` rather than exportable. A Session carrying any Covenant-derived artifact is refused with **403** `Covenant.PlaintextExportRefused` **before** the transcript is read, whether `Arcanum:Features:Covenant` is on or has since been turned off (with it off the same two content-free ledger reads run without a lease) ([DESIGN §10.19.11](Arcanum.DESIGN.md#101911-refusing-tainted-plaintext-export-and-reporting-what-a-campaign-export-left-behind)). |
| POST | `/api/sessions/{id}/rest` | Enqueue Campaign Log consolidation (**202** + `ApiResponse<bool>` when accepted; **503** + `Session.RestQueueFull` when the bounded queue rejects). |
| GET | `/api/sessions/{id}/stream` | SSE replay + live entry stream; optional `since=<entry-guid>` resumes after an Entry in that Session. A missing or foreign cursor returns **404** `Session.EntryNotFound` before SSE headers are written. |
| GET | `/api/sessions/{id}/attachments` | Revalidate tracked sources asynchronously, then list **bound** session attachments (`ApiResponse<SessionAttachmentDto[]>`; includes `indexingStatus`, the snapshot `RelativePath` for Reveal, and sanitized source provenance/refreshability; never an absolute source path; DESIGN §10.2.5). Revalidation observes each source's status, digest, length and write time and takes no Sanctum decision (DESIGN §11.15); only the live reference and the refresh (the operator route and the `refresh_session_file` tool) read a source's content. |
| POST | `/api/sessions/{id}/attachments` | Create a snapshot-only bound attachment from multipart field `file` (optional `logicalName` form field); returns `201` + `ApiResponse<SessionAttachmentDto>`. The filename and declared MIME are hints; the server validates name, detected content, kind-specific byte limit, MIME/Scrying policy, strict encoding for text, and Session byte/version budgets. Crossing a budget is **409** `Attachment.LimitExceeded`, reported only for the store's typed limit refusal; any other persistence failure is a server fault (**500**), never a limit. Content outside the reviewed Comprehension formats (legacy binary Office, RTF, EPUB, templates, unknown binaries), and a reviewed format while Comprehension is not effectively enabled, remains a valid `Binary` attachment with `NotEligible` indexing status; with Comprehension effectively enabled, the reviewed document formats, PDF among them, use the existing extraction statuses (§8.18.1), and a PDF with no text layer is `NotEligible`. |
| POST | `/api/sessions/{id}/attachments/reference` | Create a refreshable live reference from `CreateSessionAttachmentReferenceRequest` (`workspacePath`, optional `workspaceId`, optional `logicalName`); the server alone resolves/authorizes/reads the source and persists the already-verified bytes (a resolution whose bytes disagree with its own recorded hash or provenance is a server fault, **500**, not a limit); a Session byte/version budget is **409** `Attachment.LimitExceeded`; returns `201` + `ApiResponse<SessionAttachmentDto>`. |
| GET | `/api/sessions/{id}/attachments/{attachmentId}/content` | Stream the authenticated plaintext of that stored bound snapshot with `Content-Disposition: attachment`, `Cache-Control: no-store`, and `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`. Never returns or redirects to a live source path. |
| POST | `/api/sessions/{id}/attachments/{attachmentId}/refresh` | Operator-triggered secure refresh through the same source-validation/persistence core as `refresh_session_file`; returns `ApiResponse<AttachmentRefreshEvent>` only after the backend has reused or persisted the confirmed current version. |
| GET | `/api/sessions/{id}/context-pins` | List durable, structured session context pins. |
| POST | `/api/sessions/{id}/context-pins` | Create or update a context pin by `(session, kind, stable target)`; accepts file, directory snapshot, symbol/range, session entry, attachment, URL, and diagnostic kinds. Without a display label, a target longer than 160 UTF-16 units is shortened at a surrogate-safe boundary within 157 units plus `...`; the stored target remains complete. Explicit labels retain their own validation. |
| DELETE | `/api/sessions/{id}/context-pins/{pinId}` | Remove a durable context pin without changing `Entries.IsPinned` (**204**; **404** `Session.PinNotFound` in the standard envelope when the Session holds no such pin). |
| POST | `/api/sessions/{id}/fork` | Create an independent branch; `upToEntryId` is inclusive, `beforeEntryId` is exclusive, and supplying both fails with **400** `Session.InvalidForkCutoff`. Optional `expectedCopyFingerprint` rejects a changed preview with **409** `Session.ForkChanged` (**201** on success; DESIGN §11.16.1). The immutable source binding is retained unless an explicit live destination Campaign is resolved; missing/corrupt source binding refuses the fork. |
| POST | `/api/sessions/{id}/fork/preview` | Read-only fork selection using `ForkSessionRequest`; returns `ApiResponse<SessionForkPreviewDto>` with `attachmentCount`, `attachmentBytes`, and opaque `copyFingerprint`. Cutoff ownership is validated exactly as for copying. |
| POST | `/api/embeddings/reset` | Truncate embedding tables for RAG dimension-change recovery (requires `?confirm=true`; optional `?scope=all\|entry\|workspaceFile\|saga\|sessionAttachment\|tapestry`, default `all` only when `scope` is omitted; explicit empty/whitespace or unknown scopes return **400** `Validation.InvalidBody` before reset work; snake-case aliases accepted). `tapestry` removes `tapestry_generations`, `tapestry_nodes`, `tapestry_edges`, `tapestry_node_embeddings` and any reachable plain `tapestry_node_embeddings_vec` rows; returned per-table counts are captured before generation cascades. Source corpora, source-owned leaf hashes and monotonic scope revisions remain, and the background sweep rebuilds the trees (DESIGN §21.11). Unsupported legacy `vec0` mirrors are never opened and report zero; this does not claim physical removal of their inaccessible residue. A protected artifact the reset could not erase stops it with **503** `Covenant.ManualArtifactErasureRequired` before any table is truncated, unless the kernel blocked it on an environmental storage condition (a database still busy or locked, an I/O error, a full disk, a database that refuses writes), which answers **503** `Covenant.Unavailable` whether or not an item had already been erased; a plan-declared table missing from the schema is not one of those and answers `Covenant.ManualArtifactErasureRequired`. A refusal from the purge walk before it erased anything keeps its own code and the status the central mapper gives it (for example **409** `Covenant.StaleSnapshot` when a label or the operator authority changed while it was being purged). A walk stopped after it had already erased an item keeps those items erased and answers **503** `Covenant.Unavailable` when storage, a cancellation, or an unexpected exception stopped it, and **503** `Covenant.ManualArtifactErasureRequired` for any other cause, still before any table is truncated; a sensitivity label that cannot be read, or whose row cannot be parsed, stops it with **503** `Covenant.Unavailable`, which is the Grimoire's to repair and not an artifact to erase by hand. Only typed purge blockers produce manual-erasure guidance. A Grimoire maintenance refusal retains **503** `Grimoire.MaintenanceUnavailable`, and an unrelated unexpected failure uses the central sanitized **500** `Hub.Unhandled` response. The truncation asks the label guard once more, for each labelled kind it clears (Entry embeddings and Saga memories), inside its own transaction and before any table is touched: a labelled member that appeared since the walk answers **403** `Covenant.ForbiddenAuthority`, and a label table that cannot be read there answers **503** `Covenant.Unavailable`, and either removes nothing. |
| DELETE | `/api/sessions/{id}/entries/{entryId}` | Delete a single entry and its `entry_embeddings` / optional vector row in the same transaction (**204**). A Covenant-labelled assistant Entry is removed through the sensitivity purge first (DESIGN §10.20.2): **409** `Covenant.StaleSnapshot` when its label or the operator authority changed while it was being purged, **503** `Covenant.Unavailable` when its sensitivity label cannot be read or the kernel met an environmental storage condition (a database still busy or locked, an I/O error, a full disk, a database that refuses writes), **403** `Covenant.ForbiddenAuthority` when its label is found and the request holds no sensitivity-purge authority, and **503** `Covenant.ManualArtifactErasureRequired` when the kernel could not erase it for any other reason (a plan-declared table missing from the schema among them) and left it unchanged. The delete then asks the label guard again inside its own transaction: a label written since the purge answers **403** `Covenant.ForbiddenAuthority`, and a label table that cannot be read answers **503** `Covenant.Unavailable`; either leaves the Entry in place. |
| POST | `/api/sessions/{id}/entries/{entryId}/pin` | Atomically pin an entry within the code-owned per-Session cap. An already-pinned entry succeeds at the cap; a new pin over the cap returns **409** `Session.TooManyPinned`, a missing or concurrently vanished entry returns **404** `Session.EntryNotFound`, and an implementation without atomic pin admission returns **503** `Session.PinUnavailable`. Pinned entries remain included in inference context. |
| DELETE | `/api/sessions/{id}/entries/{entryId}/pin` | Atomically unpin an existing entry; repeated unpin succeeds. Missing or concurrently vanished entries return **404** `Session.EntryNotFound`; unavailable atomic admission returns **503** `Session.PinUnavailable`. |
| POST | `/api/sessions/{id}/compact` | Manually compress session context using the latest persisted assistant-answer model in canonical Entry.Sequence order, excluding user/request hints and assistant tool-call representations. Preserve the full recorded model ID, including slashes. One captured configuration supplies both tokenizer and provider window. A recorded model must be explicitly declared on its applicable provider, including a Familiar: wildcard inference routing is not proof for destructive compaction. The selected provider window uses the existing read-time clamp before compression; nonpositive values cannot silently invoke the explicit-window primitive's 8192 fallback. Default model behavior applies only when no assistant model is recorded; a newest empty/whitespace reply, missing newest attribution despite older recorded models, unresolved recorded model, or conflicting duplicate-provider tokenizer/window policies returns **409** `Session.CompactionUnavailable` with recovery guidance and no deletion. Identical applicable policies across duplicate providers are permitted; no historical provider identity is inferred. Empty placeholders cannot prove finalization and are never skipped to an older/smaller model. Successful admission deletes the oldest non-pinned entries until the token count is below the effective threshold (`ApiResponse<CompactResult>`: `tokensBefore`, `tokensAfter`, `entriesRemoved`, `stoppedBy`, and `outcome`). A refused erasure of a Covenant-labelled entry stops the compaction outright and removes none of the unlabelled remainder, and the answer is still **200**: `entriesRemoved` counts every entry already gone, including ones erased before the stop, each confirmed against the stored entry rather than the loaded window (a lower bound only when the session itself cannot be read again after the stop), `tokensAfter` is measured after them, and `stoppedBy` carries the typed code of the refusal — `Covenant.StaleSnapshot` when a label or the operator authority changed while it was being purged, `Covenant.ManualArtifactErasureRequired` when the kernel could not erase an entry for any reason other than an environmental storage condition (a plan-declared table missing from the schema among them) and left it unchanged, or the purge's own refusal: `Covenant.ForbiddenAuthority` when a label is found and the request holds no sensitivity-purge authority, `Covenant.InvalidScope` when the labelled entries of one page belong to more than one owner scope, or `Covenant.Unavailable` when a sensitivity label cannot be read or the kernel met an environmental storage condition (a database still busy or locked, an I/O error, a full disk, a database that refuses writes). `stoppedBy` is `null` when no protected erasure refusal stopped compaction. The additive string `outcome` is `Compacted`, `NotNeeded` (already within the threshold), `TooFewMessages` (below the minimum message count), `OnlyPinnedRemain` (still over the threshold with no unpinned entries left in the loaded context), or `Stopped` (a protected erasure refusal). Short-context results retain measured token counts instead of reporting a nonempty context as zero. Counts describe the repository-loaded context window, not a full-history census; `OnlyPinnedRemain` can follow actual removals. Older hosts may omit `outcome`, which clients treat as unknown rather than guessing a reason. An ordinary entry delete inside the compaction asks the labelled-artifact guard again inside its own transaction, so a label written after the purge's dispatch refuses that delete outright instead of being counted as a stop: the route then answers the guard's own error with the status its code maps to — **403** `Covenant.ForbiddenAuthority` for a labelled entry, **503** `Covenant.Unavailable` when the label table cannot be read — in the `ApiResponse<string>` envelope, never a **500** `Hub.Unhandled`. Entries already deleted before the refusal stay deleted, and compacting again continues from there (DESIGN §10.20.2). |
| POST | `/api/sessions/divine` | Session Divination — semantic search over Grimoire entries embedded by `EntryWeavingService` (`ApiResponse<SemanticSearchResult>`; body `SemanticSearchRequest` with required `query` and optional `campaignId`, `status` (trimmed and case-insensitive `active` or `archived`; omitted defaults to `active`, other values including explicit blank and `all` are **400** `Validation.InvalidBody`), and `limit` (clamped 1–50)). The `campaignId` and `status` filters are applied to the Session each hit belongs to, and the search first looks at the 4× `limit` closest candidates and, when fewer than `limit` + 1 of them survive the filters and the ranking was not exhausted, searches once more over the 500 closest (only candidates the first pass did not already read are joined, and only each hit's 200-character preview is read), so a Campaign whose closest matches rank below other Campaigns' entries is still answered; the installation's embeddings similarity threshold is applied to every candidate and is never relaxed by the widening. `hasMore` is true when more matching hits exist than `limit` returned, or when the 500-entry window filled before the search could rule that out; it is never false while another matching hit could exist. There is no cursor, so `nextCursor` is always null. |
| GET | `/api/lore` | List lore entries (`ApiResponse<ListPageResult<LoreDto>>`; paginated with optional `?limit=` and `?offset=`; the default page size is code-owned). |
| GET | `/api/lore/entries/by-key` | Get lore by exact query-encoded `?key={key}`; trim surrounding whitespace, then require 1–256 characters. Returns `ApiResponse<LoreDto>`, 400 `Validation.InvalidKey`, or 404 `Grimoire.LoreNotFound`. |
| GET | `/api/lore/{key}` | Legacy single-segment key route; same envelope and normalization. |
| POST | `/api/lore` | Upsert lore entry. Keys are nonempty after trimming and at most 256 characters; slashes, backslashes, dots and other URI characters remain valid. |
| DELETE | `/api/lore/entries/by-key` | Delete lore by exact query-encoded `?key={key}`; same key normalization and errors as query GET. Returns `ApiResponse<bool>` with `data: true` on success. |
| DELETE | `/api/lore/{key}` | Legacy single-segment key route; same envelope and normalization. |
| GET | `/api/saga` | Paginated listing of Saga memories (`ApiResponse<SagaMemoryDto[]>`; optional `?q=` Unicode ordinal case-insensitive literal substring, `?sessionId=`, `?limit=` clamped to 1–10,000, and non-negative `?offset=`). With `?sessionId=` an unknown Session returns **404** `Session.NotFound`; an existing Session with no memories returns **200** with an empty list. The listing is narrowed by the same ownership a turn in that session's scope ranks by; without it an operator sees the whole store. Ownership is what the two surfaces share, not eligibility: this reads the memory rows themselves, so retired memories remain included alongside live memories even though no turn can recall a retired memory. Every row reports its own `scopeKind` and `scopeCampaignId` either way, and its `retiredAtUtc` and `pinnedAtUtc` (DESIGN §21.9). Newest first, and memories that share an instant are ordered by identity, descending, so the order among them is the same on every request while the store is unchanged. |
| POST | `/api/saga/divine` | Semantic search over Saga memories (`ApiResponse<SagaSearchResult>`; body `SagaSearchRequest` with required `query`, optional `limit`, and optional `sessionId`; **503** when the embedding provider is unavailable). `sessionId` searches *as* that session: the scope comes from that session's immutable Campaign binding, never from anything else in the request. With Campaign scoping enabled and no `sessionId`, the search sees installation-scoped memories only (DESIGN §21.9). An explicitly unknown `sessionId` is **404** `Session.NotFound` before embedding work; existing feature/query validation keeps precedence and an omitted Session retains operator search behavior. |
| DELETE | `/api/saga/{id}` | Delete a single Saga memory (**204**; **404** `Saga.NotFound`; DESIGN §21.9). A Covenant-labelled memory is removed through the sensitivity purge (DESIGN §10.20.2): **409** `Covenant.StaleSnapshot` when its label or the operator authority changed while it was being purged, **503** `Covenant.Unavailable` when its sensitivity label cannot be read or the kernel met an environmental storage condition (a database still busy or locked, an I/O error, a full disk, a database that refuses writes), **403** `Covenant.ForbiddenAuthority` when its label is found and the request holds no sensitivity-purge authority, and **503** `Covenant.ManualArtifactErasureRequired` when the kernel could not erase it for any other reason (a plan-declared table missing from the schema among them) and left it unchanged. Every one of those refusals leaves the memory unchanged. The delete asks the label guard again inside its own transaction once the purge is done, so a label written in between answers **403** `Covenant.ForbiddenAuthority`, and a label table that cannot be read answers **503** `Covenant.Unavailable` there as well. An unsuppressed delete, not erasure: no fingerprint is recorded, so extraction or an agent can write the same content again; see `/api/memory/{store}/erase` (§8.35). |
| DELETE | `/api/saga` | Delete every Saga memory, embedding, and extraction watermark (**204**; requires `?confirm=true`, else **400** `Saga.NotEmpty`; DESIGN §21.9). Covenant-labelled memories are removed through the sensitivity purge first (DESIGN §10.20.2): **409** `Covenant.StaleSnapshot` when a labelled memory's label or the operator authority changed while it was being purged, **503** `Covenant.Unavailable` when a sensitivity label cannot be read or the kernel met an environmental storage condition (a database still busy or locked, an I/O error, a full disk, a database that refuses writes), **403** `Covenant.ForbiddenAuthority` when a label is found and the request holds no sensitivity-purge authority, **400** `Covenant.InvalidScope` when the labelled memories of one page belong to more than one owner scope, and **503** `Covenant.ManualArtifactErasureRequired` when the kernel could not erase a memory for any other reason (a plan-declared table missing from the schema among them) and left it unchanged. Each of those refusals stops the deletion before the store is emptied; labelled memories already purged stay purged, and every other memory is left in place. The emptying statement asks the label guard once more inside its own transaction: a labelled member that appeared since the purge answers **403** `Covenant.ForbiddenAuthority`, and a label table that cannot be read answers **503** `Covenant.Unavailable`, and either removes nothing. The evidence retirement leaves behind survives this, deliberately: a suppression digest names no memory and outlives the one it describes, so emptying the store does not re-enable extraction the operator rejected. An unsuppressed delete, not erasure: no fingerprint is recorded, so extraction or an agent can write the same content again; see `/api/memory/{store}/erase` (§8.35). `POST /api/data/memory/reset` with the `saga` scope is what clears them: a whole-store reset takes the digests and the key together, and one naming a Campaign takes that Campaign's digests and leaves the key every other scope's digests are still bound by (§8.20, DESIGN §21.13). |
| GET | `/api/saga/stats` | Aggregate Saga memory summary (`ApiResponse<SagaStats>`: total count, session count, oldest/newest `CreatedAt`; DESIGN §21.9). |
| GET | `/api/memory/status` | Global gates and persisted counts for each distinct memory store (`ApiResponse<MemoryStatusDto>`), including the `Tapestry` row gated by `Arcanum:Features:Tapestry`. Reads are not blocked when a prompt-time feature is disabled. The Tapestry count includes only **published** generations — a staging generation is invisible here for the same reason it is invisible to retrieval. |
| GET | `/api/memory/status/{sessionId}` | Session-narrowed memory status; **404** when the session is missing. Session-owned entries, pins, Summary, attachments/chunks, Saga, Campaign-backed workspace index, and session-scoped Tapestry trees (both the Session history tree and the attachment tree, which key one Session under different spellings) are counted independently; installation-scoped workspace trees are excluded from a session-narrowed count. |
| GET | `/api/memory/sources[/{sessionId}]` | Safe provenance and retention descriptions plus gates/counts (`ApiResponse<MemorySourcesDto>`); no attachment bytes or host absolute paths. |
| POST | `/api/memory/search` | Case-insensitive persisted-memory inspection (`MemorySearchRequest` → `ApiResponse<MemorySearchResponse>`). `scope` is `session`, `attachments`, `workspace`, `saga`, `lexicon`, or `all`; default `all` is echoed. Optional `sessionId`/`workspaceId` narrow ownership. When scope includes `workspace`, a nonblank `workspaceId` accepts the ID returned by `/api/workspaces` in any GUID format, or a case-insensitive registered name when the selector is not a GUID; surrounding whitespace is trimmed. An unknown selector returns **404** `Workspace.NotFound` before result or byte budgets can skip that scope. Omitted or blank selectors search every indexed workspace; scopes that exclude `workspace` ignore this selector. Every result includes scope, source id, provenance, and retention. Saga matching uses .NET ordinal case-insensitive literal substrings, including non-ASCII case pairs; accents and canonical Unicode spellings remain distinct. Saga and Lexicon hits additionally carry a typed read-only handoff to that store's exact `show` operation; other hits carry no action. Saga hits also carry `sagaLifecycle` (`retiredAtUtc`, `pinnedAtUtc`) and `sagaEligibility` (written as its name, §8.30), read in the same statement as the row, and a retired hit's `provenance` ends `; retired`: search reads the memory rows, so a retired memory matches exactly as a live one does. Search performs no mutation, embedding, or promotion side effect, and Covenant stays excluded. The `attachments` scope reads the stored attachment index chunks and applies no Comprehension gate: it is an operator inspection surface, not model context, so text extracted from a document while `Arcanum:Features:Comprehension` was on stays searchable after the gate is turned off. One request returns at most 10,000 results in total — a single budget shared by every scope, not one per scope — consumed in scope order (`session`, `attachments`, `workspace`, `saga`, `lexicon`). Optional `limit` narrows that budget (never widens it); a value outside `[1, 10000]` is refused **400** `Validation.InvalidBody` rather than clamped, so a full page is never mistaken for a complete one. The response carries `scopes` — one `{ scope, count, hasMore }` entry per requested scope, in phase order, including exhausted earlier and unvisited later scopes when continuing — and a top-level `hasMore` that is true when any of them was truncated. A scope the budget was already exhausted before reaching is reported as `count: 0, hasMore: true`, which is how a starved scope is told apart from an empty one. The response is also bounded in bytes: one request returns at most 8 MiB of hit `content` (UTF-8) in total, again a single budget shared by every scope. The budget is checked after each row, so the answer may exceed it by the one row that crossed it, and a scope that stopped reading on it reports `hasMore: true` (it cannot know it left nothing behind without reading another row). Narrow the query or read a hit in full through its `show` handoff or its own route. Entries, Session summaries, attachment chunks and workspace chunks use the same Unicode ordinal case-insensitive literal comparison as Saga and Lexicon; accents and canonical Unicode spellings remain distinct, and punctuation, wildcards and FTS operator words remain text. `continuation` is an optional trailing request/response member; repeat the same trimmed query, scope, selectors and current effective policy, with an adjustable `limit`, to continue. An invalid, mismatched or malformed explicit cursor returns **400** `Validation.InvalidBody`. Lexicon cursors carry only row identity/scope, and resolve the current name inside the owning authorized inspection snapshot; a deleted, moved or no-longer-matching boundary returns **400** `Validation.InvalidQuery` with restart guidance. A rename resumes after the boundary's current native name order. There is no cross-request snapshot: static-store traversal has no gaps/duplicates; concurrent changes can affect later pages. `truncationReason` is null when exhausted, or the closed string `ResultLimit`, `ContentBytes`, `ExaminedRows`, `ExaminedBytes`, or `StatementLimit`. Empty pages may have `hasMore: true` and a continuation. Four native corpora share a work budget of 512 examined rows and 2 MiB of examined searchable-field bytes, charged to nonmatches and sparse candidates before full matching/hydration, with one whole-row overshoot. At most three deduplicated printable-ASCII trigram seed queries collect at most 257 IDs each before ownership filtering or sorting; only a complete set of at most 256 is used as sparse candidates. Seeds may come from an interior ASCII window of mixed Unicode text. Queries without a safe seed (including pure Unicode and NUL-containing queries), or with only oversized candidate sets, use indexed ordered seek windows with the same work bounds and continuation; a nonmatching unfinished page does not prove absence. Statement work is also bounded. These limits cover the four native corpora; Saga selection and Lexicon's complete authority/structural verification retain their existing CPU characteristics. A pending or unhealthy native search projection returns **503** `MemorySearch.IndexUnavailable`, never an authoritative empty answer. Cursors contain no raw absolute workspace root or protected Lexicon name/content and confer no authority; selectors and current read/export admission are checked afresh. |
| GET | `/api/memory/explain[/{sessionId}]` | Explains conditional next-turn eligibility by source (`ApiResponse<MemoryExplainDto>`), distinguishing persisted data from actual inclusion. Saga is eligible only when at least one memory that is not retired, not suppressed by an exact-current-source Long Rest projection, not dormant or outside validity while Long Rest is enabled, and still has an embedding is retrievable in the scope below — every such memory when Campaign scoping is off, the installation-scoped ones and the resolved Campaign's own when it is on — not whenever rows exist; `/api/memory/status` keeps counting every stored row. `campaignScope` names the Campaign scope a turn would draw memory from before one runs — `Installation`, `GlobalOnly`, or `Campaign` with its id — resolved through the same seam retrieval uses. `/api/memory/status` carries the same block, and every `/api/memory/search` result carries it too. |
| GET | `/api/memory/lexicon` | Lists every retained Lexicon entity, including retired rows with lifecycle and eligibility markers (`ApiResponse<LexiconListDto>`); optional `?q=` searches name, type, and facts without the prompt-time match cap. |
| GET | `/api/memory/lexicon/{name}` | Effective active case-insensitive Lexicon lookup (`ApiResponse<LexiconEntryDto>`; **404** `Lexicon.NotFound`). Optional `?campaignId=` reads active Campaign then active Global. A retired Campaign row does not shadow Global. Omitted means active Global only. |
| POST | `/api/memory/lexicon/show` | Inspect one exact scope/name, including retired content, with a complete curation target (`ApiResponse<LexiconEntryDetail>`; §8.33). Never falls back to Global. |
| POST | `/api/memory/lexicon/correct` | Replace an exact target's type and complete ordered facts (`ApiResponse<LexiconCurationResult>`; §8.33). |
| POST | `/api/memory/lexicon/retire` | Remove the exact entry from operational retrieval while retaining inspectable content/history (`ApiResponse<LexiconCurationResult>`; §8.33). |
| POST | `/api/memory/lexicon/reinstate` | Restore a retired exact entry's eligibility (`ApiResponse<LexiconCurationResult>`; §8.33). |
| POST | `/api/memory/lexicon/pin` | Exempt the exact entry from automatic retention (`ApiResponse<LexiconCurationResult>`; §8.33). |
| POST | `/api/memory/lexicon/unpin` | Release the exact entry's retention exemption (`ApiResponse<LexiconCurationResult>`; §8.33). |
| POST | `/api/memory/lexicon/erase/prepare` | Measures the selective erasure of one exact Lexicon entry against the complete target `show` reported, and issues a five-minute preflight token (`LexiconErasePrepareRequest` → `ApiResponse<MemoryErasurePreflightDto>`; `SensitivityRetentionPurge`; §8.35). |
| POST | `/api/memory/lexicon/erase` | Applies that erasure in one verified transaction, or replays it from its receipt (`LexiconEraseRequest` → `ApiResponse<MemoryErasureResultDto>`; `SensitivityRetentionPurge`; §8.35). |
| POST | `/api/memory/lexicon/release` | Releases the erasure fingerprint of one Lexicon name in one exact scope, so extraction and agents may scribe it there again; never creates the erasure key and takes no `mutationId` (`LexiconErasureReleaseRequest` → `ApiResponse<MemoryErasureReleaseResultDto>`; `LifecycleManage`; §8.35). |
| DELETE | `/api/memory/lexicon/{name}` | Deletes exactly one named Lexicon entity (**204**; **404** `Lexicon.NotFound`). A Covenant-labelled entity is removed through the sensitivity purge (DESIGN §10.20.2): **409** `Covenant.StaleSnapshot` when its label or the operator authority changed while it was being purged, **503** `Covenant.Unavailable` when its sensitivity label cannot be read or the kernel met an environmental storage condition (a database still busy or locked, an I/O error, a full disk, a database that refuses writes), **403** `Covenant.ForbiddenAuthority` when its label is found and the request holds no sensitivity-purge authority, and **503** `Covenant.ManualArtifactErasureRequired` when the kernel could not erase it for any other reason (a plan-declared table missing from the schema among them) and left it unchanged. Every one of those refusals leaves the entity unchanged, and the delete asks the label guard again inside its own `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, so a label written since the purge answers **403** `Covenant.ForbiddenAuthority` and a label table that cannot be read answers **503** `Covenant.Unavailable` there as well. CLI callers must confirm. Other stores are unchanged. An unsuppressed delete, not erasure: no fingerprint is recorded, so extraction or an agent can write the same content again; see `/api/memory/{store}/erase` (§8.35). Optional `?campaignId=` targets exactly that tier with no fallback, so a delete aimed at a Campaign holding no such entity removes nothing rather than removing the installation's. |
| POST | `/api/memory/covenant/list` | Page of current heads for one scope (`CovenantListRequest` → `ApiResponse<CovenantPageDto>`; Covenant read authority; protected; §8.28). |
| POST | `/api/memory/covenant/query` | Free-text query over the protected route, compiled by the Covenant query compiler; `search` reports accelerator health (`CovenantQueryRequest` → `ApiResponse<CovenantPageDto>`; §8.28). |
| POST | `/api/memory/covenant/detail` | Both lane heads and per-lane curation, never content (`CovenantDetailRequest` → `ApiResponse<CovenantDetailDto>`; §8.28). |
| POST | `/api/memory/covenant/versions` | One lane's immutable version page (`CovenantVersionsRequest` → `ApiResponse<CovenantVersionPageDto>`; §8.28). |
| POST | `/api/memory/covenant/sources` | One head's provenance page (`CovenantSourcesRequest` → `ApiResponse<CovenantSourcesDto>`; §8.28). |
| POST | `/api/memory/covenant/explain` | What a turn in the named Campaign would receive (`CovenantExplainRequest` → `ApiResponse<CovenantExplainDto>`; §8.28). |
| POST | `/api/memory/covenant/set/prepare` | Operator `set` preflight (`CovenantSetPrepareRequest` → `ApiResponse<CovenantMutationPreflightDto>`; `CovenantManage`; §8.28). |
| PUT | `/api/memory/covenant` | Commit a prepared `set` (`CovenantSetRequest` → `ApiResponse<CovenantMutationResultDto>`; §8.28). |
| POST | `/api/memory/covenant/retire/prepare` | Retirement preflight (`CovenantRetirePrepareRequest` → `ApiResponse<CovenantMutationPreflightDto>`; §8.28). |
| POST | `/api/memory/covenant/retire` | Commit a prepared retirement (`CovenantRetireRequest` → `ApiResponse<CovenantMutationResultDto>`; §8.28). |
| POST | `/api/memory/covenant/correct/prepare` | Exact-version correction preflight (`CovenantCorrectPrepareRequest` → `ApiResponse<CovenantMutationPreflightDto>`; §8.28). |
| POST | `/api/memory/covenant/correct` | Commit a prepared correction (`CovenantCorrectRequest` → `ApiResponse<CovenantMutationResultDto>`; §8.28). |
| POST | `/api/memory/covenant/curate/prepare` | Pin, unpin, mask or unmask preflight (`CovenantCurationPrepareRequest` → `ApiResponse<CovenantCurationPreflightDto>`; §8.28). |
| POST | `/api/memory/covenant/curate` | Commit a prepared curation change (`CovenantCurationRequest` → `ApiResponse<CovenantCurationResultDto>`; §8.28). |
| GET | `/api/memory/saga/{id}` | One Saga memory read whole (`ApiResponse<SagaMemoryDetail>`): the row, the host's own `contentHash` over its content, its retirement and pin stamps, its retrieval eligibility as a typed reason, and the Annals claim governing it with its version history when one exists. **404** `Saga.NotFound`; a memory with no claim is a success reporting none (§8.30, DESIGN §21.13). |
| POST | `/api/memory/saga/long-rest` | Apply one bounded operator-declared exact Saga group (`LongRestRequest` → `ApiResponse<LongRestReceipt>`). First valid applied and no-change decisions produce immutable content-free evidence; unchanged canonical input replay writes nothing (§8.36, DESIGN §21.16). |
| GET | `/api/memory/saga/long-rest/receipts/{receiptId}` | Inspect one immutable content-free Long Rest receipt (`ApiResponse<LongRestReceipt>`); **404** `LongRest.NotFound` (§8.36). |
| POST | `/api/memory/saga/long-rest/sweeps` | Request one exact Global or canonical Campaign clean sweep (`LongRestSweepRequest` → `ApiResponse<LongRunningOperationDto>`). Protected debt waits for a fresh eligible Session request (§8.36, DESIGN §21.16.3). |
| POST | `/api/memory/saga/{id}/correct` | Replaces one Saga memory's text (`SagaCorrectRequest` → `ApiResponse<SagaCurationResult>`); the replacement vector is computed before any transaction opens, then the row's text and its vector are republished together in one transaction (§8.30). |
| POST | `/api/memory/saga/{id}/retire` | Takes one Saga memory out of retrieval by deleting its embedding rows, keeps the row inspectable, and writes content-free keyed suppression evidence (`SagaRetireRequest` → `ApiResponse<SagaCurationResult>`; §8.30). |
| POST | `/api/memory/saga/{id}/reinstate` | Puts a retired Saga memory back: re-embeds its stored content and releases the suppression covering that content in that scope (`SagaReinstateRequest` → `ApiResponse<SagaCurationResult>`; §8.30). |
| POST | `/api/memory/saga/{id}/pin` | Marks one Saga memory durable so retention will not select it. No body and no content hash (`ApiResponse<SagaCurationResult>`; §8.30). |
| POST | `/api/memory/saga/{id}/unpin` | Clears that mark, so retention may select the memory again. No body (`ApiResponse<SagaCurationResult>`; §8.30). |
| POST | `/api/memory/saga/erase/prepare` | Measures the selective erasure of one Saga memory and every memory holding its exact content in its exact scope, and issues a five-minute preflight token (`SagaErasePrepareRequest` → `ApiResponse<MemoryErasurePreflightDto>`; `SensitivityRetentionPurge`; §8.35). |
| POST | `/api/memory/saga/erase` | Applies that erasure in one verified transaction, or replays it from its receipt (`SagaEraseRequest` → `ApiResponse<MemoryErasureResultDto>`; `SensitivityRetentionPurge`; §8.35). |
| POST | `/api/memory/saga/release` | Releases the erasure fingerprint of Saga content in one exact scope, trying the content exactly and trimmed, so extraction may write it there again; never creates the erasure key and takes no `mutationId` (`SagaErasureReleaseRequest` → `ApiResponse<MemoryErasureReleaseResultDto>`; `LifecycleManage`; §8.35). |
| POST | `/api/memory/saga/review/list` | Bounded newest-first exact-scope Saga review queue (`SagaReviewListRequest` → `ApiResponse<SagaReviewPageDto>`; §8.34). |
| POST | `/api/memory/saga/review/prepare` | Validate one exact-version Saga bulk decision and issue its short-lived plan (`SagaReviewBulkPrepareRequest` → `ApiResponse<MemoryReviewBulkPlanDto>`; §8.34). |
| POST | `/api/memory/saga/review/apply` | Atomically apply or replay the prepared Saga decision (`SagaReviewBulkApplyRequest` → `ApiResponse<MemoryReviewBulkResultDto>`; §8.34). |
| POST | `/api/memory/lexicon/review/list` | Bounded newest-first exact-scope Lexicon review queue (`LexiconReviewListRequest` → `ApiResponse<LexiconReviewPageDto>`; conditional protected read; §8.34). |
| POST | `/api/memory/lexicon/review/prepare` | Validate one exact-version Lexicon bulk decision and issue its short-lived plan (`LexiconReviewBulkPrepareRequest` → `ApiResponse<MemoryReviewBulkPlanDto>`; conditional protected read; §8.34). |
| POST | `/api/memory/lexicon/review/apply` | Atomically apply or replay the prepared Lexicon decision (`LexiconReviewBulkApplyRequest` → `ApiResponse<MemoryReviewBulkResultDto>`; conditional exact protected write; §8.34). |
| POST | `/api/memory/covenant/review/list` | Bounded newest-first Covenant queue for one exact scope and lane (`CovenantReviewListRequest` → `ApiResponse<CovenantReviewPageDto>`; protected read; §8.34). |
| POST | `/api/memory/covenant/review/prepare` | Validate one exact-version Covenant bulk decision and issue its short-lived plan (`CovenantReviewBulkPrepareRequest` → `ApiResponse<MemoryReviewBulkPlanDto>`; `CovenantManage`; §8.34). |
| POST | `/api/memory/covenant/review/apply` | Atomically apply or replay the prepared Covenant decision (`CovenantReviewBulkApplyRequest` → `ApiResponse<MemoryReviewBulkResultDto>`; `CovenantManage`; §8.34). |
| POST | `/api/memory/covenant/erase/prepare` | Measures the selective erasure of one whole Covenant entry against the entry id and lane heads `detail` reported, and issues a five-minute preflight token (`CovenantErasePrepareRequest` → `ApiResponse<MemoryErasurePreflightDto>`; `LifecycleManage`; §8.35). |
| POST | `/api/memory/covenant/erase` | Drains the entry's Covenant scope and applies that erasure in one verified transaction, or replays it from its receipt (`CovenantEraseRequest` → `ApiResponse<MemoryErasureResultDto>`; `LifecycleManage`; §8.35). |
| POST | `/api/memory/covenant/release` | Releases the erasure fingerprint of one well-formed Covenant key in one exact scope, so agents may author it there again; never creates the erasure key and takes no `mutationId` (`CovenantErasureReleaseRequest` → `ApiResponse<MemoryErasureReleaseResultDto>`; `LifecycleManage`; §8.35). |
| GET | `/api/memory/erasure` | The installation's erasure status: the erasure key's state (`Absent`, `Present`, `Unavailable` or `Lost`), each store's fingerprint, unverifiable and receipt counts, and the receipts still pending a scrub; authentication only, with no operator authority, and reads no secret when no evidence exists (`ApiResponse<MemoryErasureStatusDto>`; §8.35). |
| POST | `/api/memory/erasure/scrub` | Retries the write-ahead-log checkpoint for the receipts still pending on it, and moves those whose only reason was the log to `Verified`; no body (`ApiResponse<MemoryErasureScrubResultDto>`; `LifecycleManage`; §8.35). |
| POST | `/api/memory/erasure/reset-key/prepare` | Measures the erasure key's state and each store's unverifiable fingerprints and receipts, and issues a five-minute key-reset token; no body (`ApiResponse<MemoryErasureKeyResetPreflightDto>`; `LifecycleManage`; §8.35). |
| POST | `/api/memory/erasure/reset-key` | Applies that token: keeps a present key or creates one when it is proven absent, then discards only the evidence the key cannot verify; naturally idempotent and takes no `mutationId` (`MemoryErasureKeyResetRequest` → `ApiResponse<MemoryErasureKeyResetResultDto>`; `LifecycleManage`; §8.35). |
| GET | `/api/spells` | Compatibility list is still `ApiResponse<SpellSummary[]>`; `paged=true` selects the bounded `ApiResponse<SpellCatalogPage>` contract with `workspace`, `q`, `tag`, `tool`, `source`, and opaque `cursor` (§8.14). |
| GET | `/api/spells/{name}` | Spell detail (`ApiResponse<SpellDetail>`; optional `workspace` query; **404** when missing). |
| POST | `/api/spells` | Create workspace spell (`ApiResponse<bool>`; optional `workspace` query; **400** validation). |
| PUT | `/api/spells/{name}` | Update workspace spell (`ApiResponse<bool>`; optional `workspace` query; **404** `Spell.NotFound`; **400** on built-in or validation failure). |
| DELETE | `/api/spells/{name}` | Delete workspace spell (**204** on success; **404** `Spell.NotFound` so delete-if-exists can treat a missing spell as already gone; **400** on built-in or validation failure; §8.14). |
| GET | `/api/spells/search` | Multi-source spell search (`ApiResponse<SpellSummary[]>`; `?q=`, `?tag=`, `?tool=`, `?source=`, `?campaignId=`, `?workspace=`; §8.14). |
| POST | `/api/spells/{name}/validate` | Validate spell metadata and declared tools (`ApiResponse<SpellValidationResultDto>`; §8.14). |
| POST | `/api/spells/{name}/export` | Export portable spell bundle (`ApiResponse<SpellExportDto>`; `omittedScripts` names the `scripts/*` files the bundle leaves out, at most 64, and `omittedScriptCount` counts all of them; §8.14). |
| POST | `/api/spells/import` | Import spell into workspace (`ApiResponse<SpellSummary>`; **400** `Spell.NameCollision`, `Spell.InvalidFrontmatter`, `Spell.InvalidSkillJson`, `Validation.InvalidBody` when `payload` is absent, a script is not base64, or the bundle is larger than export would emit; `Spell.InvalidScriptPath` when a script's file name is not a bare name; **500** `Spell.WriteFailed` when the workspace filesystem refuses the write, the same mapping create, update and delete use; §8.14). |
| POST | `/api/spells/{name}/execute` | Forced-spell buffered inference (`ApiResponse<PromptResponseDto>`; body `SpellExecuteRequest`; optional `?workspace=`, `?version=` (string label); **404** `Spell.NotFound`; a Campaign/Session resolution failure answers its own status, not a flat 400 (**404** `Session.NotFound` / `Campaign.NotFound`, **409** `Session.CampaignBindingRequired` / `Covenant.CampaignBindingConflict`, **503** `Covenant.IntegrityFailure` when the stored binding or Campaign path identity cannot be read intact), while a malformed request stays **400**; DESIGN §19). |
| POST | `/api/spells/{name}/execute-stream` | Forced-spell NDJSON streaming inference (same request/query as execute, including the resolution-failure statuses, which are set before any body is written; DESIGN §19). |
| GET | `/api/spells/{name}/versions` | List `SPELL.md` (active row) and `SPELL.v{label}.md` files (`ApiResponse<SpellVersionDto[]>`; **string** `version` label and `isActive` flag; optional `?workspace=` / `?campaignId=` resolution). |
| GET | `/api/spells/{name}/versions/{version}` | Read a spell version's editable body (`ApiResponse<SpellVersionDetailDto>`; optional `?workspace=` / `?campaignId=`; use the version label reported by the list route, including `(active)` when no explicit active label exists). |
| POST | `/api/spells/{name}/versions` | Create a new spell version file (`ApiResponse<SpellVersionDto>`; body `CreateSpellVersionRequest` with required `version` / `body` and optional `workspace`; **201**). |
| PUT | `/api/spells/{name}/versions/{version}` | Overwrite an existing version's body, preserving frontmatter (`ApiResponse<SpellVersionDto>`; body `UpdateSpellVersionRequest`; **404** when the version does not exist; §8.14). |
| POST | `/api/spells/{name}/versions/{version}/activate` | Activate a version, swapping its content into `SPELL.md` and preserving the prior active content as `SPELL.v{previousLabel}.md`. Re-activating the already-active label would alias that backup onto the version file being activated, so the backup is skipped and `previousVersion` comes back `null`; the version → `SPELL.md` copy and the `activeVersion` write still run, which is what applies an edit made through `PUT /api/spells/{name}/versions/{version}`. |
| POST | `/api/spells/{name}/clone` | Clone a spell (built-in or workspace) into a new workspace spell (`ApiResponse<SpellSummary>`; body `CloneSpellRequest` with required `newName` and optional `workspace`). |
| POST | `/api/spells/{name}/cast` | Dry-run cast preview: assembled system prompt, resonant dependencies, attuned tools, and spell scripts, **without** LLM inference. |
| GET | `/api/campaigns` | List Grimoire-backed campaigns, ordered by name (`ApiResponse<ListPageResult<CampaignDto>>`; optional `?type=`, `?limit=` (clamped to 1–10,000; default 100), and `?offset=` (non-negative; a negative value is treated as 0); `hasMore` / `nextOffset` report a truncated page; DESIGN §19). |
| GET | `/api/campaigns/by-path` | Lookup campaign by filesystem path (`ApiResponse<CampaignDto>`; required `?path=`; **404** `Campaign.NotFound`; the registration at that exact spelling, otherwise the registration of the same path under another spelling, a trailing separator, the Unicode normalization form of an accented name or, on Windows and macOS, the case of a name, unless the file system shows the two spellings to be two directories (a case-sensitive volume); DESIGN §19). |
| GET | `/api/campaigns/{id}` | Campaign detail (`ApiResponse<CampaignDto>`; **404** when missing; DESIGN §19). |
| POST | `/api/campaigns` | Register campaign directory (`ApiResponse<CampaignDto>`; **201** + `Location`; creates `.arcanum/`; DESIGN §19). Name and path uniqueness is pre-checked and then enforced by the database unique index: a registration that loses a concurrent race is rejected by the index and `CampaignRepository.AddAsync` re-reads the conflicting row to return the same **400** `Campaign.DuplicateName` or `Campaign.DuplicatePath` the pre-check would have produced. The same directory spelled another way (a trailing separator, the Unicode normalization form of an accented name, or, on Windows and macOS, the case of a name) is the same registration and is rejected as `Campaign.DuplicatePath` too, unless the file system shows the two spellings to be two directories (a case-sensitive volume, where both can be registered): the unique index compares bytes, so `AddAsync` checks the equivalent spelling inside the transaction that would write the row. |
| PUT | `/api/campaigns/{id}` | Update campaign (`ApiResponse<CampaignDto>`; DESIGN §19). Writes name, type, description and settings without overwriting concurrent Sanctum changes. A rename that loses the normalized-name unique-index race returns **400** `Campaign.DuplicateName`; deletion after the initial read returns **404** `Campaign.NotFound`. A supplied `settings` object replaces the stored one wholesale rather than merging member by member, so every member a partial body omits falls back to that member's own default. `CampaignSettings` has no Ward-control member; legacy `requireWardForForbiddenArts` input is ignored as an unknown property and is not emitted by reads or exports. |
| DELETE | `/api/campaigns/{id}` | Remove campaign (**204**; DESIGN §19). The Long Rest discovery data derived from the Campaign's memories (generations, nodes, edges, indexes, embeddings, work and effect rows) is removed with it. |
| GET | `/api/campaigns/{id}/spells` | Spells scoped to a campaign, merging built-ins with campaign spells shadowing them (`ApiResponse<SpellSummary[]>`; `?q=`, `?tag=`, `?tool=`; **404** `Campaign.NotFound`; DESIGN §19). |
| GET | `/api/campaigns/{id}/prompts` | Prompts scoped to a campaign (`ApiResponse<ListPageResult<PromptSummaryDto>>`; `?q=`, `?tag=`, `?limit=` (clamped to 1–10,000; default 10,000), `?offset=`; **404** `Campaign.NotFound`; DESIGN §19). Reads one page (10,000 rows by default) and reports `hasMore`/`nextOffset` from it, the same way `GET /api/prompts` does, so a client follows `nextOffset` by passing it back as `?offset=`; `?q=`/`?tag=` filter the first 10,000-row page client-side, ignore `?limit=`/`?offset=`, and therefore always report `hasMore: false`. |
| GET | `/api/campaigns/{id}/sessions` | Sessions scoped to a campaign (`ApiResponse<SessionQueryResult>`; `?status=`, `?search=`, `?limit=`, `?beforeUpdatedAt=`; **404** `Campaign.NotFound`; DESIGN §19). |
| POST | `/api/campaigns/{id}/export` | Export spells + prompts + settings (`ApiResponse<CampaignExportDto>`; DESIGN §19). Carries no Covenant content, version, receipt, hash, provenance, or tainted artifact. With `Arcanum:Features:Covenant` on it adds `exclusions` — the content-free `covenantEntryCount` and `taintedArtifactCount` this bundle left behind; the field is omitted entirely when the feature is off ([DESIGN §10.19.11](Arcanum.DESIGN.md#101911-refusing-tainted-plaintext-export-and-reporting-what-a-campaign-export-left-behind)). Whatever the feature, `omittedSpells` names the listed non-builtin spells the bundle could not carry (a `SPELL.md` that failed the regular-file read) and each spell's `omittedScripts` names the `scripts/*` files it leaves out, exactly as the spell export does; both are `[]` for a complete bundle and are ignored on import. |
| POST | `/api/campaigns/{id}/import` | Import portable campaign bundle (`ApiResponse<CampaignImportResultDto>`; DESIGN §19). Imported settings update only settings and the update timestamp, preserving concurrent name, type, description and Sanctum edits; deletion before that write returns **404** `Campaign.NotFound`. `strategy` is `merge` (the default when absent or blank) or `replace`, case-insensitive; any other value is refused with **400** `Campaign.ImportFailed` and nothing is read or written. The bundle's shape is validated in full before anything is written, so a bundle missing `campaign`, carrying a null spell/prompt entry, a prompt without a name, a version or a template, two prompts that share a trimmed name and version, or spell metadata that is not valid JSON is refused with **400** `Campaign.ImportFailed` and `strategy: "replace"` has not deleted anything. `replace` swaps the Campaign's whole prompt set in one transaction (every existing prompt is deleted, however many, and every bundle prompt written, so a bundle prompt may reuse the name and version of one it replaces and any failure leaves the previous prompts in place); under `merge` a bundle prompt whose name and version already exist in the Campaign is skipped with a warning. Only that prompt swap is atomic: the Campaign settings, the spells and, under `merge`, the prompts are written after it, each on its own (a spell or merged prompt that fails is a warning), and once the first of those writes has committed the import runs to completion even if the caller disconnects, so a dropped connection never leaves the prompts replaced and the settings and spells unapplied. Absent `spells`/`prompts` are treated as empty. With no `payload` the bundle is read from `<campaign path>/.arcanum/campaign.json`, which is not trusted: the path is contained under the campaign directory with every link resolved, the file is opened once without following a link and must be an unaliased regular file, and the read stops at **16 MiB** (the same ceiling as the request body). A link, a non-regular file, or a larger file is refused with **400** `Campaign.ImportFailed` without being read to the end, and an absent file is the existing `No import payload and no campaign.json on disk.` refusal. |
| GET | `/api/campaigns/{id}/codex` | Read campaign `CODEX.md` (`ApiResponse<CodexContentDto>`; `exists: false` with empty content only when file absent (a valid empty file has `exists: true`); **404** `Campaign.NotFound`; **400** `Codex.ContentTooLarge` when over the effective CODEX byte cap (256 KiB code-owned ceiling), `Codex.InvalidEncoding` for non-UTF-8 content, `Codex.Unreadable` for an I/O or access failure or a refused non-regular or aliased file (including a contained symbolic link or multiple hard links); **400** `Codex.PathNotContained` when `CODEX.md` is a link resolving outside the campaign root; DESIGN §19). |
| PUT | `/api/campaigns/{id}/codex` | Create or overwrite campaign `CODEX.md` (`ApiResponse<CodexContentDto>`; body `{ "content": "..." }`; **400** `Validation.InvalidBody` when `content` is missing or null, **400** when over the code-owned CODEX size limit or `Codex.PathNotContained`, **400** `Workspace.SymbolicLinkEscape` when `CODEX.md` is itself a link (a symbolic link, even one whose target is inside the campaign root, or a file with more than one hard link), **500** `Workspace.WriteFailed` when the write itself fails; DESIGN §19). Containment is checked before any directory is created, and the content goes to a same-directory temporary file that atomically replaces `CODEX.md`, so a cancelled or failed write leaves the previous file intact. Because the write replaces the entry rather than writing into it, a linked `CODEX.md` is refused and left untouched rather than followed; `DELETE` removes such a link without touching its target. |
| DELETE | `/api/campaigns/{id}/codex` | Delete campaign `CODEX.md` (**204**; unlinks the entry itself, so a link is removed rather than followed, but the directory it lives in must resolve under the campaign root: **400** `Codex.PathNotContained` otherwise, **500** `Workspace.DeleteFailed` when the entry cannot be removed; DESIGN §19). |
| GET | `/api/codex` | Read global `~/.config/arcanum/CODEX.md` (`ApiResponse<CodexContentDto>`; same absent, empty, size, encoding and secure-read failure behaviour as the campaign GET; **400** `Codex.PathNotContained` when the file resolves outside the Grimoire directory; DESIGN §19). |
| PUT | `/api/codex` | Create or overwrite global CODEX (`ApiResponse<CodexContentDto>`; same body, size, containment, linked-file refusal, atomic-write and write-failure behaviour as the campaign route; DESIGN §19). |
| DELETE | `/api/codex` | Delete global CODEX (**204**; same link, containment and failure behaviour as the campaign route; DESIGN §19). |
| GET | `/api/campaigns/{campaignId}/sanctum` | Campaign Sanctum config (`ApiResponse<SanctumConfig>`; default `Enabled: false`; DESIGN §11.15). |
| PUT | `/api/campaigns/{campaignId}/sanctum` | Update Sanctum config (`ApiResponse<SanctumConfig>`; body `SanctumConfig`). Writes only Sanctum configuration and the update timestamp, preserving concurrent Campaign header/settings changes; deletion after the read returns **404** `Campaign.NotFound`. A member the body omits takes its declared default, not `default(T)`: a partial body cannot silently clear `enforcePathBoundary`, floor `maxBreachCount`, or zero the `resourceLimits` ceilings (where `0` means *unlimited* for `maxCpuSeconds` / `maxFileDescriptors`, and for `maxMemoryMb` leaves `maxProcessMemoryMb` (clamped 64–8192) as the one effective memory ceiling; otherwise the smaller of the two applies on every platform, so raising the ceiling means raising both, 8192 MB is the hard maximum, and `maxMemoryMb` above 8192 has no effect. `workspace_check` runs instead get a code-owned memory ceiling of `max(effective ceiling, 4096 MB)`, capped at 8192 MB). Send a member explicitly to change it. **400** `Sanctum.InvalidConfig` when `networkPolicy` is `AllowList` with no `allowedDomains`, or when any `allowedPaths` entry is not an absolute path; **404** `Campaign.NotFound`. |
| GET | `/api/campaigns/{campaignId}/sanctum/breaches` | Paginated Sanctum breach history (`ApiResponse<SanctumBreachQueryResult>`; `?limit=` default 100 clamp 1–1,000, `?before=` ISO 8601 cursor, `?tool=` filter). |
| GET | `/api/wards` | List active wards (`ApiResponse<WardDto[]>`; DESIGN §11.14). |
| GET | `/api/wards/{id}` | Active ward detail (`ApiResponse<WardDto>`; **404** `Ward.NotFound`). |
| POST | `/api/wards/{id}` | Resolve a retained active compatibility Ward (`ResolveWardRequest`: `allow`, optional `reason`); returns `ApiResponse<WardResolutionDto>` whose additive `origin` is always `human` here. **409** `Ward.AlreadyResolved` when the record was already resolved, including an automatic audit tombstone. |
| GET | `/api/prompts` | List/search prompts (`ApiResponse<ListPageResult<PromptSummaryDto>>`; `?campaignId=`, `?q=`, `?tag=`; DESIGN §19). |
| GET | `/api/prompts/{id}` | Prompt detail (`ApiResponse<PromptDetailDto>`; **404** `Prompt.NotFound`; DESIGN §19). |
| GET | `/api/prompts/by-name/{name}/versions` | List versions for a prompt name (`ApiResponse<PromptVersionDto[]>`; optional `?campaignId=`; DESIGN §19). |
| POST | `/api/prompts` | Create prompt version (`ApiResponse<PromptDetailDto>`; **201**; a missing/null `template` is **400** `Prompt.InvalidRequest` with field guidance, while empty/whitespace/literal templates are accepted unchanged; **400** `Prompt.DuplicateVersion`, including when it loses a concurrent create to the unique index; **404** `Campaign.NotFound` for a `campaignId` no Campaign has; DESIGN §19). |
