| PUT | `/api/prompts/{id}` | Update prompt (`ApiResponse<PromptDetailDto>`; **404** `Prompt.NotFound`, including deletion after the initial read; a supplied `name` or `version` is trimmed and must not be blank, **400** `Prompt.InvalidName` / `Prompt.InvalidVersion`, while an omitted one is kept as stored and not validated again, so an update that does not rename the prompt never fails on its stored name; renaming onto a (name, version) another prompt of the same Campaign scope already holds is **400** `Prompt.DuplicateVersion`, including when it loses a concurrent write to the unique index, and an update that keeps its own name and version never collides with itself; DESIGN §19). |
| DELETE | `/api/prompts/{id}` | Delete prompt (**204**; DESIGN §19). |
| POST | `/api/prompts/{id}/render` | Render template with parameters (`ApiResponse<PromptRenderResultDto>`; **400** `Prompt.MissingParameter` / `Prompt.UnknownParameter`; DESIGN §19). |
| POST | `/api/prompts/{id}/test` | Assemble system prompt without LLM (`ApiResponse<PromptTestResultDto>`; a supplied `workingDirectory` is resolved through the same `Arcanum:Security:SpellWorkspaceRoots` allowlist as prompt execute, spell execute and ping before it is used as the codex containment root or the MCP tool workspace, so a directory that is missing is refused with **400** `Spell.InvalidWorkspace`, and one outside the allowlist is refused with **403** `Spell.PathNotAllowed` when the request also names a `codexPath` the working directory would contain, which is the case only for a prompt with no Campaign path (the file it asked to read is never silently dropped); otherwise (no `codexPath`, or a Campaign prompt whose codex its Campaign path contains) an unlisted directory is not an error: the preview is assembled with no workspace (no containment root, the global tool set only), so `arcanum prompt test`, which always sends its own current directory, succeeds from any directory on an installation whose roots list is empty; a blank `workingDirectory` is never resolved; an optional `codexPath` outside the campaign path (a campaign prompt's path wins) or the resolved working directory is refused with **400** `Prompt.CodexPathNotContained`, an absent file with `Prompt.CodexPathNotFound`, a file that exists but cannot be opened or read with `Prompt.CodexReadFailed`, and one over the effective codex cap with `Prompt.CodexPathTooLarge`; DESIGN §19). |
| POST | `/api/prompts/{id}/execute` | Render template and run session-backed inference (`ApiResponse<PromptResponseDto>`; body `PromptExecuteRequest`; honors `sessionId`; a Campaign/Session resolution failure answers its own status (**404** `Session.NotFound` / `Campaign.NotFound`, **409** `Session.CampaignBindingRequired` / `Covenant.CampaignBindingConflict`, **503** `Covenant.IntegrityFailure` when the stored binding or Campaign path identity cannot be read intact) rather than a flat 400; DESIGN §19). |
| POST | `/api/prompts/{id}/execute-stream` | Same as execute with NDJSON `IntelligenceEvent` stream; the resolution-failure statuses are set before any body is written. |
| POST | `/api/prompts/{id}/export` | Portable prompt JSON (`ApiResponse<PromptExportDto>`; DESIGN §19). |
| POST | `/api/prompts/import` | Import prompt (`ApiResponse<PromptSummaryDto>`; DESIGN §19). A body whose `payload` is absent, or whose payload omits `name`, `version`, or `template`, is refused with **400** `Prompt.InvalidRequest`/`Prompt.InvalidName`/`Prompt.InvalidVersion`; an absent `tags` persists as an empty list. A `campaignId` no Campaign has is **404** `Campaign.NotFound`. |
| POST | `/api/prompts/{id}/clone` | Clone a prompt to a new name/version, optionally overriding the campaign scope (`ApiResponse<PromptDetailDto>`; body `ClonePromptRequest` with required `newName` / `newVersion` and optional `campaignId`; **400** `Prompt.DuplicateVersion`, **404** `Prompt.NotFound` for the source or `Campaign.NotFound` for a `campaignId` no Campaign has). |
| GET | `/api/apprentices` | List Apprentices (`ApiResponse<ListPageResult<ApprenticeSummaryDto>>`; optional `?campaignId=`, `?status=`, `?limit=`, `?beforeUpdatedAt=`). Ordered `(UpdatedAt DESC, Id DESC)`; `beforeUpdatedAt` is a bare timestamp consumed as a strict `<`, so a page ending inside a group of Apprentices sharing an `UpdatedAt` is cut at that tie boundary and may return fewer than `limit`, and a page that is entirely one timestamp widens to the whole tie group and may exceed it. That widening is bounded at 1,000 Apprentices whatever `limit` is, because a bare-timestamp cursor cannot express a position inside a group: a larger group is a logged server fault (**500** `Hub.Unhandled`, never a clipped page that would strand the rest of the group), so narrow the query with `campaignId` or `status`. DESIGN §19.6. |
| GET | `/api/apprentices/{id}` | Apprentice detail (`ApiResponse<ApprenticeDetailDto>`; **404** `Apprentice.NotFound`; DESIGN §19.6). |
| POST | `/api/apprentices` | Create Apprentice (`ApiResponse<ApprenticeDetailDto>`; **201** + `Location`; DESIGN §19.6). |
| DELETE | `/api/apprentices/{id}` | Delete terminal Apprentice (**204**; **409** `Apprentice.Running`; DESIGN §19.6). |
| POST | `/api/apprentices/{id}/start` | Start plan generation and execution (**202**; **409** `Apprentice.AlreadyRunning`; DESIGN §5.7). |
| POST | `/api/apprentices/{id}/pause` | Pause at step boundary (**202**, also when the run already recorded the pause itself; **409** `Apprentice.Running` when the Apprentice is not running or planning, including when it reaches another status before the pause lands; DESIGN §5.7). |
| POST | `/api/apprentices/{id}/resume` | Resume from checkpoint (**202**; **409** `Apprentice.NotPaused`; DESIGN §5.7). |
| POST | `/api/apprentices/{id}/cancel` | Cancel execution or a queued start (**202**; **409** `Apprentice.NotPaused` when the Apprentice is not in a cancellable state, including when it ends before the cancel lands; DESIGN §5.7). |
| POST | `/api/apprentices/{id}/reweave` | Replace pending plan steps (`ApiResponse<ApprenticeDetailDto>`; **400** `Apprentice.InvalidPlan`; **409** `Apprentice.CannotReweave`, also when a step commits while the plan is being re-woven; DESIGN §5.7). |
| POST | `/api/apprentices/{id}/intervene` | Resolve **Escalated** Apprentice with DM guidance (**202**; **409** `Apprentice.NotEscalated`, also when the Apprentice leaves **Escalated** while the guidance is being applied; DESIGN §5.7). |
| POST | `/api/apprentices/{id}/cast` | **The Conclave** cross-Apprentice delegation: mint a child Apprentice from a parent (`ApiResponse<ApprenticeDetailDto>`; **201**; gated by `Arcanum:Features:Conclave`. No fixed depth/breadth ceiling — loops are caught by cycle detection, DESIGN §5.7.1). |
| GET | `/api/apprentices/{id}/chronicle` | Chronicle SSE stream (`text/event-stream`; CLI `watch apprentice`; DESIGN §5.7, DESIGN §19.6). A2A Sending frames (`sendingDispatched`, `sendingProgress`, `sendingCompleted`, `sendingFailed`) additionally carry `sendingDirection` (`outbound`), `sendingState` (the remote A2A task state, e.g. `working`), and — on the terminal frames — `durationMs`, `remoteCostKnown`, `remoteTotalTokens`, and `remoteCostUsd`. `sendingProgress` fires on remote **state transitions**, not once per poll, and never carries the peer's status prose, credentials, or prompt bodies (DESIGN §5.7.1). `sendingCompleted.result` and `sendingFailed.error` carry the remote agent's text itself, not the untrusted-content frame the model sees, and `summary` carries the remote task id, on all four Sending frames including `sendingProgress`, only when it is a plain token (1 to 128 ASCII letters, digits or `- _ . : / + = ~ @`). |
| GET | `/api/conclave/status` | Conclave/A2A state (`ApiResponse<ConclaveStatusDto>`): `state` is `disabled` \| `configured` \| `degraded` \| `healthy`, plus the effective server/Agent Card paths, the enabled surfaces, and the **count** of allowed remote agents (never the entries). CLI `arcanum conclave status`; DESIGN §5.7.1. |
| POST | `/api/conclave/sendings` | Dispatch a Sending to a remote A2A agent and block until it settles (`{ agentUrl, goal, name?, continuable?, callback?, skillId?, acceptedOutputModes? }` → `ApiResponse<SendingDispatchDto>`; **400** `Validation.InvalidBody`; **403** `Sending.Disabled` / `Sending.AgentNotAllowed`; **502** `Sending.AgentUnreachable` / `Sending.AgentCardInvalid`; **400** `Sending.TaskRejected` / `Sending.ModalityMismatch` / `Sending.SkillNotAdvertised`). `SendingDispatchDto` carries `taskId`, `responseText`, `costKnown` + `remoteTotalTokens` / `remoteCostUsd` (**`costKnown: false` means the peer reported nothing — not that the Sending was free**), the distinct `dispatchedAt` / `settledAt` instants, and `continuationNeed` (`input` \| `auth` \| `null`). With `continuable: true` a remote that stops at `input-required` / `auth-required` returns a continuation instead of ending, and the remote task is left alive. `skillId` / `acceptedOutputModes` are checked against the peer's Agent Card **before** the remote task is created, so a mismatch is a named local failure rather than something discovered mid-exchange (DESIGN §5.7.1). `callback: true` asks the peer to report back so the Sending stops holding a concurrency slot, falling back to the ordinary wait when the peer cannot accept one (DESIGN §5.7.1.4); it takes precedence over `continuable`. Cancelling the request cancels the remote task. CLI `arcanum conclave dispatch`. |
| POST | `/api/conclave/sendings/{taskId}/continue` | Answer a Sending the remote parked at `input-required` / `auth-required` and resume the **same** remote task (`{ agentUrl, message, continuable?, skillId?, acceptedOutputModes? }` → `ApiResponse<SendingDispatchDto>`, same shape and error codes as dispatch). Nothing is re-run. CLI `arcanum conclave continue`; DESIGN §5.7.1.3. |
| — | `/api/conclave/a2a/*` | A2A (Agent-to-Agent) JSON-RPC surface (`MapA2A`), mapped when `Arcanum:Features:A2AServer` is true, which derives `Arcanum:Features:Conclave` (the Conclave flag need not be set beside it). The path follows `Arcanum:Integrations:A2A:ServerPath`; a configured path outside `/api` is mounted under it rather than refused. |
| GET | `/api/conclave/a2a/agent-card` | Authenticated A2A Agent Card ("Heraldry") — not the public, unauthenticated `/.well-known/agent-card.json` convention. Path follows `ServerPath`; the effective value is reported by `/api/conclave/status` and `/api/meta`. `capabilities.pushNotifications` is advertised only when `Arcanum:Integrations:A2A:PushNotifications` is on. |
| POST | `/api/conclave/a2a/callbacks/{configId}` | **The one A2A route outside the API-key filter.** A remote agent posts an outbound Sending's task transitions here; the caller is a peer agent, which does not hold this instance's operator API key, so the route authenticates on a 256-bit per-Sending secret presented in `X-A2A-Notification-Token` and compared in constant time against a stored digest. **202** when accepted, **404** for a wrong secret or a callback nobody is waiting on (the endpoint never confirms which config ids exist). Mapped only when `Arcanum:Integrations:A2A:PushNotifications` is on; path follows `ServerPath`. DESIGN §5.7.1.4. |
| GET | `/api/workspaces` | List registered workspaces (`ApiResponse<WorkspaceInfo[]>`; §8.17). |
| GET | `/api/workspaces/{id}` | Workspace metadata (`ApiResponse<WorkspaceInfo>`; **404** when missing). |
| POST | `/api/workspaces` | Register a workspace directory (`ApiResponse<WorkspaceInfo>`; **201** with `Location`; **400** validation). |
| PUT | `/api/workspaces/{id}` | Update workspace name/type (`ApiResponse<WorkspaceInfo>`; **404** when missing). |
| DELETE | `/api/workspaces/{id}` | Unregister workspace (**204** on success; **404** when missing). |
| GET | `/api/workspaces/{id}/files` | List files in a registered workspace (`ApiResponse<FileListResult>`; optional `relativePath`, `recursive`, `searchPattern`, and opaque `cursor`; §8.17). Each response contains at most 500 entries plus `nextCursor` / `continuationAction` when more remain; reuse the same list arguments with `cursor=nextCursor`. The cursor is bound to that scope and exact last entry, so earlier mutations do not shift later pages; a vanished checkpoint returns `Workspace.ContinuationCheckpointMissing` with a restart-without-cursor action. |
| GET | `/api/workspaces/{id}/files/info` | File or directory metadata (`ApiResponse<FileEntry>`; optional `relativePath`; §8.17). |
| GET | `/api/workspaces/{id}/files/contents` | Read file contents as UTF-8 text (`ApiResponse<FileReadResult>`; required `relativePath`; §8.17). |
| HEAD | `/api/workspaces/{id}/files/contents` | Size/freshness check for a file (required `relativePath`): **200** with `Content-Length` (the file's size) and `Last-Modified` headers and no body, so it is a non-envelope route. A failure answers with its status and, as for any HEAD request, no body. |
| PUT | `/api/workspaces/{id}/files/contents` | Create or overwrite a file (`ApiResponse<FileWriteResult>`; **200**; required `relativePath` plus a `FileWriteRequest` body; gated by `Arcanum:Workspaces:EnableFileWrite`, otherwise **403** `Workspace.FileWriteDisabled`). A body whose `content` is missing or null is **400** `Validation.InvalidBody`. |
| PATCH | `/api/workspaces/{id}/files/contents` | Replace a verbatim text block in an existing file (`ApiResponse<TextBlockReplaceResult>`; **200**; required `relativePath`; §8.17). A body whose `oldString` or `newString` is missing or null is **400** `Validation.InvalidBody`; a target that changed between the route's read and its replace is **409** `Workspace.FileChanged`; a replaced file whose projected size (every occurrence replaced) would exceed `MaxFileWriteSizeBytes` is **413** `Workspace.FileTooLarge`, refused before the replacement is built and with nothing written. |
| DELETE | `/api/workspaces/{id}/files` | Delete a file or directory (`ApiResponse<FileDeleteResult>`; **200**; required `relativePath`; optional `recursive`; §8.17). |
| POST | `/api/workspaces/{id}/files/directory` | Create a directory, including parents (`ApiResponse<DirectoryCreateResult>`; **201**; required `relativePath`; §8.17). |
| POST | `/api/workspaces/{id}/files/divine` | Semantic search over a workspace's indexed files (`ApiResponse<WorkspaceSearchResult[]>`; body `WorkspaceSemanticSearchRequest` with required `query` and optional `limit`). |
| POST | `/api/workspaces/{id}/files/index` | Queue background reconciliation via `WorkspaceIndexingService.QueueIndexNow` (`ApiResponse<bool>`; **202** for both accepted and coalesced demand). Scheduled, watcher, and manual work share two active workspace handles; additional distinct workspaces wait in FIFO order and capacity does not produce an error. Closed indexing intake or dispatch failure returns **503** `Workspace.IndexingUnavailable`. Accepted work is host-owned and continues after client disconnect; maintenance defers it without losing its identity. |
| GET | `/api/workspaces/{id}/files/index/status` | Read-only indexing status for a workspace (`ApiResponse<WorkspaceIndexStatusDto>`): vector mode/diagnostic, `IndexingEnabled`, durable file/chunk counts, and volatile `Watching`/`Degraded`/`Overflowed`/`Reconciling` plus last-event/last-success timestamps. |
| GET | `/api/workspaces/{id}/files/chunks` | Bounded, paginated chunk previews for a workspace (`ApiResponse<WorkspaceFileChunkPage>`; optional `relativePath` filter, clamped) including character offsets and one-based source line ranges. |
| GET | `/api/unseen-servant/jobs` | List Unseen Servant jobs with base and effective polling intervals (**canonical** Unseen Servant pacer API; §8.15). |
| POST | `/api/unseen-servant/jobs/{name}/initiative` | Set adaptive initiative (dynamic interval) for a job by name; returns updated status (`ApiResponse<UnseenServantJobStatusDto>`). **400** `Validation.InvalidBody` when `intervalMinutes` is outside **1..10080**; **404** `Daemon.NotFound` when `{name}` is not configured under `Arcanum:Daemon:Jobs` (§8.4). |
| GET | `/api/daemons` | List registered daemon jobs (`ApiResponse<DaemonJobInfo[]>`; **plural** `daemons` — registry; §8.15). |
| GET | `/api/daemons/{id}` | Daemon job metadata (`ApiResponse<DaemonJobInfo>`; **404** when missing). |
| POST | `/api/daemons/{id}/run` | Run a daemon job on demand; returns `ApiResponse<DaemonExecutionSummary>` with execution id (**400** when not found, disabled, or already running on-demand). |
| GET | `/api/daemons/{id}/history` | Execution history for a daemon (`ApiResponse<DaemonExecutionSummary[]>`). |
| GET | `/api/executions/{id}` | Execution detail (`ApiResponse<DaemonExecutionDetail>`; **404** when missing). |
| POST | `/api/executions/{id}/cancel` | Cancel a running execution; returns updated `ApiResponse<DaemonExecutionSummary>` (**400** `Daemon.NotRunning` when not running). Cancellation is cooperative: the response records the execution terminal, but the job body may still be unwinding, and the daemon keeps its single-flight reservation until it actually returns — so a `POST /api/daemons/{id}/run` issued during that window still fails `Daemon.AlreadyRunning` rather than starting a second headless turn against the same target spell. |
| GET | `/api/logs` | Paginated in-memory log query (`ApiResponse<LogQueryResult>`; optional `minLevel`, `category`, `from`, `to`, `search`, `limit`, `beforeSequence`; §8.16). |
| GET | `/api/audit` | Persisted inference audit log query (`ApiResponse<InferenceAuditRecord[]>`; optional `from`, `to`, `model`, `sessionId`, `limit` up to the 1,000-record page, and opaque `cursor`; continue with `X-Arcanum-Next-Cursor`; §8.26). |
| GET | `/api/guardrails/audit` | Persisted guardrails violation audit log query (`ApiResponse<GuardrailAuditRecord[]>`; optional `from`, `to`, `stage`, `violationType`, `sessionId`, `limit` up to the 1,000-record page, and opaque `cursor`; continue with `X-Arcanum-Next-Cursor`; §8.27). |
| GET | `/api/operations` | List durable operations with optional `kind`, `state`, `limit`, and `offset` filters. Returns safe summaries only; encrypted checkpoint payloads and references are never serialized (DESIGN §10.8). |
| GET | `/api/operations/{id}` | Show one durable operation's lifecycle, links, lease, attempt, checkpoint version/presence, safe summary, and terminal error code. |
| POST | `/api/operations/{id}/cancel` | CAS-protected transition to `Cancelling`; **404** unknown. Current owner-bound `data-retention-mutation` V4 and `data-retention-factory-reset` V2 checkpoints return **409** `Operation.StateConflict` and remain unchanged. Their refusal explains authenticated ownership and, for nonterminal rows, directs the operator to resolve the blocker, restart the host for same-operation authenticated recovery, then re-check `operation show`; restart cannot repair an unresolved blocker or missing evidence. Terminal rows are described as recorded outcomes. Ordinary operations and older retention checkpoint versions retain CAS cancellation. Other stale or terminal transitions also return **409** `Operation.StateConflict`. |
| POST | `/api/operations/{id}/retry` | CAS-protected reset of eligible `Failed`, `Abandoned`, or `ReconciliationRequired` operations to `Pending`; **404** unknown. Current owner-bound `data-retention-mutation` V4 and `data-retention-factory-reset` V2 checkpoints cannot be retried: they return **409** `Operation.StateConflict` and remain unchanged, with the same authenticated-owner guidance and terminal distinction as cancel. Ordinary operations and older retention checkpoint versions retain CAS retry with their checkpoint intact. Other stale or ineligible transitions also return **409** `Operation.StateConflict`. A clean Long Rest sweep that is still its scope's current sweep, so reset, is settled at once: the retry parks it as `long_rest.readmit_requested` before it answers and wakes the background worker, which readmits it with a fresh failure budget without waiting for the next minute's rescan. The settling pass is a hint: if it fails, the failure is logged and the retry still answers as recorded, and the next recovery pass parks the row. The settling pass is the reconciler's ordinary recovery pass, run serially: before the response it settles every operation then eligible for recovery, of any kind, as `POST /api/operations/reconcile` does, so the latency of a retry grows with the recovery backlog, and the pass's summary is neither returned nor recorded as the last reconciliation. A protected sweep returns to `long_rest.authorized_request_required` and waits for a fresh eligible Session request. Retrying a sweep that a newer sweep has replaced leaves a `ReconciliationRequired` operation that nothing readmits, and only `cancel` settles it (§8.36). |
| POST | `/api/operations/reconcile` | Run the ordinary recovery pass in bounded pages and return `LongRunningOperationReconciliationSummary`, with an additional read-only nullable Int64 `ownerRecoveryRequired` count of interrupted owner-bound rows excluded from execution discovery. The manual endpoint measures it after the pass: current mutation V4 / factory V2, absent/expired lease, and Running/Waiting/Cancelling, attempted Pending, or ReconciliationRequired with Data.ReconciliationFailed / Covenant.MaintenanceFailed. It is separate from examined/skipped; null or absence means unmeasured (background summaries/older hosts), not zero. Read failure fails the request. The count conveys no recovery authority. It parks an interrupted Long Rest sweep and never runs one (§8.36). |
| GET | `/api/events/daemon` | SSE stream of `DaemonEvent` frames (daemon job lifecycle for scheduled and on-demand runs; CLI `watch daemons`); **not** wrapped in `ApiResponse<T>`. |
| GET | `/api/events/mcp` | SSE stream of `McpServerEvent` frames (MCP server lifecycle; CLI `watch mcp`); **not** wrapped in `ApiResponse<T>`. |
| GET | `/api/events/logs` | SSE stream of `LogEntry` frames after an initial `: connected` comment (live log tail from ring buffer; optional nullable `LogLevel` `level` plus free-form `category` and `search`; CLI `watch logs`); **not** wrapped in `ApiResponse<T>`. An unknown nonblank `level` returns **400** `Validation.InvalidQuery` before SSE headers are written. |
| POST | `/api/commlink/send` | Dispatch a **Comm Link** alert (`CommLinkMessageRequestDto`); **200** + `ApiResponse<bool>`; **400** validation; **502** + envelope on any dispatch failure: `CommLink.WebhookHttpError` (the receiver answered a non-success status), `CommLink.WebhookException` (the POST failed before a status was read) or `CommLink.DispatcherException` (a sink threw unexpectedly; the other sinks were still tried). A cancellation by the caller before any sink has delivered propagates; once a sink has delivered, the answer is still success (§8.23). |
| POST | `/api/tools/invoke` | Diagnostic built-in tool invocation (`ApiResponse<ToolInvokeResponse>`; DESIGN §11.27). |
| POST | `/api/providers/test` | Read-only provider connectivity probe (`ApiResponse<ProviderTestResult>`; body `endpoint`, optional `apiKey`, `type` = `OpenAICompatible`; does not write `arcanum.json`; DESIGN §19). URL rejection is **400** with `Validation.InvalidUrl`, `Provider.DnsFailed`, or `Security.BlockedOutboundUrl` and a sanitized remedy. An admitted probe answers **200** with `isReachable: false` and an actionable `error` for transport, TLS, authentication, or model-list failures. Only a valid OpenAI model list is reachable; `data: []` is valid. The outbound HTTP request and response-body read share a five-second deadline and a 4 MiB body cap; expiry returns the timeout diagnostic, while caller cancellation propagates. |
| POST | `/api/proving-grounds/trials/run` | Run an ephemeral **Trial** through **The Proving Grounds** (`Trial` body → `ApiResponse<TrialResult>`; DESIGN §20). |
| POST | `/api/proving-grounds/memory-evaluations/run` | Run an exact paired memory comparison (`MemoryEvaluationRequest` → `ApiResponse<MemoryEvaluationResponse>`; §8.21). |
| POST | `/api/proving-grounds/memory-evaluations/get` | Read retained protected evaluation status/report by body identity (§8.21). |
| POST | `/api/proving-grounds/memory-evaluations/delete` | Confirm exact terminal report-only cleanup by returned digest (§8.21). |
| POST | `/v1/chat/completions` | OpenAI-compatible chat (JSON or SSE); **not** wrapped in `ApiResponse<T>`. |
| POST | `/v1/embeddings` | OpenAI-compatible embeddings; **not** wrapped in `ApiResponse<T>`. |
| POST | `/v1/moderations` | Always **501** `not_supported`; no configuration setting enables it. |
| POST | `/v1/images/generations` | Always **501** `not_supported`. |
| POST | `/v1/images/edits` | Always **501** `not_supported`. |
| POST | `/v1/images/variations` | Always **501** `not_supported`. |
| POST | `/v1/audio/transcriptions` | Always **501** `not_supported`. |
| POST | `/v1/audio/translations` | Always **501** `not_supported`. |
| POST | `/v1/audio/speech` | Always **501** `not_supported`. |
| POST | `/v1/files` | Upload standalone file storage, `multipart/form-data`. `purpose` accepts any non-empty value except the reserved batch-artifact purposes `batch_output` and `error`, which are owned by `/v1/batches`' publisher and encrypted under a different blob purpose — those return **400** `invalid_value` on `param: "purpose"` rather than storing bytes that could never be read back. Publication captures the new owned-file identity before waiting for the database writer and revalidates it under the same immediate transaction that inserts metadata. A concurrent reset/delete that wins causes a sanitized **500** `internal_error` with no metadata row; **201** + `OpenAiFileObject` means the committed metadata and exact encrypted bytes were visible together. |
| GET | `/v1/files` | List uploaded files, optional `?purpose=` filter. |
| GET | `/v1/files/{id}` | File metadata; **404** for unknown/malformed id. |
| DELETE | `/v1/files/{id}` | Deletes metadata + owned bytes only when no batch input/output/error role references the file. Every batch insert conditionally resolves all supplied file roles, and every artifact-reference update conditionally resolves its new output/error roles, in the same database write; a concurrent delete therefore either observes the committed reference or wins and causes the reference write to be rejected. Bytes move to identity-safe same-parent quarantine around the serialized metadata mutation, are restored on rejection/failure, and are finalized after commit. **200** `deleted: true` means both are absent; terminal or active references return **409** `file_referenced_by_batch`; storage conflict/recovery returns **500** `file_delete_storage_conflict` / `file_delete_recovery_required`. |
| GET | `/v1/files/{id}/content` | Raw bytes; always `Content-Disposition: attachment`. |
| POST | `/v1/batches` | Create an async bulk chat-completion job over an uploaded JSONL file (every input line must carry `method: "POST"` and a `url` equal to the batch's endpoint, and a `custom_id` not used by an earlier line (the `line-N` id a line with no `custom_id` is reported under counts as one); a line that does not is recorded in the error file as `{"line":N,"error":"..."}` and never reaches a provider, with first-seen `custom_id` tracking bounded at 1,048,576 distinct ids per pass); the final insert conditionally rechecks the input metadata in the same database write and returns **404** if a concurrent file deletion won; otherwise **200** + `OpenAiBatchObject`, `status: "validating"`. |
| GET | `/v1/batches` | Newest-first metadata page; optional `status`, `limit` (default 20, maximum 100), and opaque status-bound `after`; returns `has_more` plus `next_cursor`. |
| GET | `/v1/batches/{id}` | Batch status + durable 64-bit `request_counts` without opening artifacts; **404** for unknown/malformed id. An unexpected processing failure (not host shutdown, not a failed accounting settlement, not a maintenance closure — those three keep the batch `in_progress` for durable recovery, which reconciles its reservation) ends the batch as `failed` rather than leaving it `in_progress`: completed lines publish in `output_file_id`, the in-doubt line(s) appear there as `batch_interrupted_after_dispatch` naming only the exception type, and `error_file_id` carries a `{"line":N,"error":"..."}` note identifying the first line that never started as the continuation boundary; when reading the input itself failed, so no line was prepared, that boundary is the line after the last page that finished. If all page lines started and EOF is not already known, the note says **at or after** the next physical line, without asserting another record exists. At known EOF it adds no continuation checkpoint or count. A resumed batch may already own paid checkpoints at the boundary or later: the note uses an unused slot, retains the boundary in its text, and requires checking all saved output/errors (including later lines) before resubmitting only requests never dispatched. An unexpected diagnostic insertion refusal preserves durable work and propagates the original fault; a cancellation-caused refusal instead preserves cancelled-result publication. Missing/unreadable input at open gets a diagnostic at the first unused checkpoint slot (line 1 on a fresh batch); that slot is not a known resubmission boundary, and paid checkpoints remain intact. Diagnostic notes count once in total/failed, never as completed/provider work. The batch object has no reason field of its own: the reason is that note, plus the operator log, which keeps the exception. Only `POST /v1/batches/{id}/cancel` produces `cancelled`; a provider-side timeout does not. |
| POST | `/v1/batches/{id}/cancel` | Idempotent cancel; stops in-flight processing within ~2s, seals every claimed unresolved line as `batch_interrupted_after_dispatch` with cancellation-specific text and an explicit-resubmission warning (including current-page checkpoints recovered after cancellation during input open/read), then publishes it before checkpoint cleanup. |
| POST | `/v1/batches/{id}/reset` | Reset a stuck `in_progress` batch back to `validating` (input must be readable and decryptable; **400** `not_found` for missing metadata/file, **400** `invalid_value` for unreadable/undecryptable input with a re-upload/create-new-batch action; **409** if currently in-flight; **200** `OpenAiBatchObject`). A private durable claim keeps the public status OpenAI-compatible while blocking cancellation, worker finalization, and new dispatch; recovery settles exact page-run accounting, resumes completed per-line checkpoints, and seals a previously dispatched line without a result as `batch_interrupted_after_dispatch`. It never replays that ambiguous provider call, and startup resumes a crash left anywhere after the claim (DESIGN §11.21). |
| GET | `/v1/models` | OpenAI-compatible models list (flattened configured models across providers via the same `ModelInfoBuilder` that backs `GET /api/models`); **not** wrapped in `ApiResponse<T>`. |

The delete-entry, pin-entry, unpin-entry and compact mutation routes require `Arcanum:Features:MemoryManagement` (`features.memoryManagement`, default `false`). Their **400** `Session.MemoryManagementDisabled` guidance names both key spellings and says to set the gate to true. This check still runs before resource lookup or mutation; the gate does not enable itself.

**Covenant status is measured, not estimated.** `MemoryStatusDto.covenant` carries `enabled`, `available`, `census`, `counts`, the three rendered byte totals, `renderedByteCeilingPerSection`, search health, retention, and an optional `degradationCode`. The counts come from one content-free census — scope, lane, lifecycle, count — taken under the one installation read capability, because a census that crosses every Campaign needs a capability that does too; a scoped lease is refused rather than answered for part of the installation. Retired heads are counted as retired and contribute no rendered bytes, which the storage guarantees rather than the reader: a tombstone version carries no compiled content by table constraint, versions are append-only, and a trigger pins every head's byte cost to its own version's. No census is attempted at all when `enabled` is false, because the scan opens a canonical connection and that permanently forbids this process the offline host-process-tools transition, which is a heavy price for a count nothing wrote. On an enabled installation it is attempted whatever health is reported, because the operation gate already refuses a capability over a tier it cannot serve and a second health check here would suppress the counts of a degraded-but-readable tier. When it cannot be taken the counts are empty and the whole status call still answers: an operator asking whether their memory works is entitled to that answer even when the number behind it is momentarily unavailable. `census` is what makes that zero readable — `Read`, `Refused`, or `Failed` — because health cannot carry the sentence on its own: the gate refuses the installation capability whenever an exclusive operation is closing the scope, and it does so with the canonical tier reporting itself `Healthy`, so `available: true, counts: []` would otherwise be indistinguishable from an installation that genuinely holds nothing. `globalConfirmedRenderedBytes` is that section's whole cost, while `maxCampaignConfirmedRenderedBytes` and `maxCampaignProposedRenderedBytes` are the largest single Campaign's rather than installation-wide sums, because `renderedByteCeilingPerSection` bounds one Campaign's rendered section on one turn and a sum would exceed it while every Campaign was well inside it. `renderedByteCeilingPerSection` is a single number for all three sections because the three placement ceilings hold the same value, and a domain-contract test pins them equal, so giving one section a ceiling of its own fails there rather than leaving an operator comparing a Campaign total against a Global bound. The object is omitted entirely when no Covenant arm is composed, since an empty count and an absent arm are different facts. `search` (`state`, `executionMode`, `guidance`) is read from the host's published Covenant availability. That is republished after every committed write that moves the search position — an operator set, retire or correct, a review apply, an agent proposal published at turn commit, and an entry erasure — and after every maintenance batch, whether it applied anything or not. The curation route (pin, unpin, mask) moves no part of that position, so it republishes nothing. A write whose search projection is still pending therefore reads `Synchronizing` with `CanonicalFallback` until the next maintenance pass applies it, and `Healthy` with `Fts` afterwards, in the same running host; a fresh installation and a Covenant reset reach `Healthy` the same way, without a restart. `guidance` is `None` whenever search is synchronized: the accelerator is then answering every query, which is also what a search page's own guidance reports. A fresh installation and a Covenant reset record a full rebuild as owed, and the first maintenance pass is what makes that stop mattering: until it has adopted the empty projection, they read `RebuildRequired` on this block and on a search page alike, and `None` afterwards, because the pass that publishes an applied tuple equal to the canonical sequence clears the recorded rebuild in the same statement. An installation whose projection an earlier build adopted keeps the recorded rebuild beside its current tuple until its next maintenance pass, which clears it, and reads `None` throughout. `RebuildRequired` appears only while search is not synchronized, either no applied tuple names the current dataset or the accelerator is not Healthy, and a recorded rebuild is what it is waiting for; a projection that is merely pending behind a published tuple reads `WaitForSynchronization`, whatever rebuild is still recorded; an adoption spread over several bounded passes keeps its recorded rebuild until the last pass reaches the canonical sequence. `POST /api/memory/covenant/query` carries the same `search` object and derives it by the same rule, so the two routes agree on a state. A restore that keeps Covenant entries is the ordinary case for `RebuildRequired`. Nothing in this release starts an index rebuild, and the route that would is among those §8.28 lists as unregistered, so those entries reach the index only once every one of them has been rewritten, which names each head in a pending delta and lets the next maintenance pass adopt the projection. Until then search answers them through the canonical fallback and `guidance` stays `RebuildRequired`. Search results never depend on this field: each query reads the persisted position itself and falls back to the canonical scan whenever the projection is behind.

**Campaign continuity inspection.** Session-narrowed memory status, sources, and explain carry optional `campaignSummary` metadata: `campaignId`, `currentArtifactId`, `revision`, `sourceGeneration`, `sourceCount`, `refoldRequired`, `sensitivity`, and `updatedAtUtc`. This projection reads no summary payload. `SessionSummary` remains the separate compression source; `CampaignSummary` has its own feature eligibility and `CampaignSummaries` retention rule. Retained metadata remains inspectable with injection disabled. A missing current pointer or refold debt makes stored continuity ineligible until a successful publication, rather than reporting an obsolete artifact as next-turn context. No source-inspection route generates a summary.

With `features.campaignRollups` enabled for a Campaign-bound context, or with `Arcanum:Features:LongRest` enabled for any canonical context (a Global-only context included), a Session-backed inference turn with the default context policy and no nonempty stateless message list uses a durable Session claim, including its first turn when `sessionId` is omitted. Buffered and streaming responses carry `Cache-Control: no-store, private`, `Pragma: no-cache`, and `Expires: 0`, with validators removed before the first byte or idle heartbeat. They bypass generic response-body replay and check the claim's authoritative native answer on every retry. A terminal interruption remains a failure on retry and permits no new provider call. A stateless transcript and the no-context marker are not eligible.

**JSON wire shape (`/api` and shared primitives):** JSON endpoints under `/api` use the `ApiResponse<T>` envelope (`Data`, `IsSuccess`, `Error`, `TraceId`) except for these non-envelope routes:

| Route | Wire format | Section |
|-------|-------------|---------|
| `POST /api/intelligence/ping-stream` | NDJSON event lines (`application/x-ndjson`) | §8.5 |
| `POST /api/web/research` | NDJSON `WebResearchStreamFrame` lines (`limits`, `progress`, `result`, or `error`) | §8.10.3 / DESIGN §11.27 |
| `POST /api/spells/{name}/execute-stream` | NDJSON `IntelligenceEvent` lines (`application/x-ndjson`) | DESIGN §19 |
| `POST /api/prompts/{id}/execute-stream` | NDJSON `IntelligenceEvent` lines (`application/x-ndjson`) | DESIGN §19 |
| `GET /api/events/daemon` | SSE `DaemonEvent` frames (`text/event-stream`) | §8.11 |
| `GET /api/events/mcp` | SSE `McpServerEvent` frames (`text/event-stream`) | §8.13 |
| `GET /api/events/logs` | SSE `LogEntry` frames (`text/event-stream`) | §8.16 |
| `GET /api/sessions/{id}/stream` | SSE entry frames (`text/event-stream`) | DESIGN §11.16 |
| `GET /api/apprentices/{id}/chronicle` | SSE Chronicle frames (`text/event-stream`) | DESIGN §5.7 |
| `GET /api/openapi/v1.json` / `GET /api/scalar` | OpenAPI document and Scalar UI (not application `ApiResponse`). The OpenAPI document is always mapped; the Scalar UI is mapped only when `Arcanum:Features:ScalarUi` is true (default false) | DESIGN §11.5 |
| `GET /api/sessions/{id}/attachments/{attachmentId}/content` | The stored attachment snapshot's raw bytes under its stored MIME type (`application/octet-stream` when none), as a download with `Cache-Control: no-store` and `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`; no range processing | §8.18.1 |
| `GET /api/presence` | Bodyless: **204** with `X-Arcanum-Presence-Version`, `-Authority`, `-Proof` and `-Capability` response headers, or an empty **400** / **404** / **503** | §1 table |
| `HEAD /api/workspaces/{id}/files/contents` | Bodyless: **200** with `Content-Length` (the file's size) and `Last-Modified` headers; a failure answers with its status and no body | §1 table / §8.17 |
| `GET /metrics` | Prometheus text exposition (`text/plain; version=0.0.4`); outside `/api`, and a **404** when metrics are disabled | §8.22 |
| `GET /api/conclave/a2a/agent-card` | The A2A SDK's `AgentCard` JSON, not an envelope. Mapped when `Arcanum:Features:A2AServer` is true, which derives `Arcanum:Features:Conclave` | §1 table / DESIGN §5.7.1 |
| `POST /api/conclave/a2a` | A2A JSON-RPC 2.0 over POST, with SSE (`text/event-stream`) for the streaming methods. Same gate (`Arcanum:Features:A2AServer`); the mount point follows `Arcanum:Integrations:A2A:ServerPath` | §1 table / DESIGN §5.7.1 |
| `POST /v1/chat/completions` | OpenAI-shaped JSON or `text/event-stream` | §1 table |
| `GET /v1/models` | OpenAI-shaped JSON list | §1 table |

Envelope-payload specifics:

- **`GET /api/meta`** wraps **`InstanceMetadataDto`** (version, OS, runtime, process identity, Grimoire paths, effective host binding, and intelligence feature flags). `ArchiveSearchEnabled` is read from the resolved intelligence settings; `LoreSystemEnabled`, `ContextCompressionEnabled` and `TokenTrackingEnabled`, which no setting could change, were removed. `EmbeddingsVectorMode` / `EmbeddingsVectorDiagnostic` identify sqlite-vec versus the complete streamed managed fallback; the retained compatibility integer `EmbeddingsManagedSearchRowBudget` is `0`, meaning no total row budget.
- **`GET /api/health`** wraps **`HealthReportDto`** even when overall status is Unhealthy. An Unhealthy report returns HTTP **503** with `IsSuccess: true` and populated `Data`; clients must not discard that readiness snapshot merely because the status is non-2xx. The **`FileEncryption`** component reports the already-published `Pending`, `Deferred`, `Ready`, or `Unavailable` runtime state in constant time; health never opens secure storage, walks blob files, or performs decryption. The **`DurableOperations`** component's detail carries the last reconciliation summary *and* the states that pass could not fix — `stale=<n>`, `awaiting_repair=<n>`, per-kind repair guidance keyed by terminal error code, and any operation kind with no registered recovery handler (DESIGN §10.8.3). It is `Degraded` whenever any of those is non-empty. A parked Long Rest sweep is an operation awaiting repair, so it degrades the component for as long as it is parked: a clean sweep the background worker will readmit after its cooldown (no action is needed unless it is parked as `long_rest.readmit_exhausted`, under a needs-operator code, or as a `long_rest.interrupted` crash park whose failure budget is already spent) and a protected sweep waiting for its next eligible Session request included. The detail is drawn only from the closed kind/state/error-code vocabularies: it never contains an operation id, a public summary, or checkpoint content. The **`LongRest`** component is present only while `Arcanum:Features:LongRest` is on: `Healthy` when the chat (`FastModel`, else `DefaultModel`, else the first advertised model) and embedding recipe resolves, `Degraded` with `LongRest: enabled but recipe unresolved: …` (configuration keys, model and provider names and window sizes, never an endpoint or a credential) when it does not: no OpenAI-compatible provider advertises the chat model, `Integrations:Embeddings` lacks a configured provider, a model or positive dimensions, a chat provider's `contextWindowLimit` is below 6,775 tokens or a set embedding `contextWindowLimit` is below 1,194 tokens, or the recipe exceeds its supported bounds. It is a warning that the sweep cannot run; it never makes the report `Unhealthy`, and an ordinary turn is unaffected. A Session turn that reads Covenant-protected memory in a scope with protected Long Rest sources fails with `LongRest.Unavailable` until the recipe resolves, because protected maintenance must finish before such a turn begins; the detail says so. A `FastModel` that no OpenAI-compatible provider advertises, or that a Familiar provider serves or is passed through to, leaves the recipe unresolved rather than falling back to `DefaultModel`.
- **`GET /api/config`** / **`PUT /api/config`** / **`POST /api/config/validate`** use **`ArcanumSettings`** as the payload type (§8.12). Read masks provider endpoints and returns only environment-variable references for provider credentials, HTTPS certificate passwords, and CommLink—not their secret values. Raw bodies fail closed on every unknown/obsolete path before source-generated deserialization; writes merge only endpoint masks.
- **`DELETE /api/sessions/{id}`** returns **204** with no body on success (soft-delete archive; idempotent — DESIGN §11.16); **`POST /api/sessions/{id}/rest`** returns **202** with `ApiResponse<bool>` when the job is queued, or **503** with `Session.RestQueueFull` when enqueue is rejected.
- **`POST /api/commlink/send`** returns **502** with `ApiResponse<bool>` when the outbound webhook HTTP call fails (`CommLink.WebhookHttpError` for a non-success status, `CommLink.WebhookException` for a transport error) or a sink throws unexpectedly (`CommLink.DispatcherException`); every message is fixed and carries no URL, exception text or sink detail.

**Daemon route families:** **`/api/unseen-servant/*`** manages Unseen Servant job **configuration** and runtime scheduling intervals (`GET /api/unseen-servant/jobs`, `POST /api/unseen-servant/jobs/{name}/initiative`). **`/api/daemons/*`** and **`/api/executions/*`** are the daemon job **registry** and **execution history** API for all registered `IDaemonJob` types (§8.15).

The `/api` and `/v1` groups are protected by the API key (DESIGN §11.3), including the OpenAPI document and Scalar reference UI on `/api` (`MapOpenApi` is always registered, and `MapScalarApiReference` only when `Arcanum:Features:ScalarUi` is true (default false), both on the keyed group, so browsers need a valid API key like any other `/api` caller). The sole local-authentication exception is `GET /api/presence`: after requiring the actual TCP peer to be loopback, it receives a random nonce and returns a nonce/authority-bound proof plus an encrypted short-lived process capability; it never receives or returns the reusable key. The normal key check runs as **middleware, before parameter binding**, so an unauthenticated request is answered **401 `Auth.Unauthorized` regardless of its body**: the body is never read, buffered, or deserialized first, and a malformed body no longer yields a binding `400` ahead of the `401`. Endpoint **matching** still precedes the key check, so `404` (no route), `405` (wrong method), and `415` (`Content-Type` the route does not accept) remain reachable without a key — none of them touch the body. `ApiKeyEndpointFilter` remains attached to the same routes as defence in depth. Immediately after the authentication stage, and still before any later decision, every matched, non-exempt `/api` and `/v1` route passes **Grimoire request admission** (§8.31): while a database transformation owns connection admission the request is refused with **503** before its endpoint runs, and the three statuses above stay reachable because a request that matched no route this host mapped is left alone. Legacy `X-Arcanum-Key` remains accepted for external clients; Arcanum's own long-lived CLI clients bootstrap through `/api/presence`, retain only the capability plus a fixed-size digest, renew single-flight before expiry, and send only `X-Arcanum-Process-Capability`. Their centralized authenticated sender binds every request to the presence-proof authority, rejects pre-populated credential headers, refreshes after one `401`, and replays at most once only for a request explicitly marked replayable. Its named clients disable redirects and proxies. Sending both credential forms is rejected as ambiguous. Capability validation is constant-time and stateless; capabilities expire and every server restart invalidates the prior process's tokens.

---

### Saga consolidation and ranking status

`GET /api/memory/status[/{sessionId}]` also returns optional `schemaTransitions`: `{ journalAvailable, runs }`. Each run contains `tier`, `fromVersion`, `completedThroughVersion`, `targetVersion`, `sweep`, `rowsProcessed`, nullable `lastErrorCode`, and `updatedAtUtc`. These are committed, content-free journal facts; no source cursor, fingerprint, percentage or exception message is exposed. Unknown sweep/error text is replaced by a fixed safe label. `journalAvailable: false` means the older database has no journal, not that no work remains. `storeCensusAvailable` defaults to true for compatible older responses. While a Core transition remains, it is false and `stores` is empty, `sagaRanking` and Session title are null; those values are unmeasured, not zero or a missing Session determination. The schema metadata remains readable while later store tables are not yet installed. An empty journal resumes ordinary store/Session inspection.

`GET /api/memory/status[/{sessionId}]` preserves the existing retained per-store counts and adds optional `MemoryStatusDto.sagaRanking` (`SagaRankingStatusDto`). A store composition or pre-Long Rest schema that does not expose the census returns null rather than inventing counts. The block contains `policyVersion`, `enabled`, `asOfUtc`, `cleanOnly`, `memoryCount`, `versionCount`, `consolidatedVersionCount`, `dormantVersionCount`, `positiveCreditVersionCount`, `negativeCreditVersionCount`, and `uncorroboratedBenefitVersionCount`.

The census reads all clean metadata rows in the resolved canonical memory scope, intersected with the exact selected Session when supplied; it is not the 50-row explanation page. `memoryCount` includes retained legacy rows; `versionCount` counts only rows with a retained current Annals version. All classification counts describe versions. These are independent dimensions, not mutually exclusive buckets that sum to `versionCount`; a retained consolidated version can also be dormant. `consolidatedVersionCount` includes equivalent-observation and explicit-supersession suppression. Positive and negative credit count the sign of the bounded usefulness modifier; uncorroborated benefit remains separate and earns no positive credit. With ranking disabled, dormancy and evidence-credit classifications are zero while exact consolidation remains authoritative.

`cleanOnly` is true: protected current versions and normalized sensitivity-labelled identities contribute neither identities nor classifications. The existing Saga store count can therefore exceed this census; it still measures retained rows under its existing Session filter. The new block contains no text, source digest, version coordinate or protected classification. One read snapshot and policy instant supply the full census with constant-space counters and the existing bounded evidence window. No embedding or model query runs.

### Saga ranking explanation

`GET /api/memory/explain[/{sessionId}]` accepts optional `sagaId` (one nonempty exact Saga identity, at most 1,024 UTF-8 bytes, no controls) and `similarity` (one finite invariant-culture cosine in [-1,1], default 0). Repeated parameters or invalid values return **400** `Validation.InvalidBody`. Authentication and conditional Covenant read-authority requirements are unchanged. This request runs no embedding/model call.

`MemoryExplainDto.sagaRanking` is `SagaRankingExplanationDto`: `policyVersion`, `enabled`, `asOfUtc`, `referenceSimilarity`, `minModifier` (-0.09), `maxModifier` (+0.13), `memories`, `hasMore`. Each memory contains `memoryId`, `eligible`, optional `eligibility` (the closed `SagaRetrievalEligibility` string), and `components`: `policyVersion`, raw `similarity`, `score`, `usefulnessModifier`, `recencyModifier`, `originModifier`, `dormant`, `temporallyValid`, `evidenceClass`, `benefitCount`, `harmCount`, `noEffectCount`, `decayExempt`, and null `versionId`. Evidence classes are closed strings: `disabled`, `unversioned`, `none`, `exposure-only`, `counterfactual-uncorroborated`, `counterfactual`, `invalid`. The explicit reference cosine explains the formula; it is not the score of an unspecified future query.

`eligibility` distinguishes `Retired`, `OwnershipUnresolved`, `EmbeddingMissing`, `Consolidated`, `OutsideValidity`, `Dormant`, and `Eligible` using the same classifier as search/detail. Explicit retirement remains ineligible even when Long Rest is disabled; residual embeddings cannot restore a retired source to search or hydration.

With `sagaId` omitted, metadata is newest-first with ordinal identity ties, limited to 50 clean rows in the resolved scope; `hasMore` reports truncation. An absent, out-of-scope or protected identity contributes no item. No content, source digest, protected identity or exact version is returned. Optional `SagaMemoryDto.ranking` and `MemorySearchResultDto.sagaRanking` carry the same content-free policy components at reference cosine 0 while enabled; the owning inspection routes retain their existing protection authority. Internal use coordinates are JSON-ignored. DESIGN §21.16.6 defines evidence, formula, bounds, default-off parity and decay exemptions.

## 8. HTTP JSON and Minimal API design (`Api` project)

### 8.1 Wire contract: the `ApiResponse<T>` envelope

```csharp
public sealed record ApiResponse<T>(T? Data, bool IsSuccess, Error? Error, string? TraceId = null);
```

- **`ApiResponse<T>`** is the default envelope for JSON under **`/api`**; streaming and OpenAI compatibility are exceptions (§1, §8.5). `sealed record` for value equality and immutability.
- `Error?` is literal `null` on success. `TraceId` from `Activity.Current?.Id ?? HttpContext.TraceIdentifier`.
- `ApiResponse<T>.FromResult` is the single mapping point from `Result<T>` to wire envelope.
- **404 bodies:** JSON routes under `/api` return an `ApiResponse<T>` envelope on **404** (for example `Campaign.NotFound`, `Session.NotFound`) — not an empty body. Use `Results.Json(..., ArcanumJsonContext.Default.ApiResponse…, statusCode: 404)` or `Results.NotFound(envelope)` so clients always receive `isSuccess`, `error`, and `traceId`.
- **Media type:** JSON routes that read the request body themselves go through `ApiRequestJson.ReadAsync`, which requires a JSON `Content-Type` before reading. A route that must inspect the raw `JsonDocument` tree instead (`PUT /api/config`, `POST /api/config/validate`, which run `ConfigurationValidator.RejectObsoleteJsonKeys` before deserializing) applies the same check and returns the same `ApiRequestJson.UnsupportedMediaTypeResult` envelope first; because `JsonDocument` decodes nothing itself, a body sent in another charset .NET can decode (for example `utf-16`) is transcoded to UTF-8 before it is parsed, as `ReadFromJsonAsync` does on every other route, rather than read as UTF-8 regardless. A missing or non-JSON media type, or a JSON one whose `charset` parameter names an encoding .NET cannot decode (`windows-1252`, `shift_jis` and `gbk` among them, a quoted value, or a made-up name; `utf-8` is always readable), is a **415** `ApiResponse<T>` failure envelope carrying `Validation.UnsupportedMediaType` and the message `Request body must be sent with 'Content-Type: application/json' and, if it names a charset, one this server can decode (UTF-8 always is).`, not an unhandled framework `InvalidOperationException` surfacing as **500** `Hub.Unhandled`. The check (`ApiRequestJson.HasReadableJsonContentType`) runs before the read and follows the framework's own charset resolution, so nothing after it catches `InvalidOperationException`: one that still escapes a read is a fault of the server's own and stays a logged **500**. A JSON-typed but unparsable body remains **400** `Validation.InvalidBody` (`Request body could not be parsed as valid JSON.`), and a well-formed body that deserializes to null remains **400** `Validation.InvalidBody`.
- **Bound-body routes:** most `/api` writes let minimal-API binding read the body instead (a record parameter). The framework's generated reader answers a failed read in one of two ways, and the host covers both so the response carries the same `ApiResponse<T>` envelope in every environment. A body that is not valid JSON for the parameter, a request with no `Content-Type` at all, a required body that was never sent, and a query, route or header value the binder cannot convert, are *thrown*: the host pins `RouteHandlerOptions.ThrowOnBadRequest` on, so they reach `ArcanumExceptionHandler`'s `BadHttpRequestException` arm rather than the framework's empty status (outside Development) or a logged **500** `Hub.Unhandled` (inside it), and are answered with the framework's status and the envelope: **400** `Validation.InvalidBody` (`Request body could not be parsed as valid JSON.`, `Request body is required.` for the missing body, or `Request parameters could not be bound to this route.` for an unconvertible or absent query, route or header value), or **415** `Validation.UnsupportedMediaType` for the missing `Content-Type`. Two other faults are never thrown, whatever `ThrowOnBadRequest` says. Everything Kestrel raises *while the generated reader pulls the body* is recorded as a status on the response and returned; and a `Content-Type` the route does not declare it accepts never reaches the route at all, because routing's accepts policy answers it with a bare **415** before the route is chosen. `UseArcanumExceptionHandler` installs a status-code hook that puts the envelope on those otherwise empty responses, for **408** `Validation.BodyReadTimeout` (a body under the minimum data rate), **413** `Validation.BodyTooLarge` (a body past the ceiling) and **431** `Validation.RequestHeadersTooLarge` (trailers over the header ceiling) on a matched endpoint, and for **415** `Validation.UnsupportedMediaType` with the message `The request's Content-Type is not accepted by this route.` (a multipart route reaches it too, so the wording does not say JSON). The A2A server's JSON-RPC route answers a body that is not JSON itself, in its own protocol, so the hook never has a 415 of its own to wrap. The hook touches no other status: a route can return a bodyless **400** on purpose (`GET /api/presence`), and a body that ends early on a bound route is also a bare **400**, because it cannot be told from that and the client that dropped the connection is no longer there to read it. A JSON `Content-Type` whose `charset` .NET cannot decode raises `InvalidOperationException` inside the generated reader, which has no pre-check of its own; the exception handler answers it **415** `Validation.UnsupportedMediaType` only when the request itself is JSON-typed with an unreadable charset and carries a body, and any other `InvalidOperationException` stays the logged **500**. The framework's own wording is never echoed, the answer is logged at Debug rather than Error, and a response that has already started is left alone. Under `/v1` the same paths write the OpenAI error shape (`invalid_request_error`, code `payload_too_large`, `unsupported_media_type` or `invalid_request`). A route that reads its body itself therefore differs from a bound one in the 415 wording and in answering a truncated body.
- **Host header:** A loopback-only host (`Arcanum:Host:ListenAny` and `ARCANUM_HOST_ANY` both off) refuses, before any other middleware or route, a request whose `Host` header names anything but `localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `[::1]` or the host of `Arcanum:Integrations:A2A:PushCallbackBaseUrl` (a port is ignored; the callback name counts only while `Arcanum:Integrations:A2A:PushNotifications` is on, which is what maps the callback route): the framework's host-filtering middleware answers a bare **400**. The socket-peer gates cannot stop a page that has rebound its own DNS name to 127.0.0.1, because that browser really is on the same machine; only the name it sends gives it away. The callback name lets a same-host proxy or tunnel that relays the public name a peer was told to post to (`reverse_proxy`, cloudflared) reach `POST {ServerPath}/callbacks/{configId}`, and only that route: any other request addressed to the callback name gets the same bare **400**, in whichever spelling it arrives (an internationalised name configured as `https://bücher.example` is answered, and confined, as `xn--bcher-kva.example`). The dev host binds loopback whatever these settings say, so it applies the loopback list even when `ListenAny` or `ARCANUM_HOST_ANY` is set. An all-interfaces bind answers any name and applies no filter: a LAN client or a reverse proxy relaying `arcanum.example.com` is not refused. No other operator-supplied name is accepted on a loopback-only bind.

### 8.2 `ArcanumJsonContext` — source-generated, public

CamelCase source-gen context for HTTP wire types (index 0 of resolver chain). Every `ApiResponse<T>` payload and `/v1` DTO needs `[JsonSerializable]`. Separate contexts: `GrimoireJsonContext`, `ConfigurationJsonContext` (Core), `McpJsonSerializerContext` / `McpConfigJsonSerializerContext` (Infrastructure). `[JsonPropertyName]` only for external snake_case/spec wires (OpenAI `/v1`, MCP JSON-RPC, selected NDJSON tool fields) — not arbitrary `/api` DTOs.

### 8.3 Service registration in `AddArcanumApiServices`

Registers Infrastructure + daemon services, `ApiKeyEndpointFilter`, OpenAPI/JSON (`ArcanumJsonContext` head of chain), named OpenAI `HttpClient`, `IChatClientFactory`, tokenizer, scoped `WizardIntelligenceProvider`. Singletons use `IOptionsMonitor`; scoped/request use `IOptionsSnapshot`.

### 8.4 Returning the envelope from a Minimal API handler

Successful endpoints use `Results.Ok(ApiResponse<T>.FromResult(result, traceId))`. Failable endpoints use `Results.Json` with the source-generated `JsonTypeInfo` and an explicit HTTP status code. No anonymous DTOs; no reflection-based model binding.

**Selected status contracts:**

- **`POST /api/intelligence/ping`** — `ApiResponse<PromptResponseDto>` on every path: **400** for request/reasoning validation, **200** on success, and shared `ArcanumErrorMapper` status for inference failures (for example 404/403/400/409/503/500 by stable code; **409** includes `LongRest.SweepParked` and `LongRest.OperationBusy` when a turn must finish a protected Long Rest partition, §8.36). The payload contract is detailed in §8.10.

- **`POST /api/intelligence/memory-delegation`** — buffered `ApiResponse<PromptResponseDto>` with **200** success; protected **400** `MemoryDelegation.InvalidGrant`, **409** `MemoryDelegation.StaleSource`, **503** `MemoryDelegation.Unavailable`, and the shared authentication, canonical context and inference mappings (§8.10.4).

- **`POST /api/intelligence/human-response`** — **400** validation (including the code-owned answer UTF-8 byte limit); **404** + `ApiResponse<bool>` failure when no waiter exists for `promptId` (`Intelligence.HumanPromptNotFound`); **200** + `ApiResponse<bool>` with `Data: true` when the answer is accepted.

- **`POST /api/unseen-servant/jobs/{name}/initiative`** — `ApiResponse<UnseenServantJobStatusDto>` on every path. **400** `Validation.InvalidBody` for a missing or unparsable body and for an `intervalMinutes` outside the inclusive range **1..10080**; **400** `Validation.InvalidJobName` for a blank route name; **404** `Daemon.NotFound` when no job of that trimmed name is configured under `Arcanum:Daemon:Jobs`, with a message naming `arcanum daemon jobs` as the way to list the configured names; **200** with the recomputed job status once the dynamic interval is applied. `UnseenServantPacer.SetDynamicInterval` is a no-op for an unconfigured name, so the **404** is what keeps a mistyped name from reading as an applied change.

- **`POST /api/mcp/reload`** and **`POST /api/intelligence/arsenal`** — Optional JSON body **`OptionalWorkspaceRequest`** (`{ "workingDirectory": "..." }` only). Responses remain `ApiResponse<T>` as today. The arsenal passes the MCP manager only the *normalized* workspace root: a `workingDirectory` that does not normalize to a workspace is treated as no workspace, so its server statuses come from the global surface alone and no workspace-local MCP configuration is resolved from the raw request text.

### 8.5 NDJSON streaming pipeline

`/api/intelligence/ping-stream` uses NDJSON (`application/x-ndjson`) for real-time token streaming:

- **Server:** Events serialized via `Utf8JsonWriter` + `ArcanumJsonContext`, newline-terminated, flushed per event. Writer: **`InferenceExecuteWriter`** (also used by spell/prompt `execute-stream`).
- **Wire shape:** Each line is an `IntelligenceEvent` with **camelCase string** discriminator **`type`**: **`"status"`**, **`"sessionBound"`**, **`"conversationBound"`** (deprecated alias emitted alongside **`sessionBound`** for one release), **`"context"`**, **`"token"`**, **`"reasoning"`**, **`"result"`**, **`"error"`**, **`"toolCall"`**, **`"toolResult"`**, **`"warded"`**, **`"wardResolved"`**, **`"toolError"`** (tolerated tool exception, emitted immediately before its `toolResult`; DESIGN §10.2.1). `context` carries the latest pre-call `ContextTokenBreakdown`; a second frame for the same call may add provider-reported input and variance after usage arrives. The enum is annotated with `[JsonConverter(typeof(JsonStringEnumConverter<IntelligenceEventType>))]` and per-member `[JsonStringEnumMemberName]` so the AOT JSON source generator emits the canonical strings. **`PingRequest.SessionId`** continues a Grimoire thread; when omitted the hub creates a new session on first assistant turn.
- **Early progress and fallback:** `status`, Session bindings, and pre-call `context` are available before provider output. A connectivity failure can still advance to another compatible provider until actual provider commitment. Exact informational repeats from a later candidate are omitted; a different candidate’s `context` accounting and changed progress remain visible. Receiving an informational frame does not promise that the named candidate will complete the answer.
- **Ward frames:** `type:"warded"` and `type:"wardResolved"` carry `wardId`, `toolName`, and (on resolve) `allowed` / `reason`. Both may carry an additive **`origin`** — `human`, `autoApproved`, `autoDenied`, `timedOut`, `cancelled`, `hostRestarted`, or **`ungated`** — omitted when unknown, so a client that ignores the field is unaffected. Every server-executed tool call, including `retire_covenant`, uses `ungated`: the host atomically records an allowed resolution without creating an active Ward, asking an operator, timing out, applying unattended auto-deny, or producing an approval prompt. All Ward frames are informational regardless of origin; Command Center writes an audit note and never opens a modal or posts a resolution. The non-`ungated` origins remain historical/compatibility vocabulary for the retained Ward engine, and a `POST` for an already-resolved record returns **409** `Ward.AlreadyResolved`. Sanctum and every other containment check still run (DESIGN §11.14).
- **Reasoning frame:** `type:"reasoning"` carries a typed, client-safe payload separate from answer `data`: `{"type":"reasoning","message":"client-safe summary","reasoning":{"text":"client-safe summary","output":"summary"}}` (the shared event envelope may also contain its normal null/default members). `reasoning.output` is exactly `none`, `summary`, or `full`; projected frames use `summary` or `full`. Provider `ProtectedData` is deliberately absent.
- **Error frame:** `type:"error"` carries the public message in `message` and the failure's typed `ErrorCodes` value in `data`, on every path: a budget refusal (`Budget.Exceeded`), a guardrail block (`Guardrails.*`), a Session held by another turn (`Hub.SessionTurnBusy`), an attachment rejection (`Validation.AttachedFiles`), a model that could not be resolved (`Hub.Model`), and the Spell-routing, accounting, Covenant, and structured-output failures each carry the same code the buffered route returns in its envelope. `Hub.Error` is the code of a failure with no narrower type: an untyped provider or transport failure (a provider client that could not be built, a stream that failed before or after it committed, and the writer's own sanitized stream failure), an unexpected exception anywhere else in the turn pipeline, a reply the Grimoire could not finalize (the buffered route returns the same code for it), and a turn that ended cancelled or abandoned without a typed error, a stream that ended without a terminal frame included. The HTTP status is already 200 once the stream starts, so `data` is the machine-readable signal a streaming client backs off or retries on, and the `/v1` stream error `type` derives from the same code.
- **Committed reply:** once the exact native assistant finalization receipt exists, a subsequent claim-settlement or Session-event notification failure does not turn it into a save failure. An exact accepted-key retry recovers and replays the durable outcome without another provider call; a claim without authoritative finalization evidence remains refused. A committed protected tool call/result pair likewise survives notification failure. Cancellation continues to propagate according to the request's existing policy.
- **Dropped partial reply:** if a streaming turn staged Covenant mutations and interruption or publication failure discards a nonempty reply, its terminal `error.message` explicitly says the partial reply was dropped. The original `error.data` code remains unchanged. This notice contains no reply or proposal text and is emitted only after successful discard; empty output and failed cleanup do not claim a dropped partial reply. A disconnected caller cannot receive a terminal frame.
- **Protected maintenance status frame:** with `Arcanum:Features:LongRest` on, a streaming Session turn whose scope has protected Long Rest debt emits one `status` frame (`Finishing protected memory maintenance...`) before the first paid protected work and waits up to 5 seconds for the writer to flush it. The in-process acknowledgement crosses both event queues and is consumed only after a successful response flush; queue insertion, failed writes, cancellation, and disconnected replay draining do not acknowledge delivery. The existing timeout may release maintenance without claiming delivery; a turn with no protected partition to finish emits none, and a buffered turn is never told (§8.36).
- **Disconnect / cancellation (`InferenceExecuteWriter`):** the code-owned policy is **`Auto`**. With an `Idempotency-Key`, continue-then-replay — do **not** link `RequestAborted` to the inference token; drain the hub enumerator and keep exact-byte capture so the claim may Complete. Without a key, caller cancellation abandons the claim. Arcanum adds no inference deadline; caller/host cancellation propagates, while unexpected provider/transport cancellation is sanitized as a generic inference failure. Either way, ledger provider-billed partial usage and reconcile/release the reservation.
- **Clients (`ArcanumApiClient` and The Forge):** transport-fragmented UTF-8 is reassembled into complete lines, including multibyte characters split across transport reads. `ArcanumApiClient` decodes each of its line-framed streams (`ask`, `web research`, the Apprentice Chronicle and the `watch` SSE streams) through its own `StreamingTextReader`, and The Forge reads its NDJSON and SSE streams (and its terminal command output) through a reader of the same name; each hands characters over after every single stream read and never goes back to the stream for more to fill a buffer. A `StreamReader` does that whenever one read filled its byte buffer exactly, which held back an event of exactly that many bytes (an `ask_human` prompt, a tool-start event ahead of a long tool run) until the server sent more, and a server waiting for the client sends nothing more. Before strict source-generated deserialization, an AOT-safe `Utf8JsonReader` scan validates the root `type`. Canonical values are matched case-insensitively and normalized before `ArcanumJsonContext` / `TheForgeJsonContext` deserialization; a truly unknown, nonblank future string is silently skipped so later frames continue. Invalid JSON, a missing/non-string/blank discriminator, or any whitespace-padded discriminator is **malformed** and retains the surface's diagnostic behavior. This narrow pre-scan does not install a permissive enum converter or reflection serializer: direct source-generated deserialization remains strict. The terminal **`result`** event carries native **`usage`** (`prompt_tokens`, `completion_tokens`, `total_tokens`, optional `cached_tokens`, optional `reasoning_tokens`) on the `IntelligenceEvent` payload; **`data`** still duplicates **`total_tokens`** as a decimal string for backward compatibility, while the final answer remains in accumulated **`token`** frames and the result `message`. Assistant text is never reconstructed from legacy result `data`.

### 8.6 Request Delegate Generator

`<EnableRequestDelegateGenerator>true</EnableRequestDelegateGenerator>` on `Api` ensures Minimal API endpoints in a referenced class library are source-generated.

### 8.7 Session-Based Consolidation (Campaign Logger)

With `features.campaignRollups` enabled, this path additionally publishes per-Session native contributions and a bounded Campaign Summary over their immutable revisions. Completed short Sessions contribute independently of compression thresholds. A Campaign-bound live turn performs eligible maintenance before assistant begin, then freezes one exact Campaign artifact for all provider attempts and tool rounds. `context inspect` reads the same artifact without invoking maintenance or a provider. The Campaign block is `### Campaign Summary (cross-session context)`; the compression block becomes `### Session Summary (compressed context)`. Their token sources are `campaignRollup` and `sessionRollup`. Disabling the feature preserves the existing `### Campaign Summary (compressed context)` bytes and performs no Campaign rollup lookup. With `Arcanum:Features:LongRest` enabled and `campaignRollups` off, the same live turn publishes no rollup and injects no Campaign Summary; before assistant begin it schedules the clean Long Rest sweep (best effort) and finishes any protected partition under the turn claim (§8.36).

When protected Long Rest work returns deferred or incomplete progress, its existing **503** `LongRest.Unavailable` message names the actual operation GUID and a reason only from the closed Long Rest fault policy (`Unknown`/`Incomplete` otherwise). Inspect it with `arcanum operation show <id>`. A transient protected condition requires a fresh eligible Session request after it clears; the clean worker cooldown is not a protected-work timer. Restart-generation reasons require fresh current source/authority validation. `long_rest.authorized_request_required` explicitly says operation retry alone cannot authorize protected work. Needs-operator/unknown outcomes require inspection before deciding on another attempt, without an unconditional paid-work retry.

Protected maintenance requires the authenticated requesting turn claim, canonical Campaign, exact source vector, and a live executor lease. It uses zero tools, pinned structured output, and a dedicated client with no hidden retries. Each physical dispatch records a disclosure before sending. Publication atomically binds the output, sensitivity label, source receipts, and checkpoint; invalid or oversized output leaves progress unchanged. A bound artifact is revalidated before each subsequent dispatch, so erasure, remapping, feature disablement, restore, or authority changes refuse further use. The Long Rest's protected maintenance (§8.36) runs at the same point under the same pending claim and borrowed turn lease.

Three mechanisms trigger Campaign Log consolidation:

1. **Message-count threshold** (`CampaignLogThreshold`) — safety valve for unbounded growth.
2. **Idle timeout** (`CampaignLogIdleTimeoutMinutes`) — natural session boundary.
3. **Explicit rest** — `POST /api/sessions/{id}/rest`.

The queue consumer resolves **`IArcanumIntelligenceProvider`** in a per-item DI scope alongside **`IGrimoireRepository`**, loads the session header via **`GetSessionHeaderAsync`**, and batches rows with **`CreatedAt > (LastSummarizedMessageAt ?? DateTime.MinValue)`**. It builds a stateless **`PingRequest`**: empty `Prompt`, `StatelessMessages` (system persona + user payload with prior summary and batched turns), **`SkipSpellRouting: true`**, **`DisableMcpTools: true`**, **`UnattendedMode: true`**, **`Model`** from **`Arcanum:FastModel`** when set else **`Arcanum:DefaultModel`**, else omitted for first-provider fallback, and **no** `SessionId` so the hub does not append a new **`Entry`**. On **`ExecutePromptAsync`** success, **`UpdateSessionCampaignRollupAsync`** atomically persists the LLM text into **`Session.Summary`** and sets **`LastSummarizedMessageAt`** to the latest batched entry time. On **`Result.IsFailure`** or exception, **no** DB update — the session remains eligible on the next sweep. The intelligence hub **reads** `Summary` for optional read-time compression (DESIGN §10.2.3).

For attachment privacy, the successful source turn records only typed consultation metadata. The Campaign Logger adds logical key, version, opaque attachment id, and source type for consultations inside the summarized window; it never loads attachment bytes, automatically submits the session's attachment index, or copies hashes/host paths into the summarizer payload. The prompt asks for useful decisions and source references, not an attachment archive.

Under the same **Session-Based Consolidation model of AI memory**, **Chronosync reporting** (DESIGN §5.4.2) addresses **spatial** drift: thread lines and `DomainType` deltas vs the last persisted `PatternSnapshot`, not chat log length. Campaign Logger and Chronosync are separate triggers; the hub folds `ChronosyncReport` into the system prompt via `PingRequest.ChronosyncDelta`; MCP context remains separate.

### 8.8 OpenAI `/v1` Chat Completions compatibility subset

`OpenAiV1Endpoints` advertises a **Chat Completions compatibility subset**, not full OpenAI API parity. Moderations/images/audio remain **`501 not_supported`**. Polymorphic `content` (string | parts) is AOT-safe; unsupported part types / over `MaxContentPartsPerMessage` → **400** `invalid_value` before mapping. Vision parts map to MEAI `TextContent`/`UriContent`/`DataContent` (DESIGN §10.2.4).

**Request body framing** (`POST /v1/chat/completions` reads the body itself rather than model-binding it, so it applies the §8.1 media-type rule in the OpenAI envelope): a missing or non-JSON `Content-Type`, or a JSON one whose `charset` .NET cannot decode, is **415** `invalid_request_error` / `unsupported_media_type` with the message `Request body must be sent with 'Content-Type: application/json' and, if it names a charset, one this server can decode (UTF-8 always is).`, not an unhandled `InvalidOperationException` surfacing as **500** `api_error` / `inference_failed`.
