A body over the endpoint's 16 MiB `WithLargeRequestBody` ceiling keeps Kestrel's **413** as `invalid_request_error` / `payload_too_large`; any other Kestrel `BadHttpRequestException` keeps its own status code as `invalid_request_error` / `invalid_request`. A JSON-typed but unparsable body remains **400** `invalid_json`, and a body that deserializes to null remains **400** `missing_body`. A null element in `messages` returns **400** `invalid_request_error` / `invalid_value` with `param:"messages[index]"` before buffered or streaming inference. Batch chat lines use the same validator and record the same `invalid_value` code and indexed message in the per-line `error` object, with `response:null`, without failing the whole batch or invoking a provider.

**Parameters applied** (`ApplyInferenceParameters`): temperature, top_p, max tokens, penalties, seed, stop, response_format. Explicit sampling values are validated before inference: temperature must be finite in `[0,2]`, top_p in `[0,1]`, and presence/frequency penalties in `[-2,2]`; token limits must be positive integers. Invalid values return HTTP 400 with the offending parameter rather than being clamped. Both `max_tokens` and `max_completion_tokens` must be valid when supplied; `max_completion_tokens` takes precedence. An explicit `response_format.type` must be `text`, `json_object`, or `json_schema` (trimmed and case-insensitive). `json_schema` requires an object schema directly or in its `schema` wrapper; optional wrapper `name` must be a nonempty string and `strict` a Boolean. The supported schema subset is parsed before inference. Case variants receive the same host validation, correction and strict stream buffering as lowercase. Native `PingRequest` uses the corresponding `temperature`, `topP`, `presencePenalty`, `frequencyPenalty`, `maxOutputTokens`, `responseFormat`, and `responseFormatJsonSchema` fields and returns field-specific `Validation.InvalidTemperature`, `InvalidTopP`, `InvalidPresencePenalty`, `InvalidFrequencyPenalty`, `InvalidMaxOutputTokens`, `InvalidResponseFormat`, or `InvalidResponseSchema` codes. OpenAI live and batch validation share the same rules and report `invalid_request_error` / `invalid_value` with the original wire parameter in `param`. Reasoning controls are additive: `reasoning_effort` = `none|minimal|low|medium|high|xhigh`, `reasoning_budget` = positive integer, and `reasoning_output` = `none|summary|full`. `reasoning_effort` and `reasoning_budget` are mutually exclusive and map to native `PingRequest.reasoning`; capability validation runs before provider I/O for buffered and `stream:true` requests. `reasoning_output` is an Arcanum-local projection/exposure preference and is passed to Microsoft.Extensions.AI only as a best-effort hint. It is not a guaranteed provider wire control, and Arcanum does not patch an unsupported `reasoning_output` field into provider JSON. When omitted, the resolved capability chooses `full` when `SupportsFull`, otherwise `summary` when `SupportsSummary`; `AllowsClientOutput` is required, and streaming also requires `SupportsStreaming`. Native effort/output and configured control-support/wire-dialect enums are strict string-only AOT contracts. OpenAI `reasoning_effort` and `reasoning_output` are also string-only. A numeric enum (defined or undefined) or an unknown enum string fails JSON binding before semantic validation. `n` must be `1` when present. When `Arcanum:Features:ClientTools` is disabled, requests with no tool definitions may still use the no-forwarding choices `tool_choice: "auto"` or `"none"`. Any supplied `tools` are rejected **400** `unsupported_parameter`; `"required"` and named choices are also rejected, either as **400** `invalid_value` when no tool was supplied to satisfy them or by the preceding `tools` rejection. Enabling the feature permits client-tool forwarding with schema/count validation (§8.8.3). `"required"` and named choices require at least one supplied tool (**400** `invalid_value` otherwise). If the health-selected model explicitly declares `supportsTools: false`, optional `tool_choice: "auto"` safely runs without tools, while `"required"` or a named function is rejected **400** `unsupported_parameter` before provider I/O. A requirement removed by effective tool policy is rejected **400** `invalid_value`, and a pre-commit runtime no-tools rejection may restart without tools only for an optional choice.

Every `image_url.url` must be an absolute HTTP(S) URL or a `data:<mime>;base64,<payload>` URI. Blank URLs, relative paths, other schemes and malformed data URI envelopes return **400** `Scrying.InvalidImageData` (OpenAI `invalid_image_data`) before inference, including streaming requests. The error names `messages[i].content[j].image_url.url` without echoing the URL or payload. OpenAI live and batch requests validate this shape before content mapping, including omitted or null `image_url`/`url` objects, so the indices identify the original wire fields even when preceding content parts would be discarded. Data URI MIME, decoded size and base64 validation still apply; Arcanum does not fetch remote image URLs at this gate.

**Log probabilities:** `logprobs: true` or any supplied `top_logprobs` returns **400** `invalid_request_error` / `unsupported_parameter` naming the offending field before inference. Omitted or explicit `logprobs: false` remains valid. Buffered, streaming and batch chat requests share this validation; Arcanum does not return token log probabilities.

**Responses:** buffered answers remain in `choices[].message.content`; additive reasoning is in `reasoning_summary` and/or `reasoning_content`. Streaming answers remain in `choices[].delta.content`; reasoning uses the same additive fields on the delta, in provider order. A client that ignores the fields still reads an unchanged answer. Usage keeps `completion_tokens` and `total_tokens` authoritative and projects the reasoning subset at `completion_tokens_details.reasoning_tokens`; cached prompt subsets use `prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens`. Buffered `message.tool_calls` still reports server-executed calls (§8.8.1); streaming SSE includes keep-alives and usage only when requested by `stream_options`. Semantic reasoning failures are typed OpenAI error bodies/chunks, never `delta.content`: they use HTTP **400**, `type:"invalid_request_error"`, `param:"reasoning"`, and the reachable stable code `invalid_reasoning_options` (effort plus budget), `invalid_reasoning_budget` (budget outside 1–2,097,152), `unsupported_reasoning_control`, `reasoning_budget_exceeds_model_limit`, or `unsupported_reasoning_output`. Numeric/unknown reasoning enum JSON never reaches those semantic branches; strict binding returns HTTP **400** `invalid_request_error`, code `invalid_json`, and no parameter. Unknown model → **404** `model_not_found`; tool-loop/timeout → **503** `server_error`.

**Current streaming projection topology:** production `/v1/chat/completions` obtains native `IntelligenceEvent` frames from `WizardIntelligenceProvider` (`TurnExecutionCoordinator` → `IntelligenceEventProjection`) and maps them to SSE chunks in `OpenAiV1Endpoints`. That endpoint mapper is the only OpenAI compatibility implementation (there is no second OpenAI projection), and the typed-error rules it applies live in `OpenAiStreamErrorMapper`. Terminal usage chunks, `stream_options.include_usage`, and tool-argument fragmentation are covered directly by production endpoint tests.

#### 8.8.1 Server-executed tools on `/v1` (buffered + streaming tool_calls)

Arcanum executes MCP tools server-side; `/v1` surfaces calls for observability/replay. Buffered: `PromptTurnResult.ToolCalls` → `message.tool_calls`. Streaming: `ToolCall` events → `delta.tool_calls` (40-char argument fragments; monotonic per-response `index`; fresh `call_…` ids). **`toolResult` never surfaced** on `/v1`. Forwarding mode preserves provider-minted ids and returns `finish_reason: "tool_calls"` without executing client tools. Richer native surface: `/api/intelligence/ping(-stream)`.

#### 8.8.2 `GET /v1/models` capability enrichment

`ModelInfoBuilder` is shared with `GET /api/models`, and both routes read the same latest successfully persisted configuration snapshot. Additive OpenAI fields: `context_window`, `supports_vision`, `provider_name`/`provider_type`, effective `supports_tools` (false only for an explicit `providers.models.supportsTools: false`), `supports_streaming` (always true), optional `reasoning_wire_dialect` and `reasoning_max_budget_tokens`, and optional `prompt_caching`. The native equivalents are the flat `wireDialect`, `maxBudgetTokens`, and `promptCaching` fields. Neither route emits a nested `reasoning` object; these metadata fields do not expose every configured reasoning capability.

#### 8.8.3 Client tool security (forwarding mode)

When `Arcanum:Features:ClientTools` is enabled, Sanctum, Ward recording, and server tool audit do **not** apply to client-supplied tools (provider executes). Default remains reject. The native `/api/intelligence/ping` and `/api/intelligence/ping-stream` routes enforce the same feature switch when `forwardClientTools` is requested; the public `PingRequest` flag is not a bypass. Native `clientTools` or `clientToolChoice` supplied without `forwardClientTools=true` returns **400** `ClientTools.InvalidSchema`; omitted fields remain valid. Malformed native definitions identify `clientTools[index]`, while `/v1` continues to identify `tools[index]`. `tool_choice` is parsed once with kind-safe JSON checks and is validated again after every Arcanum policy filter: a required or named choice is never converted to optional/no-tools behavior. Buffered JSON and streaming SSE use the same sanitized `tool_choice` error mapping.

### 8.9 NDJSON anti-buffering headers (`/api/intelligence/ping-stream`)

The NDJSON streaming endpoint sets `Cache-Control: no-cache` and `X-Accel-Buffering: no` (parity with the SSE endpoint in §8.5/§8.8) so reverse proxies (nginx, Cloudflare, k8s ingress) do not coalesce incremental frames.

**Idle keep-alive.** While the provider has said nothing for a whole `Arcanum:EventBus:HeartbeatSeconds` interval (default 30; `0` disables it, the same setting the SSE routes use) the stream writes a **blank line** (`\n`), so an idle connection is not dropped by a proxy or client that treats silence as a dead peer. NDJSON is one JSON document per non-blank line, so every conforming reader skips it, as `arcanum` and The Forge do. It is written between frames only, never inside one, and it counts as a response byte, so a stream whose provider fails before its first event still answers `200` with the terminal error frame, exactly as before.

### 8.10 Buffered `/api/intelligence/ping` envelope

The buffered ping endpoint wraps a **`PromptResponseDto`** (Core) inside `ApiResponse<T>`: `text` (assistant answer only), `usage` (native token counts, including additive top-level `reasoning_tokens`), `toolCalls` (the assistant-issued calls executed server-side, when any), `finishReason`, and `reasoning` (an ordered array of `{ text, output }` client-safe segments; empty by default). Reasoning is never concatenated into `text`. Previously the envelope held only the assistant text as a bare `string`; clients now get the full turn context without falling back to NDJSON.

**Request-side `toolPolicy`** (on `PingRequest`, and likewise on `SpellExecuteRequest` and `PromptExecuteRequest`) narrows what a turn advertises: `allTools`, `noTools`, `readOnlyTools`, or `noForbiddenArts` (semantics in DESIGN §10.2.1). Omitted is equivalent to `allTools`. Like the reasoning and tokenization enums (§8.8), it is a **strict string-only** AOT contract: a numeric enum — defined or undefined — and an unknown enum string both fail JSON binding before any semantic validation, surfacing as **400** `Validation.InvalidBody` on the native routes (written by the host's body-fault envelope for the routes that bind the body, §8.1, and by `ApiRequestJson` for the routes that read it themselves) and **400** `invalid_json` on `/v1`. The strictness is load-bearing rather than stylistic: an integer that bound to an undefined member would carry a restriction nothing in the system recognizes. Every consumer now resolves an unrecognized policy to `noTools` — the advertisement path advertises nothing, and the invocation context reports `noTools`, so an unknown value can never be the arm that widens what a turn may do. `disableMcpTools` and `toolPolicy` are both narrowing instructions and compose as their intersection: `noTools` disables MCP tools outright, and `readOnlyTools`/`noForbiddenArts` apply their advertisement filter **and** still honour the flag rather than superseding it.

### 8.10.1 Mana counter (`POST /api/intelligence/mana`)

Read-only model-aware estimate (`ManaCountRequest` → `ManaCountResult`); no inference/Grimoire writes. `model` resolves the configured provider/canonical model profile, while an unconfigured model uses the conservative fallback. The result retains legacy `manaCount` / `encoding` / per-message fields and adds classification, profile id, safety margin, and the complete `ContextTokenBreakdown`. `tools:true` materializes the current native + MCP declarations and includes their names, descriptions, and full JSON schemas in both the total and source breakdown. **400** when neither `messages` nor `prompt` is supplied.

### 8.10.2 Effective context preview (`POST /api/intelligence/context/inspect`)

`ContextPreviewRequest` accepts optional `prompt`, `model`, `workingDirectory`, `sessionId`, and `campaignId`, plus `showContent` and `noRetrieval`. Unified-run previews can additionally supply `overrideSpellName`, preview-only `attachedFiles` (`AttachedFileDto[]`) and `scryingFoci` (`ScryingFocusDto[]`), `disableAllTools`, `unattendedMode`, `additionalSystemPrompt`, `maxOutputTokens`, and the production sampling fields `temperature`, `topP`, `stop`, `seed`, `responseFormat`, `presencePenalty`, and `frequencyPenalty`. The assembled request passes through the shared native preflight validation pipeline used by live inference, including attached-file, request-bound, and Scrying validation. An empty prompt is valid so an existing Session can be inspected as it stands. The response reports the effective provider/model and context window, selected Spell and routing mode, resonant dependencies, included and excluded tools with reasons, every `ContextTokenSource` row with estimate classification, reserved output, the production compression decision, and auxiliary routing/embedding work. With retrieval enabled those auxiliary calls are real provider calls: each embedding opens and settles its own `embedding` run and is ledgered as an `embedding` billable operation, while the model-backed routing and Lexicon extraction calls are reported here but not ledgered. `noRetrieval` makes the preview spend-free; the main response is never inferred.

Prompt input uses the production ping bound. When `campaignId` is supplied without `workingDirectory`, the endpoint resolves the Campaign's server-host path exactly as buffered and streaming inference do; an unknown Campaign returns **404**.

**Canonical Campaign resolution (§10.12).** Every inference and preview route now resolves Campaign authority through the one canonical resolver before doing any other work, and the sources must agree rather than the first populated one winning:

| Condition | Status | Code |
|---|---|---|
| `sessionId` names a Session that does not exist | **404** | `Session.NotFound` |
| `sessionId` names a Session that predates immutable Campaign binding | **409** | `Session.CampaignBindingRequired` |
| `campaignId`, the Session's binding, or the supplied `workingDirectory` name different Campaigns | **409** | `Covenant.CampaignBindingConflict` |
| `workingDirectory` is supplied but is not contained by the resolved Campaign's registered root | **409** | `Covenant.CampaignBindingConflict` |
| a Global-only Session is given any Campaign source | **409** | `Covenant.CampaignBindingConflict` |
| the resolved Campaign no longer exists | **404** | `Campaign.NotFound` |

A supplied Session ID is never silently replaced with a new Session, and an unregistered working directory contributes nothing only while no other source has established a Campaign.

**Registered root (Long Rest-scoped, §10.12).** The resolved context carries the path-identity policy version and, only when `workingDirectory` resolved to the Campaign's root, that root's revision and identity digest. While `Arcanum:Features:LongRest` is on, the turn boundary of a request that is a Long Rest turn (a default-context request on ping, ping-stream, prompt execution or spell execution, buffered or streaming, with or without a `sessionId`, with no stateless transcript and without the no-context marker; a Global-only context qualifies as a Long Rest turn but loads no root), the Long Rest sweep route (§8.36), and the revalidation of that turn's snapshot additionally load the Campaign's registered root so the Long Rest fence can compare it with the live registry: an observed `workingDirectory` must still equal that root, and a root that moved, vanished or cannot be trusted since it was observed is **409** `Covenant.StaleSnapshot`. Context inspection (`POST /api/intelligence/context/inspect`), a request with a stateless transcript and a no-context request are not Long Rest requests: they get exactly the table's answer, the registry is not read for them, and a moved or vanished root does not fail them. With Long Rest off the answer is exactly the table's for every request and the registry is not read. The Campaign a turn resolves to is server-owned: no request body, MCP schema, or tool argument can name it directly.

The endpoint uses `WizardIntelligenceProvider`'s production routing, RAG readers, tool builder and policy filters, `SystemPromptBuilder.BuildDocument`, `InferenceContextBuilder.TryApplyContextCompressionIfNeeded`, and `IModelTokenEstimator`. It never enters the turn coordinator, invokes a tool, reserves turn budget, creates an assistant Entry, persists preview files/images as attachments, or calls the main inference model. `overrideSpellName` uses the same forced-Spell load and dependency resolution as a live turn and continues to resolve when `noRetrieval:true`; only automatic semantic Spell routing is skipped. `disableAllTools` and `additionalSystemPrompt` let a research dry run reproduce synthesis policy without executing it. `noRetrieval:true` skips embedding/RAG and automatic semantic Spell routing, with explicit unavailable reasons. Model-visible content is omitted unless `showContent:true`; that opt-in returns the assembled system prompt and messages through the authenticated operator API. Pre-call token values are labeled `exact`, `estimated`, `unknown`, or `reserved`; provider-reported values are never fabricated.

When `features.tapestry` is enabled, preview and live inference use the same typed DAG retrieval and exact/raw-before-summary ledger admission. Only an operator Default invocation carrying a current clean read epoch and consistent canonical Session/Campaign/workspace answer is eligible, even when the Covenant feature is off. None/no-context/delegated children receive no ambient Tapestry access. Preview owns ephemeral admission state and revalidates observed Tapestry generations before returning content; live inference additionally checks every physical send/retry independently of prompt caching. A changed injected source or identity returns the existing `Hub.InvalidAdmission` refusal; it never forwards stale content as a successful send. If an earlier physical attempt occurred without a usage receipt, its reservation remains uncertain rather than being presented as zero spend. No request/response/capsule field is added.

`arcanum run --dry-run` always requests `noRetrieval:true`, making it a spend-free static, pre-inference plan rather than an exact copy of the eventual live `PingRequest`. It validates and shows the resolved user payload, forced Spell, server context, tool policy, output reserve, and sampling controls without search or provider inference. A later live Agent handoff may still add locally produced `PatternSnapshot` and `ChronosyncDelta` context before inference.

### 8.10.3 Web research workflow request (`POST /api/web/research`)

`WebResearchWorkflowRequest` carries a nonempty `question` of at most **3858 UTF-16 characters after trimming**, optional positive `sourceTarget`, `model`, `tokenBudget`, optional `costBudgetUsd`, `continueSessionId`, and `attachToSessionId` fields. It also accepts optional `workingDirectory`, `campaignId`, current-turn `attachedFiles`, current-turn `scryingFoci`, and synthesis controls `temperature`, `topP`, `stop`, `seed`, `responseFormat`, `presencePenalty`, `frequencyPenalty`, and `unattendedMode`. These additions let `arcanum run --research` carry the same effective Campaign, Workspace, Session, Model, and explicit turn context as the ordinary Agent route. The run's Campaign Sanctum is the Campaign the run is bound to: its `continueSessionId`, its `campaignId`, or the registered root that contains its `workingDirectory` (DESIGN §11.15), so a run that names no `campaignId` but stands inside a contained Campaign gates its citations like the ordinary route does. When those sources name different Campaigns (a `continueSessionId` bound to one Campaign and a `workingDirectory` inside another, which the ordinary route refuses as a binding conflict), the stricter Sanctum governs the run's citations, and a run whose Campaign's Sanctum cannot be read denies every citation as `source_denied`. `tokenBudget` remains the synthesis output-token authority.

Before any search-provider work, the server validates the request and prospective synthesis request, resolves a Campaign-only request to its server-host working directory, and validates the continuing and attachment target Sessions. It then performs progress-driven search/read passes and uses the preflighted `PingRequest` for final synthesis. The research-only untrusted-source system instruction and `DisableAllTools` remain server-owned. Client filesystem paths are never dereferenced by this endpoint: callers send typed content, and the host validates and materializes it through the normal inference path.

There is no configurable hop limit. The full supported pass number is reserved in the question bound; at pass 2147483647, discovery ends with `search_pass_limit_reached` and synthesis preserves the collected work instead of wrapping the counter. Passes continue while they discover new unique URLs and stop at an optional `sourceTarget`, deterministic source exhaustion/no-progress, caller cancellation, explicit token/cost policy, provider/context failure, or a safety denial. The fetch phase that follows is bounded even when the request names no `sourceTarget`: a code-owned ceiling of 50 citations applies, and fetching stops once the retained page text already covers the synthesis prompt's character budget, since everything past it would be held in memory and then dropped unread. An explicit `sourceTarget` is the operator's own authority and is honoured as written.

Field validation reports the first offending API field and its accepted rule without echoing the rejected value. `tokenBudget` defaults to 2000 when omitted and must be positive when supplied; `sourceTarget` must be positive and `costBudgetUsd` nonnegative. Explicit zero is not replaced by a default. On `/api/web/search`, `query` must be nonempty and at most 4000 UTF-16 characters after trimming, `resultCount` is 1–20 (default 5), and optional `freshness` is `day`, `week`, `month`, or `year`. Each `includeDomains` / `excludeDomains` list has at most 20 entries; each entry is a nonblank bare host of at most 253 characters without a scheme, slash/path, colon/port, or whitespace. Invalid entries name their zero-based position, such as `includeDomains[1]`; validation does not resolve DNS. Null domain lists remain equivalent to empty lists. Browse guidance names `url` and `renderMode`; JavaScript degradation advises `renderMode: "static"`.

Research uses **HTTP 200 with a terminal `Error` NDJSON frame** for workflow validation, policy/budget refusal and unexpected orchestration failure, including failures before the first progress frame. Authentication, malformed JSON and middleware admission errors before this handler retain their ordinary HTTP contracts. Clients must inspect the terminal frame; HTTP status alone does not establish research success. The source-generated enum wire values are `Limits`, `Progress`, `Result`, and `Error`. A disconnected/cancelled caller cannot be promised a terminal frame.

Every handler frame carries the same `traceId` (`Activity.Id` when present, otherwise the HTTP trace identifier). Terminal failures have an associated warning/error with that identity; unexpected exceptions are logged by type without their message or payload. `search_completed` frames publish `usage` before more work begins, and every terminal error includes the latest reported `usage` and `partialResultsRetained: false`: no partial final research answer or answer attachment is returned. `usage.costUsd` is the accumulated **reported search-provider subtotal**, null when no cost was reported, not a zero-cost claim or a total including synthesis or unreported/failed calls. Budget failure retains the spend evidence but does not synthesize an answer after the ceiling was exceeded. Optional frame fields are additive; older clients may ignore them and newer clients accept their absence from older servers.

On live synthesis, supplied `attachedFiles` and `scryingFoci` enter the normal attachment pipeline: when Attachments are enabled they are persisted and bound to the synthesis Session before model inference; when Attachments are disabled they remain in-memory current-turn content. A context preview never persists them. `attachToSessionId` is separate: it stores the final research Markdown, not the supplied synthesis context. Attachment is an optional side effect on an answer that is already billed by the time it runs, so a failure there is never terminal: the stream emits an `attachment_failed` progress frame carrying the attachment error and still emits the `result` frame, with `attachmentId` null. `/api/web/search` and `/api/web/browse` have no progress channel, so they carry the same fact as an `attachmentError` field on the result itself.

Research evidence is limited to nonempty page bodies actually included in the synthesis prompt. A failed read, empty body, unavailable URL reader, or a block whose URL/title consumes all remaining body space never becomes a citation. Successful source numbers are contiguous after these exclusions. `source_denied` identifies the Campaign Sanctum or outbound network policy; other unsuccessful reads emit `source_unreadable`. Both carry a bounded `code` and a safe hostname in `message`, without the URL path, credentials, query, fragment or provider error text. Invalid citation URLs, including failed IDN canonicalization or a canonical hostname longer than 253 characters, produce `source_unreadable` with `WebResearch.InvalidUrl` before policy/read work and carry no hostname or URL text. Valid diagnostic hostnames are bounded after canonicalization and omit IPv6 zone identifiers. Unknown reader error codes become `WebResearch.ProviderUnavailable`. A run with no emitted page evidence sends `evidence_limited` and instructs synthesis to treat search summaries as unverified leads without invented citations. Event, publication and retrieval dates in retained evidence keep their distinct meaning.

The terminal `result` contains:

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `answer` | Synthesis text, retained even when the provider reaches its output-token limit. |
| `citations` | Contiguous `index`, source `url` and optional `title` for page bodies actually emitted to synthesis. |
| `provider` | The search provider; synthesis may use a different configured inference provider. |
| `model` | Canonical model of the successful synthesis lease, including fallback resolution; `null` when the intelligence implementation supplies no such evidence. Never a guessed request alias or server default. |
| `sessionId` | The requested continuing Session, or `null`. |
| `truncated` | True when a search answer was truncated or synthesis ended with `finishReason: length`. Local prompt-size source selection is separately observable through source-budget/evidence progress. |
| `finishReason` | Synthesis provider finish reason, or `null` when unknown. |
| `warnings` | Synthesis warnings, including guidance to increase `tokenBudget` / CLI `--token-budget` when the synthesis reaches its output-token limit. Each also arrives as a `synthesis_warning` progress frame before the result. |
| `usage` | `promptTokens`, `completionTokens` and `totalTokens` sum reported search and synthesis tokens; `searchQueries` counts reported search queries. `costUsd` is **only reported search-provider cost**, or `null` if none was reported; it is not total research cost and does not include synthesis inference cost. |
| `attachmentId` | Stored answer attachment id, or `null` if unrequested or unsuccessful, with failure reported by `attachment_failed` as above. |

### 8.10.4 Explicit subagent memory delegation (`POST /api/intelligence/memory-delegation`)

The authenticated native route accepts source-generated `MemoryDelegationRequest(PingRequest Parent, MemoryDelegationGrant Grant)` and returns **200** `ApiResponse<PromptResponseDto>` on success (§8.10). The parent can be stateless or Session-backed. The grant scope must equal the server-resolved canonical parent scope; a body Campaign id does not establish authority. The original authenticated `ProtectedRead` authority, clean request epoch and genuine Turn lease remain live through protected response serialization. A parent with context policy `None`, an explicit `noTools` policy, `disableAllTools: true` or forwarded client tools cannot select this capability. Health-aware preflight requires a configured candidate that supports native tools before protected hydration; effective candidate and concrete provider-lease checks apply again before execution and each fallback. Preparation holds no provider lease and makes no inference probe.

The example illustrates the wire shape only. Its placeholder identities and zero digest do not identify a usable source or credential:

```json
{
  "parent": {
    "prompt": "Delegate the bounded task using the selected memory.",
    "model": "exact-configured-model",
    "sessionId": "11111111-1111-1111-1111-111111111111",
    "skipSpellRouting": true,
    "disableMcpTools": true,
    "disableAllTools": false,
    "maxOutputTokens": 256
  },
  "grant": {
    "scope": { "kind": "Global", "campaignId": null },
    "memory": {
      "covenant": [
        {
          "entryId": "22222222-2222-2222-2222-222222222222",
          "versionId": "33333333-3333-3333-3333-333333333333",
          "fragmentDigest": { "bytes": "AAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAA=" }
        }
      ],
      "saga": [],
      "lexicon": []
    },
    "purpose": "DelegatedTask"
  }
}
```

The Session must already exist with its immutable Global-only binding for this example. Parent surface selection follows the ordinary turn contract: an explicit `sessionId` is Session-backed; omission selects the ordinary stateless operator classification unless Default context, no stateless transcript and a nonempty canonical context select the Session-backed surface through enabled Long Rest or enabled rollups for a bound Campaign. A prompt without an explicit stateless transcript can still create a durable Session through the ordinary writer; protected tool persistence reserves its genuine creation claim without changing that invocation classification or enabling maintenance. A nonempty explicit `statelessMessages` transcript remains nonpersistent. A Campaign grant uses `"kind": "Campaign"` and the matching nonempty canonical `campaignId`. The body is strict UTF-8 JSON with the ordinary 16 MiB native-parent transport ceiling and unknown-member rejection throughout the request closure. `purpose` is a string-only enum with canonical token `DelegatedTask`, also the omitted-field default. Scope canonical tokens are `Global` and `Campaign`; numeric scope and purpose values are refused.

Exact coordinate shapes follow §8.21 without granting evaluation authority. Supply all three `memory` arrays, using empty arrays for unselected stores. Covenant `entryId` and `versionId` are nonempty GUIDs. Saga `memoryId` and Annals `versionId` are bounded nonempty strings; Lexicon `entryId` is a nonempty GUID and its Annals `versionId` is a bounded nonempty string. Saga and Lexicon add numeric `hashFormat` and `contentDigest` in the same digest-object shape: `1` is `LegacyStoreDigest`, and `2` is `LexiconStructuredSnapshot`; Saga permits only 1, while Lexicon must match its exact head's supported format. A digest contains base64 for exactly 32 bytes. For Covenant, obtain the current committed entry/version and matching eligible explain decision, then decode its hexadecimal `fragmentDigest` to bytes before base64 encoding. The source must be an exact current eligible head, not merely retained history. Normal scope, Campaign shadowing, masks, lifecycle, owner, validity, sensitivity and provenance checks still apply; historical dependencies are metadata proof only. Duplicate or invalid coordinates refuse the whole grant.

| Code-owned bound | Limit |
|---|---|
| Selected exact versions across all three stores | 1–16 |
| Selected plus dependency proof coordinates | 128 |
| Rendered selected strict UTF-8 memory, including framing | 32 KiB |
| Canonical proof metadata | 64 KiB |
| Grant lifetime | At most five minutes from preparation, clipped by selected-source validity and parent lifetime |
| Grants per root / live grants per process | One / 32, including consumed children until drained |
| Successful consumption | Once, for one host-bound child |

Overflow refuses the entire selection. The child must also fit the effective model context with explicit input/output reservations; selected fragments are never silently dropped to fit. No configuration knob widens these limits.

Only this root's bound native `delegate_task` gains optional model argument `memory_grant`, whose accepted selector is `"memory"`. It is not a wrapper body field or bearer credential. The host validates and freezes the executed task, model, explicit files and attachment identities, token/cost limits and purpose, binds their canonical digest to the child RunId, and atomically consumes the grant. Missing selection follows ordinary isolated delegation. Bad, stale or consumed selection refuses that child and can return a tool failure while allowing the ordinary parent to continue. Siblings and nested invocations receive no selectable grant. Native delegation does not require `Arcanum:Features:Apprentices`; `disableMcpTools: true` leaves native tools available, and `skipSpellRouting: true` avoids auxiliary spell selection without disabling delegation.

The selected child remains `ArcanumInvocationContext.None`, stateless and tool-free, without ambient retrieval or mutation/promotion authority. It receives only its ordinary explicit task inputs and the frozen selected fragments, preserving CONTEXT versus fenced DATA and source-token attribution. Its trusted maximum sensitivity and union provenance cover both selected sources and any frozen explicit parent-derived inputs. Later ordinary delegation can carry only explicit derived input with its non-reading output guard and label; it receives no additional selected fragments or source-read authority.

Each physical protected provider send is durably disclosed and source-revalidated immediately before admission, remains cancellable and counted until teardown, and records known provider usage durably exactly once even when revocation withholds the result. Relevant source mutations conservatively revoke all live capsules in v1, cancel/drain their admitted effects before opening the SQLite write transaction, and prevent later protected sends or publication. This includes all three stores, deletion, retirement, suppression, reset/restore and authority/generation changes. Consumed-root protection persists through parent continuation and the whole HTTP serialization. An unused expired grant only prevents later selection; it does not cap ordinary untainted parent work. Earlier remote disclosure remains disclosed.

Labels and provenance survive empty, failed and interrupted output, tool conversion, atomic Session tool-call/result text plus label persistence, subsequent parent sends and final output. A consumed root with actual nonempty staged Covenant mutations refuses the combined finalization before the mutation writer or its effects run: neither those source changes nor the assistant reply commit. Ordinary and unused-root combined commits retain their existing behavior. Immediate parent memory tools may commit and revoke the root, after which protected pair/final publication refuses (DESIGN §10.21.9).

Responses carry `Cache-Control: no-store, private`, `Pragma: no-cache`, `Expires: 0` and no `ETag` or `Last-Modified`; original authority is revalidated before the first byte and the protected publication effect spans the entire serialization. Locally recognized transport/strict-shape and parent-preflight failures may return fixed caller guidance in the existing error message: JSON content type, UTF-8, declared/streamed 16 MiB body limit, declared parent/grant fields, or a known parent field/bound. Attached-file guidance includes only its zero-based field index and measured/limit counts; it never echoes the relative path or content. An overflow measured by the bounded reader reports a lower bound, not a full-body measurement. A transport-level size rejection names that condition without inventing a byte count. Prompt/stateless bound codes that were otherwise unmapped return **400** only when produced by that local typed preflight branch; the same code from a provider/source retains its normal status and constant protected message. No validation-code prefix or arbitrary error message establishes caller provenance. The captured original authenticated feature/issuer is revalidated before delivering even an early safe caller error; a stale/refused revalidation replaces that detail with the protected refusal. No diagnostic handle or metadata field is added to the wire. Refusals are content-free: **400** `MemoryDelegation.InvalidGrant` for invalid body/scope/purpose/coordinates/bounds; **409** `MemoryDelegation.StaleSource` for changed, consumed, expired or revoked proof/authority, process quota, duplicate root or closed mutation admission; **503** `MemoryDelegation.Unavailable` for unavailable native composition/candidate or excluded client-forwarding mode. Shared authentication, Covenant, canonical Campaign/Session, preflight, provider and context-budget errors retain their established mapping. No idempotency cache/replay, durable grant CRUD, renewal or recovery can recreate executable authority. No CLI, Forge or configuration surface is added.

#### 8.10.5 Native scoped memory recall

`features.agentMemoryRecall` (default `false`) makes `recall_memory` available to an eligible attended native operator turn through the existing inference API. Its hand-authored schema accepts `query` (nonblank, at most 512 characters), `scope` (`All`, `Saga`, `Lexicon`, `Covenant`; default `All`), `limit` (1–20; default 10), and optional `cursor` (at most 2,048 characters). Unknown fields are refused. Campaign, Session and authority are host-bound and cannot be supplied as tool arguments. MCP disabling leaves native tools available; all-tools disabling, no-tools/no-context policy, lack of Spell attunement, unattended/background and isolated child execution exclude this tool.

The tool returns one bounded JSON page with `items`, `nextCursor` and `candidateLimitReached`. Each item names `source`, `id`, `content`, optional `similarity`, `campaignId`, nullable exact `versionId`, and bounded `provenance`. Legacy data without an exact retained version reports no invented version. The page shares the existing tool-output byte allocation. Continuation is valid only for the exact tool instance, unchanged query/scope/page size/canonical scope and unchanged selected candidate snapshot. A restart, another turn, malformed authentication or a changed source refuses continuation. A saturated candidate window reports `candidateLimitReached: true`; it does not claim the entire store was exhausted. An oversized next item is refused without silent truncation or skipping.

Saga and Lexicon obey ordinary runtime retrieval and active lifecycle rules. Covenant uses only the current turn's linked eligible snapshot under clean generation-bound read authority. Protected tool results retain exact provenance and sensitivity through provider continuation, Session tool-pair persistence and the final HTTP body, with `Cache-Control: no-store, private`, `Pragma: no-cache`, `Expires: 0` and no response validators. Generic `/api/memory/search` and `/v1` gain no Covenant search authority. The tool is read-only and adds no mutation, promotion or subagent search permission. DESIGN §10.21.10 owns the implementation and lifecycle contract.

### 8.11 Daemon event SSE bus (`GET /api/events/daemon`)

In-process `IEventBus` uses code-owned bounded per-subscriber channels with `DropOldest`. Wire: `text/event-stream` `DaemonEvent` frames + best-effort `[DONE]`. A maintenance window ends the stream at a complete frame boundary with that same `[DONE]` (§8.32). `Arcanum:Execution:MaxSseConnections` and `MaxSseConnectionsPerType` feed `SseConnectionGate` → **503** `Api.TooManyConnections`. Anti-buffering headers; API key on the `/api` group. Rate limiting admits the HTTP request only, not open-stream duration.

`arcanum watch daemons` is the terminal consumer. It keeps heartbeats and `[DONE]` out of data output, supports free-form event/tool filtering, and offers opt-in reconnect. Reconnect cannot recover process-local daemon frames and therefore always warns that a gap may exist.

### 8.12 Configuration API (`GET` / `PUT` / `POST /api/config`)

Read: redacted secret-bearing URLs/endpoints (`***`) plus non-secret credential references; environment values are never read into the response. `GET /api/config`, `GET /api/models`, `GET /api/providers`, and `GET /v1/models` resolve one latest-successfully-persisted snapshot, so their configuration and discovery projections agree immediately after a successful write. Write: under the configuration-writer lock, re-read the current file, merge recognized redacted URL placeholders, run outbound and semantic validation, and atomically replace `arcanum.json`. Holding that lock across the entire read-validate-write transaction prevents a concurrent retention-rule update from committing between the full PUT's snapshot and replacement. Validate-only also merges recognized endpoint masks against the current snapshot before outbound and semantic validation, so an unchanged redacted `GET` document remains a valid update candidate; it never writes. Residual masks for new/unmatched providers fail closed. Provider API keys and PFX passwords are not accepted fields. Other non-retention runtime consumers remain bound to the process-start snapshot and require a host restart to adopt configuration changes; referenced secret environment values are resolved only at provider/certificate use. Status: **400** `Configuration.ValidationFailed`, `Config.UnresolvedMask`, or `Security.BlockedOutboundUrl`; **500** `Configuration.WriteFailed` or `Configuration.LockUnavailable` (another process held the configuration transaction past the bounded acquisition window). `POST /api/config/validate` answers **200** with `isSuccess: true` and `data: true` only when the merged document clears residual-mask, outbound, and semantic validation; every failure — semantic included — is a **400** failure envelope, so a status-code-driven probe cannot read an invalid configuration as validated.

#### 8.12.1 Familiar readiness (`GET /api/providers/{name}/familiar-probe`)

Answers whether a subscription-backed CLI provider is ready, without asking it for a completion. Runs host-side next to the process runner so Compendium — an editor that does not spawn processes — can ask over HTTP instead of growing a second implementation. Non-billable (ADR 0002 / `NonBillableSurfaces.FamiliarProbe`), alongside `GET /v1/models`.

`FamiliarProbeResult` carries `providerName`, `providerType`, `status` (`NotInstalled` | `NotConfigured` | `Configured`), `version` when the CLI reports one, a one-line `summary`, `remediation` text, a copyable `remediationCommand` the **operator** runs, `enumeration` (`Discovered` | `OperatorDeclared` | `Unknown`), `models`, and `hiddenModels`.

Redaction is structural rather than a filtering pass: the bound DTOs have no field for the account e-mail, organisation identifiers, or local paths that `claude auth status --json` and `codex doctor --json` print, so that material cannot reach this payload or a log. Arcanum never authenticates — `remediationCommand` is text the operator runs themselves. `enumeration: "Unknown"` is the ordinary outcome, because neither CLI publishes a machine-readable model list; clients fall back to free-text model entry and everything still works.

### 8.13 MCP server event SSE bus (`GET /api/events/mcp`)

`McpConnectionManager` publishes `McpServerEvent` on state changes. Same SSE back-pressure/caps/auth as §8.11, and the same maintenance quiescence (§8.32). `arcanum watch mcp` uses the common watcher, including repeatable `--tool` / `--tool-name` and `--event-type` filters. Reconnect does not imply replay of MCP lifecycle events.

### 8.14 Spell Management API (`/api/spells`)

Legacy list, named lookup, and multi-source search reuse metadata discovery for up to 300 seconds. External additions or metadata edits may therefore take up to five minutes to appear; first-party authoring and import invalidate affected and overlapping workspace catalogs immediately when the write finishes, including a partial publication followed by failure. Hydration and export still validate current file identity, timestamps, and containment. Campaign import/export use a fresh batch inventory; the paged catalog retains its existing cursor and freshness behavior.

Workspace resolution: `?workspace=` → `Arcanum:Workspaces:DefaultRoot` → CWD. CRUD needs a resolvable workspace; empty `Arcanum:Security:SpellWorkspaceRoots` denies all (**403** `Spell.PathNotAllowed`). Built-ins under `~/.config/arcanum/spells/` are read-only (`Spell.BuiltinReadOnly`). Format: `SPELL.md` frontmatter + body; optional `SPELL.json` (legacy `SKILL.json` read fallback; writes always `SPELL.json`). Search shadow order is campaign > workspace > builtin and returns the complete deterministic match set rather than silently dropping results after 1,000. Declared dependency lists and cycle-safe graph traversal have no count/depth ceiling; single-file allocation, cancellation, visited identity, `MaxDeclaredTools`, and provider-facing `MaxResonantBytes` remain. Versions: string labels `SPELL.v{label}.md` (`^[A-Za-z0-9.]+$`); activate swaps into `SPELL.md` and records `activeVersion`, and skips the prior-content backup when the requested label is already the active one, because the two resolve to the same file. Import writes through the same frontmatter and `SPELL.json` bounds gate as create, so an imported bundle's `description`/`tags`/`model`/`provider` cannot smuggle a newline and forge an extra frontmatter key. `payload`, and its `fullContent` and `scripts`, are `[JsonRequired]`: a body omitting any of them is refused at the binding layer rather than binding null and importing a spell with no content. Every write failure answers **500** `Spell.WriteFailed` with a fixed sanitized sentence (§8.23, alongside `Workspace.WriteFailed`); the exception detail stays in the server log rather than returning an absolute server path to the caller. Export (`POST /api/spells/{name}/export`) reads `SPELL.md`, the sidecar, and every `scripts/*` file through the same fail-closed handle-identity read the scanner uses (DESIGN §11.6): a script or sidecar that is a symlink, a hard-linked file, a FIFO or device, or that resolves outside the workspace is skipped and logged rather than exported, and a `SPELL.md` that is not a readable regular file inside its root answers **404** `Spell.NotFound`. Script bytes are returned base64-encoded exactly as read, never decoded and re-encoded as text. Scripts are taken in ordinal file-name order, so a bundle that hits the 64-script or aggregate-size cap keeps the same scripts on every export, and `omittedScripts` names the `scripts/*` files the bundle does not carry (a skipped, oversized or over-cap script; at most 64 names, in the same ordinal order, `[]` when the bundle is complete) while `omittedScriptCount` counts every one of them, so a caller can tell a partial bundle from a complete one, and a list truncated at 64 names (`omittedScriptCount` greater than its length) from a complete one. A skipped sidecar leaves `metadata` null, and an import ignores `omittedScripts` and `omittedScriptCount`. `fullContent` is the whole `SPELL.md`, frontmatter included: an import parses that frontmatter back into the new spell's `description`, `tags`, `systemPrompt`, `template`, `model`, `provider`, `tools` and `requiredMcpServers` (a bundle's `metadata`, when present, still wins for the fields it carries) and writes only the text after it as the body, so an export followed by an import neither nests the old frontmatter inside the prompt nor drops the fields only the frontmatter held. Version activation reads the requested version file the same way and refuses one that is not a regular file inside the workspace (a link, a hard-linked file, a FIFO or device), is over the spell file limit, or is not UTF-8 with **404** `Spell.NotFound` before it writes the backup; the message says which. Clone/cast/import quirks and status codes: §1. Import accepts no more than export emits, and refuses anything larger as **400** `Validation.InvalidBody` before it writes: a spell file or script over the per-file limit (the code-owned spell file limit, 256 KiB by default and never above the workspace read size), scripts that together pass the aggregate read-size envelope, more than 64 scripts (export stops at the same 64), or a script whose content is not base64. A bundle whose name matches a built-in spell is refused with **400** `Spell.NameCollision` exactly as a workspace duplicate is, because a workspace spell shadows a built-in one of the same name; create refuses the same name. A caller that cancels after the spell directory has been moved into place (clone or import) is not told the write failed: the spell exists, the summary is read back on a token of its own, and an error is logged only for a real failure. A read-back that cannot finish, or does not list the spell, does not fail the write either: the response carries the summary the written `SPELL.md` describes (name, description, tags, declared tools, source `workspace`) and a warning is logged. A caller that cancels before the move has stopped the write: the cancellation propagates, nothing is published, and the staging directory is removed; an update or a version write cancelled before its replace likewise changes nothing, and neither answers `Spell.WriteFailed` or logs an error. Per-workspace locks are taken before the spell snapshot is read, so concurrent updates, version writes, clones and imports each build on the previous result rather than rewriting one stale copy; once `SPELL.md` has been replaced by an update or a version activation, the `SPELL.json` write that completes it is not cancelled by the caller, and if that write fails the caller is told so (**500** `Spell.WriteFailed`) and `SPELL.md` is put back to the content it held (an activation also removes the backup it created), so a failed write does not leave a spell whose file and sidecar disagree. Delete only under `{workspace}/spells/{name}`. Updating or deleting a spell that does not exist in the resolved workspace returns **404** `Spell.NotFound` through `ArcanumErrorMapper` (§8.23), matching `GET /api/spells/{name}`; built-in and validation refusals stay **400**.

`GET /api/spells` preserves the legacy `ApiResponse<SpellSummary[]>` response unless the nullable query flag is explicitly `paged=true`. Paged mode returns `ApiResponse<SpellCatalogPage>` with 50 metadata items, `hasMore`, opaque `nextCursor`, and `continuationAction`; it accepts `workspace`, `q`, `tag`, `tool`, `source`, and `cursor`. The cursor binds all filters/workspace plus the exact source/name/path identity of the prior anchor. A mismatched query, malformed cursor, or missing or changed anchor returns a structured **400** that instructs the client to restart with `cursor` omitted. Replaying the same input cursor is deterministic; the returned cursor advances. One cursor-name frame retains a 65,536-byte strict-UTF-8 boundary; **400** `Spell.ContinuationFrameTooLarge` reports the measurement and asks the operator to rename the spell or narrow the filters, then restart. The resource selector follows every advancing page, while Command Center `/spell list [opaque-cursor]` fetches one page and prints the exact next command. Legacy array consumers remain unchanged.

### 8.15 Daemon job management (`/api/daemons`, `/api/executions`)

**Route families:** `/api/unseen-servant/*` = Unseen Servant interval control; `/api/daemons/*` + `/api/executions/*` = job registry + execution history. Watermarks: DESIGN §5.5.5. On-demand `POST .../run` waits for completion; scheduled path shares `DaemonRunner` single-flight per daemon. History process-local (`ExecutionHistoryLimit`); detail includes correlated ring-buffer logs.

### 8.16 Log ring buffer (`GET /api/logs`, `GET /api/events/logs`)

Serilog → `SerilogLogRingBufferSink` → a code-owned bounded in-memory ring that overwrites the oldest entry. Query filters + `beforeSequence` cursor. Live SSE accepts nullable `LogLevel` `level` (`trace`, `debug`, `information`, `warning`, `error`, or `critical`) plus free-form `category` and `search`. The CLI forwards the level token without adding another client-side allowlist; API enum binding remains authoritative, and an unknown nonblank value returns **400** `Validation.InvalidQuery` before the response becomes SSE. A successful stream begins with the SSE comment `: connected`, not a synthetic data object; a stream entered during a maintenance window writes no `: connected` and ends immediately on `[DONE]` (§8.32). The route uses the same caps/auth as §8.11 and is not persisted across restarts. Post-build sink registration avoids a Build()-time logging DI deadlock.

`arcanum watch logs` maps `--level`, `--category`, and `--search` to those server filters and may add repeatable client-side `--event-type` / `--tool` filters. Like the other process-local streams, an opt-in reconnect continues across retryable disconnects until cancellation or clean completion, uses capped exponential delays, warns on every possible gap, and never claims missed log entries were replayed. Permanent 4xx/authentication/cap denials remain terminal.

### 8.16.1 Unified CLI watch projection

The six CLI sources map one-to-one to existing server contracts:

| CLI source | Server contract |
|---|---|
| `watch session [session]` | `GET /api/sessions/{id}/stream`; optional `since=<entry-guid>` |
| `watch apprentice [apprentice]` | `GET /api/apprentices/{id}/chronicle` |
| `watch logs` | `GET /api/events/logs`; optional `level`, `category`, `search` |
| `watch mcp` | `GET /api/events/mcp` |
| `watch daemons` | `GET /api/events/daemon` |
| `watch health` | Repeated `GET /api/health`; `--interval` defaults to five seconds |

`watch <source>` is the only live-stream entry; the CLI ships no alias spellings for these. Every request uses the normal `/api` credential; the CLI neither widens authorization nor bypasses SSE connection caps. The shared SSE consumer joins multi-line `data:` fields under one runaway guard, which the server can reach (a single line, or one event's joined data, is held to 64 Mi characters. That guard is sized to the server's 64 MiB tool-output ceiling, not a payload policy, and a JSON-escaped record can exceed it. An event over the limit is discarded instead of being buffered without bound and is reported as a recoverable `Api.InvalidResponse` diagnostic that names the limit and says the host still holds the full record, to be read with the matching `show` or `export` command; the stream carries on with the next event. Holding one line at the limit costs several hundred MiB transiently, which is why the guard is no larger. The `ask`, `web research` and Apprentice Chronicle NDJSON streams apply the same per-line bound with the same diagnostic), accepts LF, CR and CRLF line endings however the bytes are fragmented in transit, surfaces heartbeat comments as stderr liveness diagnostics while excluding them from data, reports the no-activity diagnostic after one to two idle intervals (the idle timer is armed once per window, not once per line), stops cleanly on `[DONE]`, uses UTC timestamps and event-type coloring in terminal mode, and returns **130** on Ctrl+C. `--json` reserves stdout for compact newline-delimited source objects and routes all diagnostics, including reconnect/gap messages, to stderr.

Repeatable `--event-type` and repeatable `--tool` (alias `--tool-name`) accept arbitrary case-insensitive values; blank values are ignored, and log tool matching recognizes structured `properties.ToolName` metadata. Reconnect is opt-in, retries unexpected disconnects and transient HTTP 408/425/429/5xx failures until cancellation with a code-owned capped exponential delay, and always reports a possible gap. Permanent validation/authentication/not-found/cap errors exit. A Session cursor may reduce the gap by continuing after the last received valid Entry id, but the bounded Session window is not a replay guarantee; Chronicle, log, MCP, and daemon streams have no replay cursor. Health polling accepts any positive whole-second interval and renders a valid Unhealthy 503 envelope as an observation. These are per-process CLI options and add no configuration surface.

### 8.17 Workspace registry and file browser/writer (`/api/workspaces`)

Campaign-backed when Grimoire ready (`persisted: true`); else in-memory. Writes gated by `Arcanum:Workspaces:EnableFileWrite` (default off) → **403** `Workspace.FileWriteDisabled`. Path policy: reject `..`/absolute; symlink escape → `Workspace.SymbolicLinkEscape`; revalidate before I/O. Atomic temp+rename for PUT/PATCH. Size clamps: DESIGN §3.4. PATCH ordinal replace with ambiguous/not-found codes; its read of the existing target is bounded by the same maximum file read size as GET contents, so an oversized target returns `Workspace.FileTooLarge` rather than buffering the whole file. HEAD contents returns size/`Last-Modified` only. The read routes (`GET .../files`, `.../files/info`, `.../files/contents`) map failures through the §8.23 authority exactly as HEAD and the write routes do — `Workspace.FileNotFound` → **404**, `Workspace.AccessDenied` → **403**, `Workspace.FileTooLarge` → **413** — while the list route's paging/search codes (`Workspace.ContinuationInvalid`, `Workspace.ContinuationCheckpointMissing`, `Workspace.InvalidSearchPattern`) stay **400**.

Link handling on the write routes is explicit rather than a generic I/O failure: a recursive DELETE first walks the tree (never following links) and, if any entry resolves outside the workspace, returns `Workspace.SymbolicLinkEscape` naming that link and deletes nothing; PUT or PATCH to a destination that is a symbolic link or has more than one hard link returns `Workspace.SymbolicLinkEscape` and leaves it untouched. A PUT or directory create whose path runs through an existing file, or through a symbolic link that does not lead to a directory (for example an in-workspace link whose target does not exist, which passes containment but cannot be created through), returns **400** `Workspace.PathIsFile` and creates nothing, including nothing at the link's target. Every write route (PUT, PATCH, DELETE and directory create) refuses protected workspace metadata: a path with a `.git` segment at any depth (including the numbered `GIT~n` and `ARCANU~n` short names and NTFS stream suffixes on Windows) or whose first segment is `.arcanum` returns **403** `Workspace.PathNotAllowed` before anything is created, changed or removed (DESIGN §11.6); a recursive DELETE of a directory that holds such an entry anywhere beneath it returns the same code, names the entry, and removes nothing; the same refusal applies when an in-workspace symbolic link resolves into `.git` or `.arcanum`, and reads are unaffected. An operator can lift the refusal for every write route (and for the in-process `write_file`, `replace_text_block` and `apply_patch` tools) by setting `Arcanum:Workspaces:AllowProtectedPathWrites` to `true`; it does not replace the `Arcanum:Workspaces:EnableFileWrite` gate, so with that gate off every write route still returns **403** `Workspace.FileWriteDisabled`. PATCH is a read-modify-write: it replaces the target only if the file is still byte-for-byte what it read (length and SHA-256, including any BOM), so an edit by someone else that lands between the read and the replace, including an in-place edit that keeps the inode and length, or a target deleted in that window, returns **409** `Workspace.FileChanged` (a conflict the caller resolves by re-reading, not the **500** `Workspace.WriteFailed` of an infrastructure fault) with the other edit left intact and nothing written; re-read and retry. The in-process `replace_text_block` tool applies the same rule. Both write routes are UTF-8 text routes, and neither rewrites bytes it cannot represent. PATCH decodes the target strictly: a target that is not valid UTF-8, or that contains a NUL byte and is therefore binary, returns **403** `Workspace.PathNotAllowed` and the file is left byte-for-byte untouched. PUT carries an existing destination's UTF-8 BOM across the overwrite, matching PATCH, so a read-modify-write loop through `GET .../files/contents` does not strip the preamble; a `content` value that already begins with U+FEFF supplies its own BOM and is never doubled.

Registration creates the workspace `.arcanum` directory **before** the Campaign row is persisted, mirroring `POST /api/campaigns`. A directory that cannot be created returns `Workspace.AccessDenied` / `Workspace.WriteFailed` and leaves nothing registered, so a retry is never blocked by a half-completed first attempt. Recursive file listing skips a subdirectory that vanished or cannot be read and returns the rest of the tree instead of failing the whole page. The listing walk runs off the request thread and proves containment once per directory chain rather than re-walking every ancestor component for each child; a symbolic link is still validated individually, since it is the one entry that can break a chain its parent already proved.

The CLI exposes this boundary directly as `arcanum workspace list|current|register|show|tree|info|read|search|index|index-status|chunks|unregister`. `tree`, `info`, and `read` call the authenticated file-browser routes and never read the client filesystem directly. File writes remain absent from this command family, so `Arcanum:Workspaces:EnableFileWrite` is neither bypassed nor implicitly enabled. `register [path]` sends the path to the server registry; omission uses the client current directory only because the shipping CLI targets the bundled loopback host. Help and output call every such value a server-host path so this convenience cannot silently become a remote path assumption.

### 8.18 Session API

Search, export, analytics, CRUD, manual entry append, SSE live stream, and Campaign Log **`/rest`** use the Grimoire-backed **`/api/sessions`** surface. See **DESIGN §11.16 Session lifecycle** for persistence and lifecycle architecture.

**Search expressions:** `GET /api/sessions?search=...` and `GET /api/campaigns/{id}/sessions?search=...` accept native SQLite FTS5 expressions over transcript `Content`: implicit AND (`schema migration`), Boolean operators (`schema OR migration`, `schema NOT migration`), grouped expressions, quoted phrases (`"schema migration"`), phrase concatenation (`schema + migration`), prefixes (`migrat*`), proximity (`NEAR(schema migration, 2)`), initial-token anchors (`^schema`) and column filters (`Content:schema`). Put prefix wildcards after the term (`foo*`); raw leading-star expressions such as `*` or `*foo` are invalid. Quote a literal star (`"*"` or `"*foo"`). Quote literal punctuation (`"C++"`) and double quotes inside a phrase (`"quoted ""beacon"""`). `Id`, `SessionId` and `Role` are unindexed, so filters on those columns yield no transcript matches. Session titles also match a literal substring of the complete search expression; FTS operators are not applied to titles. Use the standalone session endpoint's `title` filter for title-only substring searches.

An omitted `search` leaves the list unfiltered by text. A supplied empty/whitespace expression, malformed native syntax, or an expression beyond the code-owned archive-query length bound returns **400** `Validation.InvalidQuery` in `ApiResponse<SessionQueryResult>`. Expressions are never truncated. Exceeding the native parser nesting limit also returns `Validation.InvalidQuery`, with guidance to reduce parentheses nesting. Valid expressions without hits, including tokenless quoted expressions such as `""`, return success; the title branch can still contribute matches. Query text and native parser diagnostics are not included in validation responses. Storage/admission failures and cancellation are not reclassified as invalid queries. The MCP `search_archives` tool uses the same native grammar and reports validation as tool errors.

#### 8.18.1 Standalone session attachments

All routes are API-key protected and operate only on a bound attachment belonging to the route Session. Successes use the native `ApiResponse<T>` envelope except the content stream.

- **Snapshot:** `POST /api/sessions/{id}/attachments` requires multipart form field `file`; an optional `logicalName` chooses the version family. It persists the supplied bytes as an immutable encrypted snapshot with `SourceKind=SnapshotOnly` / `SourceStatus=NotApplicable`. The request may originate from any client-readable path or stdin because no client path is sent or retained. Content outside the reviewed Comprehension formats (for example legacy binary Office and unknown binaries) is stored as `Binary` and remains `NotEligible` for text extraction; so is a PDF while Comprehension is not effectively enabled, and so is a PDF with no text layer (a scan) when it is. A snapshot is not rejected merely because Arcanum cannot materialize it into model context. The reviewed document formats also retain `Binary` kind and follow the gated extraction behavior below. A reviewed package format is chosen by the filename extension, with no ZIP or package sniffing (the detector's image and PDF signature checks still run first), so a package whose extension is not recognized is stored as an ordinary `Binary` snapshot. The detected type is what an operator's `Arcanum:Security:AllowedUploadMimeTypes` allowlist is matched against, whether or not Comprehension is on, so a non-empty allowlist must name the registry types of the documents it accepts (Compendium README). A non-image attachment is limited to 1 MiB (1,048,576 bytes), so a larger document is refused with `Attachment.TooLarge` and never stored. The parser admits a maximum-size file plus 64 KiB of multipart envelope, but rejects aggregate overflow with `Attachment.TooLarge` for both declared-length and chunked requests.
- **Live reference:** `POST /api/sessions/{id}/attachments/reference` accepts `{ "workspacePath": "docs/notes.md", "workspaceId": "optional-id", "logicalName": "optional-key" }`. `workspacePath` is interpreted only on the server, relative to the selected registered workspace or the configured active/default workspace. Absolute values do not widen authority: canonical containment, link/file identity, stable bounded reads, and Campaign Sanctum must all pass. The governing Campaign is the stricter of the Session's Campaigns (its binding and the Campaign its row stores) and the Campaigns whose registered roots contain the path the request spells: the root that contains the text of the spelling with its links unresolved, the root that contains the directory it stands in with its links resolved, and the root nearest the path in every form it can be read in. A link that stands inside a root and leaves it is therefore that Campaign's read wherever it leads, and where claims tie the Campaign the spelling is inside governs; the guard judges the path the opened file resolves to and the governing Campaign's mode decides the outcome: Strict refuses the read and records the breach, AuditOnly records the breach and lets a policy refusal through, and neither mode lets through a Sanctum that cannot be read or a breach that cannot be recorded. A refusal is **400** `Attachment.SourceUnavailable`, and a path no Campaign claims is unrestricted. The breach is recorded under the surface `attachment_reference`. The response exposes only opaque/sanitized provenance; canonical paths and file identities stay encrypted server metadata.
- **List/versions:** `GET /api/sessions/{id}/attachments` returns all bound versions. Clients derive a latest-per-logical-key list or a version history without a second persistence authority. The server fulfills that all-version array contract by exhausting bounded SQL keyset pages; turn indexes and logical-name tool selectors use SQL-selected latest projections and retrieve version ordinals only for the requested index keys, rather than loading every full historical row first. `SessionAttachmentDto.SessionId` enables direct CLI output; `RelativePath` names the encrypted stored snapshot, never the live source. CLI Reveal uses it only when the corresponding local file is present and has an `ARCABLOB` envelope; otherwise the user is directed to authenticated export.
- **Refresh:** `POST /api/sessions/{id}/attachments/{attachmentId}/refresh` accepts no body or path. It calls the same `ToolExecutionPipeline.RefreshSessionAttachmentAsync` core used by `refresh_session_file`. Its Sanctum decision is the live reference's: the stored workspace-relative path under its workspace root is the spelled path, the governing Campaign and its mode are chosen the same way, and a refusal fails the refresh with `Attachment.SourceUnavailable`, with its breach recorded under the surface `attachment_refresh` (a refresh made by the `refresh_session_file` tool inside a turn is judged by the Sanctum that turn is bound to and recorded under that tool's name). An unchanged hash reuses the row; changed verified bytes create the next version under the measured per-Session byte protection without an incidental per-key version-count cap. Detected MIME determines the refreshed version's current Text/Image/Binary kind. Kind-specific policy is reapplied, but this operator endpoint does not require model vision capability because it injects no content. Only a typed physical-storage or version-counter limit returns **409** `Attachment.LimitExceeded`. A provenance/hash mismatch or another persistence refusal during refresh returns **400** `Attachment.SourceUnavailable` with fixed guidance to reattach the source and consult host logs if it continues; raw exception details never enter either response.
- **Content:** `GET /api/sessions/{id}/attachments/{attachmentId}/content` opens only the authenticated stored snapshot through `ISessionAttachmentStore.OpenReadAsync`. It returns a download, never inline content, with `Cache-Control: no-store`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, and no source path in headers. Attachment bytes are therefore available for atomic client export without ever being printed by metadata commands.
- **Pins:** the existing context-pin routes create/delete `kind=attachment` pins whose target is an attachment id and whose content version is its snapshot hash. Text pins and effectively enabled Comprehension documents may materialize implicitly within the shared pin/turn budgets. Image pins remain stored but materialize with `Unsupported`; a vision-capable turn must explicitly pass that bound attachment id.
- **Inference references:** native `attachmentReferences` remain ordered opaque IDs for Bound snapshots in the effective Session. Preparation validates metadata without loading every file. After provider/model resolution, the turn opens and admits each reference independently against the actual context window; overflow stops before opening later references or calling the provider. A reference to a Bound document whose normalized MIME is in the Comprehension registry is validated against the current settings, is accepted while both `Arcanum:Features:AttachmentRetrieval` and `Arcanum:Features:Comprehension` are on, and is extracted when the turn is built; a reference to any other non-text, non-image attachment, or to a document while either gate is off, is refused with `Attachment '<id>' cannot be injected into model context.` Successful content retains request order and typed attachment provenance. Caller cancellation propagates immediately and does not materialize later references. Missing, unbound, or wrong-Session references return **400** `Validation.AttachedFiles` with a code-owned explanation. A typed storage-limit refusal during native turn preparation retains `Validation.AttachedFiles`; the standalone refresh limit contract above remains `Attachment.LimitExceeded`. Unexpected preparation/store failures return sanitized **500** `Hub.Error` (the same code in an error frame after a native stream starts), never a caller-validation error containing exception text. Document extraction failures retain the extractor’s fixed reason; only a deferred busy extractor advises trying again shortly. Unreadable snapshots advise refresh or replacement, without exposing internal paths or document content.

**Comprehension (IN DEVELOPMENT on `general-enhancements`):** no new endpoint, payload, attachment kind, or schema is introduced. `Arcanum:Features:Comprehension` defaults to `false` and is effective only with `Arcanum:Features:AttachmentRetrieval`. One shared eligibility policy applies to indexing/retrieval, attach/refresh, per-turn attachment references, and durable pins. The supported MIME types are the closed registry tabulated in DESIGN §21.8: the Arcanum text container `application/vnd.arcanum.comprehension-text` (the ASCII header `ARCCOMP1\n` followed by strict UTF-8 text) and the Word, Excel, PowerPoint, OpenDocument, and Visio package types, whose macro-enabled variants are read as text only, and `application/pdf`. Legacy binary Office, RTF, EPUB, MIME messages, and OCR are not supported. Extraction reads authenticated stored bytes under the existing text input cap (1 MiB: a larger non-image attachment is refused at intake with `Attachment.TooLarge`, so no document above it is stored or extracted), emits at most 262,144 normalized UTF-16 characters without splitting surrogate pairs, waits at most 30 seconds for its parser family's extraction slot on attach, refresh, per-turn references, and pins, while background indexing never waits for it under its Grimoire work lease and takes it only when it is free (a slot not acquired in time or not free, or a family quarantined by an outstanding parser, is a transient deferral and never a failure of the document), and then uses a code-owned five-second budget for the read and the extraction. The authenticated store read is cancelled at that deadline and its cleanup is awaited under caller admission; if it exhausts the budget, extraction fails before the parser starts. PDF is the exception to everything about the in-process parser below: it is parsed only in a separate, jailed worker process that the host starts for the document and awaits, under a 10-second wall clock, a 768 MB memory and 15 s CPU ceiling, no network, one process, no writable location, and a cleared environment (on Windows only `SystemRoot` and `windir` are kept), with one slot for the worker and no quarantine; DESIGN §21.8 lists what each platform enforces. Only the pure parser over already-read bytes has a bounded caller wait and quarantine: an outstanding parser quarantines its family, further documents of that family are deferred until it ends, and callers already waiting for its slot are released as deferred at once. The store must cooperate with cancellation: arbitrary uncooperative I/O cannot be preempted or abandoned, and admission remains held until its resources are released. Unsupported MIME, valid empty output (a PDF with no extractable text on any page, such as a scan, is valid empty output), an encrypted package (password-protected OOXML, an encrypted ZIP entry, or ODF encryption data), and a package using an unsupported ZIP feature (ZIP64, multi-disk, or a compression method other than stored or deflate) are `NotEligible`. A missing or incomplete text-container header, invalid UTF-8, a malformed or over-bound package (an invalid ZIP, entry-count, expansion, or structure bound, an unsafe or duplicate entry name, a missing required part, content that does not match its declared format or whose structure leaves it open which content a reader would see, such as a part shared by two sheets, slides, pages, or masters, a duplicated style or note definition, or a property element that follows the content it governs, malformed XML or a DTD), and expiry of the document's own five-second budget are terminal `Failed`. A PDF is terminal `Failed` with one of these fixed reasons, or with one of the document reasons named after them: `Password-protected PDFs are not supported.` (a PDF that needs a password other than the empty one, or is protected by a public-key (certificate) security handler; no password is supplied, guessed, or stripped), `The PDF document is malformed.` (not a PDF, truncated, no readable trailer or root, a cross-reference stream whose entries are garbled, which leaves nothing to find by scanning, or structure the strict parser refuses, such as nesting past its ceiling or a form that invokes itself; a damaged cross-reference table whose objects can be found by scanning, and a page whose content refers to an object the file does not define, are read, not refused), `The PDF document exceeds its extraction bounds.` (more than 2,000 pages, a cross-reference table that lists more than 100,000 objects, a page of more than 250,000 glyphs, more than 2,000,000 glyphs, or the output ceiling reached before any visible text), `PDF extraction exceeded its resource limits.` (the worker crossed its memory or CPU ceiling or, on macOS, was ended by its 1 MiB file-size ceiling, a backstop since its jail gives it nowhere to write), `PDF extraction exceeded its deadline.`, `PDF extraction is unavailable on this host.` (the worker cannot run here: the host is not a Native AOT binary, so a `dotnet run` or DevHost process never starts a worker, or there is no applied filesystem jail, no limits, no re-launchable binary, or a binary replaced since the host started), or `PDF extraction failed.`.
