The document reasons a PDF shares with every other format are `Document extraction failed.` (the worker call itself threw), `Document extraction exceeded its deadline while reading its snapshot.`, `Document input does not match its bounded snapshot.`, `Document input exceeds its byte ceiling.`, and, because a PDF's chunk ranges count pages, `Document page provenance is unavailable.` (the extraction carried no page marks). Each carries a bounded, code-owned reason that never echoes an entry name, part name, document text, parser exception, exit code, or path, and none is retried automatically. A deferred extraction leaves the index row `Pending` with the reason `Document extraction is temporarily busy.` and no extraction timestamp; indexing retries it automatically, without spending an attempt, until it is extracted or fails on its own merits, and reconciliation re-queues the pending row when that in-memory retry is lost to a restart, a full queue, or a gate toggle. Attach, refresh, per-turn references, and pins report it unavailable for that call and record nothing durable. When `attach_session_file` materializes nothing, its tool result is `Not attached: '<name>' vN. <reason>` with a code-owned reason (the extraction's fixed reason, the busy reason followed by `Try attaching it again shortly.`, the image policy message, the existing refusal, or `It is already in this turn's context.`) instead of its acceptance, and the Comprehension gates for that tool are judged by the turn's settings snapshot, not by the host's current settings. A shortened Arcanum text container ending at a valid UTF-8 boundary remains valid text; that container has no length declaration and cannot detect the truncation. Empty parsed rows retain an extraction timestamp. Reconciliation re-evaluates prior `Binary`/`NotEligible` rows with no extraction timestamp when effectively enabled, without rewriting their kind. That covers earlier `.docx`, `.xlsx`, and `.pptx` attachments, the only extensions that were already detected as their registry types; earlier `.docm`, `.xlsm`, `.pptm`, `.odt`, `.ods`, `.odp`, `.vsdx`, and `.vsdm` attachments were stored as `application/octet-stream` and are read only after the file is uploaded again. A MIME is normalized by cutting it at the first `;`, trimming only space, tab, CR, and LF, and folding ASCII case; a no-break space, another Unicode space, or a non-ASCII letter is part of the value, so that spelling is not a registry type. Gate-off suppresses retained document chunks as well as new extraction; `POST /api/memory/search`, an operator inspection surface, still searches them. Documents are excluded from The Tapestry regardless of the gate until a later change adds support; attachment generations containing document leaves, including mixed summaries, contribute no context. Extracted context identifies the source format by its per-format label (for example `Word document`), does not preserve layout, and uses the existing adaptive fences as literal untrusted DATA. When the extraction was truncated (at the 262,144-character output ceiling, or by a retention overflow such as the shared-string limit), the attach, refresh, per-turn reference, and pin notices read `UNTRUSTED DATA; <label>; layout not preserved; truncated at the extraction limit` and a pin reports status `Truncated`; retrieval chunks are not marked as truncated. PDF text carries one more clause. The notices read `UNTRUSTED DATA; PDF document; layout not preserved; may include text a reader would not see` (followed by `; truncated at the extraction limit` when cut); a retrieved excerpt states the same clause on its `source-format:` line, `PDF document; layout not preserved; may include text a reader would not see`, with the `UNTRUSTED DATA` warning on its own `warning:` line and no truncation suffix, because retrieval chunks are not marked as truncated. A PDF is extracted from every text-showing run of every page in content-stream order, with right-to-left runs that a producer wrote in visual order restored to logical order, the Latin ligature code points U+FB00 to U+FB06 spelled out, and every run of horizontal whitespace collapsed to one space with each line trimmed and the lines left empty dropped (so the padding of a producer that lays text out on a character grid neither reaches the model nor uses up the 262,144-character ceiling), and with no visibility projection, so the text may include runs a reader would not have seen (white-on-white, zero-size, invisible-render-mode, or off-page text). Each page with text begins with a `[PDF page <n>]` line (a form a document's own footer does not print, so a footer reading `Page 1` on PDF page 3 reads as page text under `[PDF page 3]`, and a `[PDF page` that a page's own text prints is written `\[PDF page`, so only Arcanum's lines are headings), a page with no text is skipped, and a retrieved PDF excerpt states its range in pages: its `chunk/range` line reads `chars S-E; pages A-B`, where the chunk's `StartLine` and `EndLine` columns hold the first and last page rather than lines. With Comprehension off, a PDF stays `NotEligible`. Office, OpenDocument, and Visio extraction is a bounded visible-text projection: hidden text, hidden sheets, rows and slides, comments, speaker notes, deleted tracked changes, formulas (the cached value is kept), and metadata are not emitted. A table is projected as one tab-separated line per row, Word equations as linear text, and footnotes and endnotes after the body (a Word note only when a shown reference reaches it); headers and footers, charts, SmartArt, Excel drawings and text boxes, PowerPoint equations, Word `altChunk` content and symbols, automatic list numbering, and the marks of footnote and endnote references are not projected. A spreadsheet's visible sheet with nothing to show writes no `Sheet:` header. See DESIGN §21.8 for the extractor isolation boundary.

`Attachment.Disabled` → **403**; `Attachment.InvalidRequest`, `InvalidContent`, `InvalidReference`, and `SourceUnavailable` → **400**; `Attachment.NotFound` and `SourceNotFound` → **404**; `Attachment.LimitExceeded` → **409**; `Attachment.TooLarge` → **413**. Error messages are bounded and never echo a source path. Refresh failures use this same mapping instead of collapsing every outcome to a conflict.

### 8.19 Server lifecycle (PID file)

The code-owned path is `~/.config/arcanum/arcanum.pid`. Startup claims the file with an atomic exclusive create and fails if it names a live Arcanum owner, with a message that names the file and says to remove it when no Arcanum process owns that PID; a stale file (malformed, a dead process, or a recycled id held by a process that started after the file was last written) is replaced, and two starters can never both claim it: a fresh claim is the atomic create, and a stale file is deleted and re-claimed only while holding an exclusive `arcanum.pid.replace.lock` beside it, after reading the file again under that lock, so a starter that read the same stale file just before another replaced it finds that starter's claim and refuses with the live-owner message instead of deleting it. A stale file that cannot be removed, or a PID file this account may not read, fails with its own message and reason rather than the generic "another Arcanum process is starting at the same time". Shutdown deletes the file only if it still names this process. DevHost and `serve` share the same path and therefore cannot run concurrently.

### 8.20 Unified data lifecycle API (`/api/data`)

Every route in this family is protected by the normal `/api` key filter. Status and planning are read-only. Mutations are operation-specific; there is no generic memory-delete route and the CLI never mutates the Grimoire directly. CLI confirmation is an additional operator gate, not a substitute for HTTP authentication. Factory reset also requires the exact body token shown below.

**Requests (camel-case property names; canonical enum values retain CLR casing):**

```jsonc
// POST /api/data/prune/plan
{
  "operation": "Prune",
  "targetId": null,
  "memoryScope": null
}

// POST /api/data/prune
{
  "request": {
    "operation": "Prune",
    "targetId": null,
    "memoryScope": null
  },
  "expectedPlanId": "optional SHA-256 plan id from a prior preview"
}

// PUT /api/data/retention
{
  "dataClass": "archived-sessions",
  "enabled": true,
  "days": 180
}

// PUT /api/data/retention (disable without replacing the prior day value)
{
  "dataClass": "archived-sessions",
  "enabled": false
}

// POST /api/data/memory/reset
{
  "scope": "Entry",
  "expectedPlanId": "optional SHA-256 plan id from /api/data/memory/reset/plan",
  "campaignId": "optional GUID; Saga and Lexicon only"
}

// POST /api/data/memory/reset/plan
{
  "scope": "Covenant"
}

// POST /api/data/factory-reset
{
  "confirmation": "factory-reset",
  "expectedPlanId": "optional confirmed plan id",
  "requestedOperationId": "optional installation-handoff replay GUID"
}

// POST /api/data/factory-reset/plan
{
  "scope": "Workspace",
  "workspace": {
    "campaignId": "b43d153c-60bf-4cb9-a3e4-46353abfb9ec",
    "workspaceRoot": "/absolute/registered/campaign/root"
  }
}
```

An installation-reset-owned global/all request adds the optional trailing `installationResetHandoff` object; ordinary callers that omit it keep the exact handoff-free JSON and behavior above:

```json
{
  "confirmation": "factory-reset",
  "expectedPlanId": "confirmed online data-plan id",
  "requestedOperationId": "0ef8ab55-5c55-4f88-97ef-d204ad13e67d",
  "installationResetHandoff": {
    "requestedOperationId": "0ef8ab55-5c55-4f88-97ef-d204ad13e67d",
    "installationPlanId": "confirmed installation-plan id",
    "scope": "Global",
    "acceptedBinding": {
      "bindingId": "confirmed installation binding id",
      "selectedRoots": ["grimoire"],
      "excludedRoots": ["backups"],
      "preservedBackups": [],
      "credentialAccounts": ["master-api-key"],
      "dataPlanIds": ["confirmed online data-plan id"]
    }
  }
}
```

Every handoff property is required once the object is present. `requestedOperationId` must be nonempty and identical in both locations. `installationPlanId` is the installation-reset plan identity and is distinct in meaning from the singleton `acceptedBinding.dataPlanIds[0]`, which must equal the request's `expectedPlanId`; their text is not required to differ. `Global` forbids `workspace`; `All` requires the exact valid workspace binding. Arrays and strings retain the current installation-plan bounds, and the complete candidate is preflighted against the 64 KiB authenticated-envelope boundary before any key, external identity, anchor, or file write.

`POST /api/data/factory-reset/plan` is a loopback-only planning seam used by the installation-reset coordinator. "Loopback" is judged as every other loopback-only gate judges it (`ArcanumTransportPeer`): the socket the request arrived on **and** the effective peer after forwarded-headers rewriting must both be loopback, so a same-host proxy relaying a remote client, or a forged `X-Forwarded-For`, is **403** `Data.Blocked`. `Global` forbids a workspace binding. `Workspace` requires the exact registered Campaign id and canonical root. The existing `POST /api/data/factory-reset` confirmation contract remains available for compatibility and retains its data-only boundary. An ordinary installation reset of any scope does not call this route: it plans locally under the stopped-host maintenance lock, so it needs the host stopped. Only the external-remediation arm (`data factory-reset --all --apply --external-remediation-attestation <file>`) obtains this authenticated online plan and rebinds its exact `planId` into the local installation plan before disclosure or confirmation; there an unreachable host, missing API key, absent current Covenant inventory, or local/online candidate mismatch fails before active state or shutdown. The reset-plan route, reset apply, factory-plan route, and factory apply all require `CovenantAuthorityRequirement.LifecycleManage` through the pre-binding authority boundary.

Factory managed-tree inventory refusals return **409** `Data.ManagedTreeRefused` in the existing `ApiResponse<DataRetentionPlan>` error envelope from `POST /api/data/factory-reset/plan`. Factory apply preflight returns the same code/status in its existing apply envelope before mutation admission. The message identifies the closed managed-root role (`attachments`, `files`, or `logs`), closed refusal cause, and a safely rendered entry relative to that original lexical root. `.` names the root; `<unavailable>` withholds an entry whose containment cannot be proved. The display normalizes separators, escapes literal backslashes and quote/control/Unicode format/line-separator/surrogate code units (`\uXXXX`), and is bounded to 240 characters including `...` when truncated. It never includes an absolute installation/workspace path, symlink target, original exception or driver text. General inventory warnings contain only the closed role/cause and typed operation ID when known. Inspect and restore the managed-directory layout before planning again; preserve uncertain files and recovery evidence. Missing roots remain healthy empty inventory. A failure after reset work may have changed state retains its existing reconciliation/recovery code, rather than this preflight refusal. Authentication, loopback/Covenant authority, read-lease disposal, and cancellation behavior are unchanged; no new payload fields are introduced.

A Covenant reset or healthy-catalog factory erasure that returns a non-commit completion preserves its typed error envelope and status mapping. Its content-free message includes the durable operation identifier, separate confirmed/not-confirmed canonical-erasure and local-secure-erasure facts, the verified admission disposition, and `arcanum operation show <id>` guidance. Unconfirmed progress is not proof that nothing was erased; only a verified rollback or commit disposition reports reopening. The operator is told to resolve the current outcome before starting a second erasure. Local proof cannot revoke external disclosures. No payload fields or error codes are added. A verified rollback additionally confirms that nothing was erased; when its code is `Grimoire.WorkDrainTimeout`, the message explains that ordinary work exceeded the drain window and says to retry when the host is idle. That no-erasure claim requires the verified rollback disposition, never just false progress flags. A factory failure reported after a verified commit acknowledges that admission reopened; an unknown disposition never claims reopening.

The typed handoff arm is host-owned. After API-key authentication and recovery-route admission, the endpoint validates the complete request and active recovery identity before it borrows the singleton installation maintenance lock. It reads the exact database installation UUID, begins or recovers the authenticated V2 publication, and only then starts/replays the data operation. Handoff-free requests do not resolve the coordinator or lock accessor. A pre-effect `Data.PlanChanged` retires the prepared publication under that same lock before the error response; cancellation, an uncertain response, any post-effect failure, or mismatched replay evidence leaves it active. On success the host advances the publication with the exact requested/server operation identities, data-plan identity, reconciliation, and observed counts before serializing the result. An existing exact proof is idempotent; a completed LRO replay that reports zero counts cannot erase a prior nonzero proof.

The V2 active envelope is internal persistence, not a second public payload. Its outer profile, installation, operation, revision, previous digest, location, scope, and plan fields are authenticated data and remain display-only until AES-GCM authentication succeeds and the decrypted payload repeats the exact operation/scope/plan. The envelope format version stays 2 while the record's payload version is 3, because what moved is the plaintext inside the envelope rather than the envelope itself: a record now carries the nested-transition receipt described below. Payload version 2 is read as a strict legacy value, may carry no receipt, and is migrated forward by the record's next publication; only version 3 is sealed. Partial, unknown, rolled-back, cross-profile, cross-installation, wrong-location, missing-key, missing-anchor, and noncanonical evidence all produce one content-free recovery refusal, and so does every parent-binding disagreement: a claimed receipt whose transition journal is absent, a journal carrying a parent binding with no reset record to be the nested arm of, a binding neither record can reproduce, a receipt naming a different nested operation, a nested transition of any kind other than a healthy-catalog factory erasure, a completion receipt whose effect digest is not the journal's, a stored completion beside a journal outside the retirement suffix that receipt implies, and two present terminal winner digests that differ. Which arm refused is deliberately absent from the message, because every one of them has the same remedy and the difference between them is not operator-visible detail.

`DataRetentionOperation` is emitted as `Prune`, `DeleteSession`, `DeleteAttachment`, `ResetMemory`, `FactoryReset`, or `ResetWorkspace`. The two DELETE routes construct the corresponding target operation from `{id}`; callers do not send a body. `MemoryResetScope` is emitted as `Entry`, `Attachments`, `Workspace`, `Saga`, `Lexicon`, `Covenant`, or `CampaignSummary`. `CampaignSummary` (code 6) selects contribution and aggregate summary artifacts, and accepts optional `campaignId`. `CampaignSummaries` (retention code 31) has its own default-disabled 365-day policy. Ordinary reset refuses selected labelled artifacts before deleting anything; authorized protected erasure uses the shared sensitivity purge kernel. `Covenant` uses the same plan/apply vocabulary but never the ordinary reset executor: preview returns its content-free inventory, and apply commits the V4 `InventoryPrepared` launch checkpoint before acquiring exclusive admission and then awaits the ten-phase coordinator (DESIGN §10.20.1–§10.20.4). A Covenant reset is always standalone work: only a healthy-catalog factory erasure may run as the nested arm of a broader workflow, and a Covenant reset that arrives carrying a parent binding is refused rather than adopted. Enum input matching is case-insensitive; responses use the canonical casing shown here. `RetentionDataClass` response values likewise retain their CLR casing, such as `ActiveSessions`. `Covenant` is inventoried and has no configurable rule at all: `PUT /api/data/retention` with that class is refused **400** `Data.InvalidRequest` with a message saying so, deliberately distinct from the "not recognized" refusal an unknown class receives. `RetentionRuleUpdateRequest.dataClass` names one configured rule. Matching is case-insensitive after hyphens, underscores, and spaces are removed; grouped aliases include `attachments`, `workspace-indexes`, `accounting`, and `daemon-history`, while typed attachment, batch-file, workspace, and accounting subclasses resolve to their governing rule. `days` is clamped to 1–3,650 and is required when `enabled` is `true`. It is optional when `enabled` is `false`; disabling a rule preserves its prior day value and does not add a capability restriction to explicit deletion. `GET /api/data/retention` returns the complete effective policy, including sweep bounds, accounting floor, and protected session ids. A successful PUT persists the normalized rule and makes it immediately authoritative for subsequent GET, status, and planning calls. The same process-authoritative snapshot controls subsequent inference- and guardrail-log planning and apply calls. Log writers only create, secure, and append dated files; they never enumerate or delete historical files. The former host/security day fields are removed. A query without `from` searches all dated files, and only the canonical `AuditLogs` / `GuardrailLogs` retention rules can trigger age-based mutation. Mutation selectors and `enabled` are required: omission never selects a default destructive scope or silently substitutes `false`. The sole presence-aware field is retention `days`, under the rule above. Data-class and memory-scope choices accept documented names and aliases only; JSON/CLI numeric enum spellings are rejected before mutation.

**Status.** `DataRetentionStatus` contains `generatedAt`, `items`, aggregate `rows`, `files`, and `estimatedBytes`, plus `preservedOutsideSelectedRoot`. Each `DataRetentionStatusItem` has `dataClass`, `rows`, `files`, `estimatedBytes`, `policyEnabled`, nullable `retentionDays`, `store`, and safe `provenance`. The typed class list and physical ownership are defined in DESIGN §5.4.7. For a composite physical owner, `rows` includes its canonical row plus owned companion, index, and provenance rows. File counts and `estimatedBytes` describe managed files that actually exist at inspection time; they do not estimate missing metadata targets or SQLite page allocation. Batch input/output/error items count role references, so one uploaded file may appear in more than one of those items; the response-level aggregate totals exclude those reference-only roles and sum physical owners. The response contains no plaintext content, secret, or host source path. Optional `memoryErasure` carries `fingerprints`, `receipts`, and `receiptSubjects`: content-free counts of the selective-erasure evidence (§8.35), with no fingerprint, key id, digest, store, or scope. It sits beside a `MemoryErasureEvidence` item whose `rows` is the sum of the three counts, always with `policyEnabled: false` and a null `retentionDays`; a catalog below Core 13 reports zeros. Erasure evidence is never aged out and no prune or reset selects it: `PUT /api/data/retention` with `memory-erasure-evidence` is refused **400** `Data.InvalidRequest` with its own message, and the rows go only through a release, operator re-creation, `memory erasure reset-key`, restore's destination-authoritative join, or a full installation reset.

**Covenant inventory.** `DataRetentionStatus` and `DataRetentionPlan` each carry an optional `covenant` object with `rows`, `managedFiles`, `localArtifacts`, `affectedSessions`, `possibleDisclosures`, and `disclosureCountKind` (`Exact` or `LowerBound`). It is counts only — no subject text, key, digest preimage, path, or Campaign identity. `possibleDisclosures` folds only **nonrevocable** buckets, because it sits beside a statement about what local removal cannot revoke, and one lower-bound bucket carries the whole total to `LowerBound`. The buckets are the effective disclosure state: each acknowledged receipt counts as soon as it commits, and receipts recorded before the live disclosure fold existed are counted too, which makes `disclosureCountKind` `LowerBound` rather than reporting no disclosure. Status and non-erasure plans omit the field when no Covenant read capability could be taken: a zero would read as a measurement where the honest answer is that nothing was measured. A `Covenant` status item appears in `items` only when measured, always with `policyEnabled: false` and a null `retentionDays`. Explicit Covenant reset and factory plans instead require a current installation inventory and fail closed rather than returning an unmeasured destructive plan. The `covenant` object remains outside `planId` for prune and workspace planning, so unrelated family writes do not expire operations that do not select it. Covenant reset and factory reset do select the family, so their plan identities bind all five counts and `disclosureCountKind` (DESIGN §10.20.1).

When a reset or factory preview carries Covenant inventory, its endpoint acquires exactly one installation read lease with that inventory and transfers both to `CovenantProtectedJsonResult<DataRetentionPlan>`. The lease is revalidated immediately before the first byte and released only after serialization or typed refusal. Success and failure on all four lifecycle-authorized routes carry `Cache-Control: no-store, private`, `Pragma: no-cache`, and `Expires: 0`; `ETag` and `Last-Modified` are removed. The lease itself never crosses the wire or survives into CLI confirmation. A protected `Request` or `Installation` plan admission that does not return its required live lease is refused; this does not change the ordinary lease-consuming `PlanAsync` wrapper used by prune/workspace callers or feature-off status projection. Within a healthy-catalog factory apply, the current plan and catalog are established from one installation snapshot and any mismatch before effects fails closed. Reset apply re-plans under installation authority and binds the optional `MemoryResetRequest.expectedPlanId`; the bundled CLI always sends the preview id after confirmation. Omitting it preserves backward-compatible direct API behavior. Factory installation validation binds its matching online plan.

**Dry-run plan.** `DataRetentionPlan` contains `planId`, the normalized `request`, `generatedAt`, per-class `items`, `blockers`, `conflicts`, aggregate `rows`/`files`/`estimatedBytes`/ `derivedRecords`, deterministic `candidateIds`, and `requiresConfirmation`. Every item reports `dataClass`, `rows`, `files`, `estimatedBytes`, and `derivedRecords`. Blockers report `dataClass`, `resourceId`, `reasonCode`, and a sanitized `message`; conflicts report `code`, `resourceId`, and `message`. Optional `sagaCuration` contains `pinnedRows` and `pinnedRowsExemptFromPlan` (§8.30): every pinned Saga memory, and the pinned memories this plan's own cutoff would otherwise have selected. Optional `lexiconCuration` contains `pinnedRows` and `pinnedRowsExemptFromPlan` (§8.33). Both are plan-only inventory. Optional `memoryErasure` (`fingerprints`, `receipts`, `receiptSubjects`) appears on factory-reset plans only and counts the erasure evidence a standalone factory reset keeps: a `--global` or `--all` installation reset removes that evidence with the Grimoire and the erasure key, so after one nothing remains in force. It is never a plan item, it is absent from every other plan, and it is outside `planId`, so a release between two previews does not change the plan identity. Planning reads the same dependency graph used by apply and does not write rows, files, operation history, or checkpoints.

**Apply.** Apply recomputes the current plan. If `expectedPlanId` is present and differs, the server returns `Data.PlanChanged`; clients must preview again. The bundled CLI always fetches the exact plan immediately before `--apply`, shows it in human mode, confirms its id and totals, and sends that id as `expectedPlanId`; `--json --yes` performs the same binding but emits only the final apply result as one machine-readable JSON value. Policy prune leaves blocked/conflicting candidates untouched while applying unrelated eligible candidates. Explicit session/attachment deletion and scoped/global reset fail when their selected target has a blocker or conflict. A prune candidate that becomes protected after planning is preserved and reported through a `Data.PlanChanged` conflict while later independent candidates may still run. Its checkpoint stays before the earliest preserved candidate so recovery re-evaluates that boundary instead of silently skipping it. A successful `DataRetentionApplyResult` reports `operationId`, the applied `planId`, deleted row/file/estimated-byte/derived-record totals, `reconciled`, and the plan diagnostics. A memory reset of any scope but `Covenant` whose preview selected nothing applies as a successful, reconciled no-op with zero counts, and the bundled CLI exits `0`; rows that appear after that empty preview are refused as **409** `Data.PlanChanged` (or, when they arrive inside the apply's own transaction window, **409** `Data.Conflict`), and a fresh preview is needed. A Lexicon candidate pinned after planning is preserved with the distinct `Data.PinnedAfterPlanning` conflict; the plan inventory is not repeated in the apply result. Execution is bounded, durable-operation-backed, checkpointed, and restart-idempotent. Each planned candidate receives a bounded post-delete ownership check for its selected rows, derived records, and owned files; `reconciled` is true only after every applicable candidate check succeeds. This is not a global orphan-vacuum operation.

`MemoryResetScope.Covenant` is the protected exception to ordinary result accounting. Apply takes a fresh installation planning lease, rebuilds the current plan and five-count inventory, validates a nonempty current dataset, and compares the supplied preview plan id before it starts a durable mutation. For this explicit erasure, the identity includes the inventory; neither ordinary-plan drift nor changed counts can silently cross confirmation. It then commits the `InventoryPrepared` launch, releases the planning lease, and enters `CovenantErasureCoordinator` under the launch-derived owner. It returns only after `CommitAndReopen`, fresh disclosure-writer reopen, general-admission reopen, exclusive release, and durable terminalization. Its success keeps the originally confirmed plan id and reports zero deletion counters: retained disclosure evidence is part of the preview, so those aggregates are not an exact deleted-row/file/byte ledger. A client cancellation before immutable proof may park the operation for exact-owner recovery. After proof it cannot interrupt publication, writer reopen, disposition, or failure recording.

**Protected erasure inventory refusal.** The existing error envelope gains no fields: `Error.CovenantInventoryFailure` is in-process only and ignored by source-generated serialization. Reset/factory completion propagates its existing blocking error code with operation/proof/disposition guidance; safe label/artifact GUIDs, declared artifact kind and closed reason are recorded in the coordinator warning, rather than added to the response. `StorageBusy` (SQLite primary 5/6), environmental `StorageUnavailable` (7/8/9/10/13/14 or I/O/access failure), and `AdmissionUnavailable` map to **503** `Covenant.Unavailable`. Missing/malformed adopted managed ownership maps to **503** `Covenant.ManualArtifactErasureRequired`; cleanup failure maps to **503** `Covenant.MaintenanceFailed`. Writer restoration or terminal finalization failure can replace the caller code with `Covenant.MaintenanceFailed`; the warning retains the original inventory cause and reports reopening as unconfirmed when no completion disposition was established. Invalid datasets, moved snapshots, malformed labels, unknown artifact kinds, unsupported requests, capacity overflow and query/schema faults map to **503** `Covenant.IntegrityFailure`. In particular, a missing declared table is a structural fault, not a retryable busy condition. No response or warning includes stored payloads, raw invalid identifiers, file paths, driver messages or exceptions. Retry only after a reported transient condition is resolved and the completion confirms reopen; a kept-closed outcome requires diagnosis of that same operation. See Command Reference, **Covenant erasure inventory refusal**.

**Covenant storage and disclosure admission diagnostics.** Sensitivity-label and Campaign-summary storage failures use existing **503** `Covenant.Unavailable` for busy/locked or environmental storage conditions and **503** `Covenant.IntegrityFailure` for structural/query/guard faults, with contextual guidance and numeric SQLite codes rather than engine text. A disclosure writer's initial preparation refusal retains its original code. Subsequent requests while it remains closed after that fault return **503** `Covenant.Unavailable`, identify the earlier closed cause and direct the operator to diagnose storage and restore the host safely; repeating a request or publishing healthy availability cannot reopen it. Genuine writer maintenance/dispose refusal remains **409** `Covenant.LifecycleConflict`. A failed connection release remains **503** `Covenant.MaintenanceFailed` and cannot be treated as permission to reopen or retry. These refinements add no payload fields or public reopen operation; startup adoption's existing manual-recovery contract and erasure completion proof/disposition remain unchanged.


For every file-bearing prune candidate and explicit deletion, the durable checkpoint carries an exact `ARCAMUT2` manifest with the mutation subtype/target, managed-root role, normalized relative path, captured no-follow identity metadata, and operation-scoped same-parent quarantine prefix. Recovery examines only that manifest-declared namespace; unrelated or malformed quarantine is rejected fail-closed. Factory reset uses a separate whole-root recovery contract and re-inventories the complete managed roots when it resumes.

The dependency contract removes owned derived rows and bytes—attachment chunks/embeddings/index state with the attachment, Entry embeddings with Entries, and workspace embeddings with chunks. Long Rest discovery data (generations, nodes, edges, indexes, embeddings, work and effect rows) derived from a Session's memories goes with the Session, and is counted among the derived records. Managed-file deletion uses no-follow identity capture and apply-boundary revalidation; an object that changes identity is preserved with its metadata and the operation fails closed. Batch references protect uploaded files. Pinned Entries/context/attachments, protected sessions, active operations/inference/idempotency leases, outstanding budget reservations, and in-progress batches remain visible as blockers/conflicts. A global reset reports a durable operation under `Data.ActiveOperation` when starting the host settles it, and under `Data.OperationNeedsAttention` when it is waiting for an operator and so stays exactly as it is across any number of host starts (a `ReconciliationRequired` operation that startup recovery does not select again, such as a parked Long Rest sweep, or a queued Long Rest sweep request that never started): the first is cleared by starting the host once, the second by `arcanum operation cancel <id>`, or `arcanum operation retry <id>` and letting it finish, against a started host. The effective accounting age is never shorter than `accountingMinimumDays`, and `Sessions.TotalCostUsd` is not accounting authority.

Deleting an attachment does not silently delete independently retained Saga or Lexicon facts. Their typed provenance remains and resolves the missing source as unavailable. Factory reset clears only managed data beneath the configured root and never silently deletes external backups, registered out-of-root workspace content, configuration, or security/credential/key material. The Forge-owned local histories are outside this API's implementation boundary and remain untouched; no coordinated cleanup integration is added by this feature. Prior terminal operation history is cleared in dependency order, while the successful factory reset necessarily leaves its own completed durable-operation marker as the audit/recovery record for that mutation. The `DaemonExecutions` status/plan item includes both process-local execution summaries and persisted Unseen Servant schedule watermarks, so factory-reset previews account for both before clearing them. Factory previews exclude reference-only batch roles and count dependency/index/provenance rows as derived records.

With a healthy Covenant catalog, one `DataRetentionFactoryReset` operation owns both protected and ordinary deletion. V2 `InventoryPrepared` is committed from the confirmed installation snapshot; protected database and managed-file kernels run first; then a required restart-idempotent ordinary continuation rebuilds and applies the ordinary plan after `ManagedArtifactsProcessed` and before `HandlesClosed`. The same exclusive lifecycle therefore covers ordinary cleanup, handle closure, WAL truncation, compaction, sidecar proof, runtime publication, writer reopen, general-admission reopen, and exclusive release. Recovery adopts the exact checkpoint owner before readiness, reruns the continuation at `ManagedArtifactsProcessed`, and skips it at `HandlesClosed` or later; V0 remains unchanged. The response preserves the original plan id and reports the continuation's executor-observed ordinary totals. A same-snapshot catalog or plan mismatch is fail-closed with zero effects.

Factory plans retain `Data.InferenceRunActive` and its exact resource identity in the confirmed `planId`. Only this diagnostic is drainable at the two pre-launch eligibility checks: already-admitted inference must finish during stage one. The factory-only closed-catalog preflight then refuses any still-`Running` inference row with `Data.Conflict` (**409**) before canonical, managed-file, ordinary, or credential effects, and performs a proven pre-effect rollback and reopen. All other conflicts and stale `Data.PlanChanged` checks remain strict; ordinary continuation and its transactional conflict check remain strict too. Completed accounting, audit, and indexing work from the drain is included in the continuation's re-inventoried ordinary totals without changing the protected launch binding or the response's confirmed plan id.

A global or all-scope installation reset that runs its data phase offline is the one workflow that launches this erasure as a nested arm. It claims the nested operation identity in the same publication as its point of no return, because that is the same fact from the other side: from there a nested database transition is about to exist, and a crash before the launch has to read as "one was started" rather than as "none ever was". The erasure's transition journal commits a parent-receipt binding before any effect — SHA-256 under a dedicated domain over the outer operation identity, the nested operation identity, the nested canonical effect digest, and the completion phase. The terminal winner digest is deliberately outside that preimage: it cannot be known before the effect, and a binding computable only afterwards could not be committed beforehand. After the exact terminal compare-exchange and before the journal may retire, the erasure publishes its completion receipt into the outer authenticated record, rereads that record, and recomputes the binding from what came back; equality is the proof that the two records describe one piece of work. An already-exact receipt is reread and never republished, so a replay does not advance the outer envelope revision a second time. A receipt that cannot be published, or that reads back as something else, keeps the transition closed and repeats no effect. A workspace-scope reset, an online handoff completion, and a direct factory reset claim nothing and publish nothing. The offline arm runs in the CLI process, through a reset-only facade that acts on the claimed identity alone and never restarts a launch row at version zero.

`requestedOperationId` is the optional installation-handoff replay key and is valid only with `expectedPlanId`. The server atomically starts or replays one factory LRO for that identity and returns the caller identity separately in `DataRetentionApplyResult.requestedOperationId`; `operationId` is always the distinct server-created durable operation identity. The replay key is never a root operation, server operation, Covenant gate owner, or lease owner. A replay with the same fixed apply/effect digests returns the same operation outcome; an identity/digest conflict is refused without creating a second erasure. The installation reset accepts completion only when the response proves the confirmed plan, matching requested identity, distinct server identity, reconciliation, and terminal success, then stores that content-free proof monotonically before shutdown.

The factory LRO enters one lease maintainer immediately after named or unnamed durable start. Maintenance spans re-plan/catalog proof, V2 checkpoint publication, coordinator execution, the ordinary continuation, and terminalization, closing the former pre-checkpoint adoption window. Ordinary continuation renewal/deletion is a compare-and-set over operation ID, `Running`, exact owner, and live expiry. Factory V0/V2 and reset V4 recovery use the same maintainer with the reconciler-adopted owner; loss of ownership stops further work and returns content-free requires-attention recovery. The launch checkpoints are `DataRetentionFactoryTransitionLaunchV2` for a healthy-catalog factory erasure and `CovenantOfflineTransitionLaunchV4` for a Covenant reset, filed under different durable kinds because one shape spanning both would be a payload whose meaning depended on the row it happened to be read from. Nesting adds no `CovenantResetPhase` member, no reconciliation step, and no further checkpoint version.

Global factory apply never treats a missing Covenant lease or inventory as permission to delete only ordinary data. The stopped-host command line is no exception: it measures the inventory through the same gate and refuses with the cause's own code when it cannot. Production composition supplies the gate and catalog independently of the inference feature policy and may prove an empty family; an unmeasurable inventory, unhealthy catalog, or missing exclusive lifecycle fails closed before ordinary effects. The ordinary cleanup kernel still uses its restart-idempotent immediate database transaction plus daemon-start gate, but only as the protected coordinator's continuation or the unchanged legacy V0 recovery arm. Prune and explicit data mutations also share an atomic cross-kind single-flight start, while elapsed-time heartbeats keep ownership during a slow candidate. Inference and guardrail audit writers share a singleton managed-log publication gate with factory reset. The reset takes that gate before its database writer transaction and holds it through log inventory, quarantine, commit, cleanup, and reconciliation: an append that wins is counted and cleared, while an append waiting behind the reset is published afterward and is not reported as a deleted file. Each logger retains its private append serialization and best-effort failure behavior. Managed factory-reset files move into identity-verified, owner-only same-parent quarantine before the database commit. Rollback restores them; after commit cleanup removes them, and restart recovery re-inventories the managed roots, discovers any quarantine left by a crash, and resumes the idempotent whole-root cleanup. Logical row deletion and file unlinking do not promise physical secure erasure on SSD, copy-on-write, snapshot, WAL/free-page, cache, encrypted-replica, or backup media.

This route family maps `Data.InvalidRequest` and `Data.ConfirmationRequired` to **400**, `Data.NotFound` to **404**, `Data.PlanChanged`, `Data.Blocked`, `Data.Conflict`, and `Data.OperationNotFinalized` to **409**, and `Data.ReconciliationFailed` and `Data.QuarantineRecoveryRequired` to **500**. Other failures delegate to the central `ArcanumErrorMapper`; protected erasure failures therefore keep their frozen statuses, including **503** for `Covenant.ErasureIncomplete`, `Covenant.MaintenanceFailed`, `Covenant.ManualArtifactErasureRequired`, and `Covenant.ManualRecoveryRequired`. An unexpected unclassified apply failure is **500**. The retention rule PUT route returns **400** for `Data.InvalidRequest`; an unexpected policy-store failure is **500**.

### 8.21 The Proving Grounds

Ephemeral Trial + Inquisitors (`regex` / `jsonSchema` / `semantic` FastModel judge). `jsonSchema` delegates to the shared `JsonSchemaHelper` and enforces exactly these keywords: `type` (a string, or a non-empty array of type strings of which any one satisfies the schema, `null` included; an unrecognized type anywhere in it fails the verdict, and a `type` of any other shape is an unreadable schema), `properties`, `required`, `items`, `enum`, and `additionalProperties` (boolean). Every other keyword (for example `minimum`, `pattern`, `oneOf`, `$ref`) is ignored, and an unreadable schema is a failed verdict. Targets: spell / prompt / apprenticeGoal. Terminology strict — industry LLM-test jargon prohibited. Errors §8.23.


#### Exact paired memory evaluations

If a live execution is still publishing its initial registration/checkpoint, exact replay, get and delete wait for that boundary and then read current metadata. Get/delete resolve ownership after waiting, including genuine Campaign ownership. Waiting honors request cancellation and performs no hydration, provider call or charge. Erasure during initial publication still yields the retained-identity Gone result; waiting never recreates evidence. If creation crosses an absent ownership read, the retained exact identity triggers one publication wait and ownership re-read. A proven pre-creation NotFound remains 404 under the original request authority even if creation follows; it cannot become a scope conflict from mixing observations.

The three POST routes above require master-key authentication and clean ProtectedRead authority **before the body is read**. IDs, scope and digests travel in JSON bodies only. All replies use the protected `Cache-Control: no-store, private`, `Pragma: no-cache`, `Expires: 0` tuple and no validators. `X-Arcanum-Context-Policy: none` is refused: an explicit selected-memory experiment cannot silently suppress its treatment. No request can supply authority, a lease, execution surface, tools, history, attachments, workspace path, additional option fields or arbitrary check labels.

`run` accepts the following closed source-generated body (identities/digests below are illustrative; obtain exact current target coordinates from their owning store):

```json
{
  "evaluationId": "11111111-1111-1111-1111-111111111111",
  "scope": { "kind": "Global", "campaignId": null },
  "providerName": "configured-provider",
  "model": "exact-configured-model",
  "options": { "temperature": 0, "topP": null, "seed": null, "maxOutputTokens": 512, "reasoningEffort": null },
  "workload": { "systemPrompt": "Answer the supplied task.", "userPrompt": "The bounded operator workload." },
  "memory": {
    "covenant": [{ "entryId": "22222222-2222-2222-2222-222222222222", "versionId": "33333333-3333-3333-3333-333333333333", "fragmentDigest": { "bytes": "AAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAA=" } }],
    "saga": [],
    "lexicon": []
  },
  "checks": [{ "kind": "regex", "pattern": "expected", "shouldMatch": true, "ignoreCase": false }],
  "pairCount": 5
}
```

Scope is explicitly `Global` with no Campaign ID or `Campaign` with one nonempty `campaignId`; no Session or physical-root proof is inferred. Covenant targets name `entryId`, exact immutable `versionId` and the admitted `fragmentDigest` (available from Covenant explain decisions; authored/rendered hashes are different). Saga targets name `memoryId`, exact Annals `versionId`, `hashFormat` and `contentDigest`; Lexicon targets replace `memoryId` with `entryId`. Every evaluation digest on input and output uses the existing generated `CovenantDigest` object shape: `{"bytes":"<base64 encoding of exactly 32 bytes>"}`. This includes target `fragmentDigest`/`contentDigest`, response `reportDigest`, registration/plan/check/response-evidence digests and cleanup `expectedReportDigest`; a digest is not a hex string on these routes. Missing/null/wrong-length `bytes`, invalid base64, and unknown digest-object members are malformed requests. Covenant explain decisions expose FragmentDigest as hexadecimal: decode that hex to exactly 32 bytes, base64-encode those bytes, then put the base64 text in `bytes`. Do not base64-encode the hexadecimal characters or substitute authored/rendered hashes. The zero-byte digest object in the example illustrates the encoding only; a real target must use the exact retained owning-store digest. The adapter proves current intervention eligibility and retained exact dependencies under the real request Turn lease. Scope and source memory never change as a result of evaluation.

Only Regex and JsonSchema checks are accepted. Regex uses invariant matching, optional ignore-case, `shouldMatch` and a one-second timeout. JsonSchema uses `{"kind":"jsonSchema","schema":{...}}`; supported keywords are `type` (including nonempty supported unions), `properties`, `required`, `items`, `enum` and boolean `additionalProperties`. Unknown keywords, references, unsupported types and malformed schemas are rejected before spending. Supported `integer` means lexical signed Int64: `1.0`, `1e0` and integers outside Int64 fail that check. Numeric assertion keywords such as `minimum` are unsupported and rejected, never ignored. Valid JSON that fails a valid schema, or non-JSON output under that schema, is a valid failed assertion. Evaluator failure/timeout is invalid evidence. These stricter preflight rules apply to this memory capability; the ordinary ephemeral Trial contract above is unchanged.

Policy-v1 bounds are code-owned: raw strict UTF-8 JSON at most **128 KiB** (no charset transcoding; unknown members are rejected throughout the closed DTO tree); system/user prompt **16/32 KiB**; **1–16** distinct targets; **128** manifest coordinates; selected rendered memory **32 KiB**; complete provider prompt **96 KiB**; **1–16** checks with **16 KiB** total specifications, **1 KiB** per regex and schema nesting **16**; **3–16** pairs (default **5**); **1–4,096** output tokens (default **512**). Each provider response is bounded to **1 MiB before SDK parsing** and retained text to **64 KiB**. Complete evidence metadata is bounded to **64 KiB**; installation quota is **1,024** retained registrations, with **one active evaluation**. Stricter context/financial/transport limits still apply. There are no automatic retries, fallback models, tools, retrieval/cache injection, feedback between arms, selective reruns or early success. Even zero-based pairs send Control then Treatment; odd pairs reverse them.

Every success response has `evaluationId`, coordinator `operationId` (which may differ), `state`, `reason`, nullable `report` and exact nullable retained `reportDigest`. The report contains registration/ownership and frozen digests, exact content-free source/dependency coordinates, terminal UTC/state/decision, per-check 2×2 pass/fail counts, pair counts, response-evidence digest, nullable input/output token totals and decimal cost, and elapsed milliseconds. It contains no workload, answers/reasoning, memory, regex/schema/literal/label, URL, credential or raw error. Missing full usage/cost remains null, never a fabricated zero. `reportDigest` is null until terminal; terminal run/get/replay returns the stored digest object unchanged. For example, a terminal digest field has the shape `"reportDigest":{"bytes":"AAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAA="}`; its actual bytes depend on the retained report.

**200** means terminal metadata is available, including a terminal execution failure. `Completed` means the request-run terminalized; it can carry `InsufficientEvidence` for controlled source/configuration/authority/provider/output/usage/accounting/evaluator failure. `Outcome`, `Reason` and counts determine validity. Explicit cancellation uses `Cancelled`; crash or abrupt proof loss uses `Interrupted`. Benefit requires every requested valid pair to improve at least one check with no regressions; Harm requires every pair to regress without gains; NoMeasurableEffect requires every check equivalent in every pair. Mixed or incomplete evidence is InsufficientEvidence. A gain never hides another check's regression. These are descriptive results for the selected plan, workload/model/checks and finite samples, with no significance, independence, universal usefulness or per-member causal claim; fixed seeds may correlate samples. Reports grant no mutation/ranking/review authority.

`get` accepts `{"evaluationId":"..."}`. It first reads metadata ownership, canonically resolves that scope and retains a real current read lease through serialization, without source hydration or provider work. Both status get and identical run replay return **202** while in flight and **200** when terminal. Same-ID identical requests have no new hydration, sends or repeat charges; any changed canonical request conflicts. Replay does not assert that historical sources remain current.

`delete` accepts an object-shaped digest, for example `{"evaluationId":"11111111-1111-1111-1111-111111111111","expectedReportDigest":{"bytes":"AAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAA="},"confirm":true}` (illustrative identities/bytes). Copy the complete returned `reportDigest` object unchanged into `expectedReportDigest`; do not convert it to hex, stringify the object or recompute the canonical report hash. It deletes the complete exact terminal report/registration/manifest atomically, releases quota, and leaves source memory and independent disclosure/accounting history intact. Running cleanup or a mismatched digest conflicts; `confirm` must be true. A retained exact evaluation identity prevents re-execution after cleanup: get/run returns **410**. Repeating confirmed cleanup for that retained identity returns `{"evaluationId":"...","deleted":false}`. Unknown or other-kind identities return **404**. Erasure removes old ownership too; absence is classified under genuine current Global read authority without reconstructing any former Campaign/root or revalidating an erased digest. No list/export/UI/CLI/MCP surface or new configuration key is added.

| Refusal | HTTP |
| --- | --- |
| Malformed/unknown-member/UTF-8/raw-size/policy/bounds/confirmation/metadata | 400 |
| Missing master authentication / missing clean read authority | Existing 401 / 403 (unavailable authority uses existing 503) |
| Unknown or other-kind identity | 404 |
| Changed request, stale source/authority/scope, busy, exact digest conflict, quota | 409 |
| Retained exact evaluation identity with erased report | 410 |
| Required schema/provider capability unavailable | 503 |

Kestrel transport faults retain the existing sanitized 400/408/413/431 mapping. Quota refusal names confirmed report-only cleanup and spends nothing. Controlled failures after registration normally settle terminal **200 InsufficientEvidence**; abort can prevent delivery, after which the report is read separately. Provider configuration A→B→A still invalidates the frozen experiment.

Execution holds request authority only while its synchronous request lives. Operation cancellation is checked independently each second; successful heartbeat renewal cannot override Cancelling. Recovery kind `memory-evaluation` is AbandonSafely, never provider resume/retry. Checkpoint version 0 permits only the empty creation gap (payload/reference null); new version 1 carries exact IDs/digests, bounded PairCount and numeric progress. Only this kind's Pending attempt-zero creations enter recovery discovery after a **two-minute initial-start grace**; normal live-lease/process-ownership CAS still prevents theft. Recovery may preserve terminal evidence or append interrupted insufficient metadata, never recreate erased evidence or acquire source authority.

Accounting records measured usage through normal bills. Unknown/ambiguous usage keeps estimated admission pressure outstanding. The durable UncertainPaid marker precedes final authority/source/disclosure/configuration fences; a later refusal deliberately retains the estimate even if it proves no HTTP began, potentially indefinitely, and creates no numeric bill. Report cleanup does not release those independent holds. No manual release/reconciliation facility is introduced by this API.

### 8.22 Metrics endpoint (`GET /metrics`)

Prometheus text `0.0.4` via `System.Diagnostics.Metrics` + hand-rolled exporter (no OTel/prometheus-net — AOT). Catalog: HTTP requests, inference duration/tokens, tool outcomes, SSE gauge, active sessions (scrape-time query), Sanctum breaches, plus `arcanum_estimated_input_tokens`, `arcanum_provider_reported_input_tokens`, absolute `arcanum_input_token_estimation_variance` (low-cardinality `direction=underestimated|overestimated|exact|inconsistent`), and `arcanum_context_budget_rejections_total`. `arcanum_tool_invocations_total` has the closed `outcome=success|denied|error` domain (Sanctum and other live-policy refusals are `denied`) and uses the invocation's canonical `tool_name`; a model-supplied name that was never registered collapses to `unregistered`, so arbitrary model text cannot create a metric label. `arcanum_ward_decisions_total` applies the same `tool_name` rule and counts Ward outcomes with the closed `origin=human|auto_approved|auto_denied|timed_out|cancelled|host_restarted|ungated` domain; current server tool calls use `ungated`, while the other values remain compatibility vocabulary. Resolution reason text, tool arguments, and paths are never labels. `arcanum_apply_patch_artifact_cleanup_total` is count-only with closed `outcome=complete|retained`; it never labels paths, sessions, or receipt IDs. The Long Rest meters (`arcanum.long_rest.sweep.parked` with the closed `class=resume_later|restart_generation|needs_operator` label, `.sweep.readmitted`, `.sweep.budget_exhausted`, `.sweep.skipped_candidates` with the closed `reason=oversize|unpaired|incohesive|pack_limit` label, `.sweep.reused_decisions`, `.clean_schedule_refused` with the closed `LongRest.*` `code` label, `.summary.embedding_window_refused`, `.hierarchy.oversize_children`, `.discovery.lexical_verifications`, `.discovery.pairing_floor` with the closed `floor_le_permille=600|700|800|900|920|950|980|1000` label (one count per anchor with nominated neighbours, in the bucket of its Background Floor), and the histograms `.discovery.selected_summaries`, `.discovery.frontier` and `.discovery.expanded_leaves`) are listed in DESIGN §10.8.3; none carries an identity or content. Token histograms use token-scale buckets rather than duration buckets; provider/model labels remain low-cardinality (+ runtime meters via `MeterListener`). Path outside `/api`/`/v1`, which is also what keeps it outside Grimoire request admission (§8.31): admission is selected by whole-path-segment prefix, never by whether a route carries API-key metadata, so an authenticated `/metrics` bypasses it while `/apiary` and `/v10` are never swept into it. A scrape during a maintenance window still queries active sessions and is refused by the database itself, which reaches the caller as the same sanitized **503**. `Arcanum:Features:Metrics=false` → **404**. `Arcanum:Security:MetricsRequireApiKey` defaults true and is forced true on ListenAny. Auth: `X-Arcanum-Key` or Bearer.

### 8.23 Error code catalog and HTTP status mapping

Wire-stable codes live on `ErrorCodes` (Core). General HTTP mapping authority is `ArcanumErrorMapper.ResolveStatusCode` (Api); the data-lifecycle family has the route-local mapping defined in §8.20. `ResolveStatusCodeDefaultBadRequest` treats unmapped codes as **400** on Apprentice/Campaign/Spell/Prompt/ProvingGrounds routes while still honoring explicit **500** mappings (`ProvingGrounds.InferenceFailed`, `Workspace.WriteFailed`, `Workspace.DeleteFailed`, `Spell.WriteFailed`, `Saga.WriteFailed`, `Lexicon.WriteFailed`, `Covenant.WriteFailed`, `Saga.SearchFailed`, `MemoryErasure.ErasureIncomplete`, `Hub.InvalidAdmission`, `LongRest.InvalidAdmission`, `Hub.Error`). Unrecognized strings (including `Hub.Error` via default arm) → **500**. Keep in sync with `ErrorCodes.cs`, `ArcanumErrorMapper.cs`, and the route-local endpoint mappers and tests. `ApiErrorCatalogDocumentationTests` fails when a code the mapper arms has no row below, or has one under a different status, so the catalog is complete for every arm in `ResolveStatusCode`; it also lists codes the mapper does not arm, which are documented where their route is. It also fails when the explicit **500** codes listed in this paragraph are not exactly the codes `ResolveStatusCodeDefaultBadRequest` keeps at **500**.

**Default / unmapped:** unlisted codes → **500**; `ResolveStatusCodeDefaultBadRequest` downgrades unmapped → **400** except the explicit **500** set above. `WebResearch.InternalError` is deliberately absent from the table: it is reported inside a structured native web-tool result, never as an HTTP status.

**/api vs /v1:** native `/api` uses `ApiResponse<T>` + codes below. OpenAI `/v1` uses the OpenAI error envelope (`message`/`type`/`code`/`param`); hub failures map similarly (e.g. timeout → **503** `server_error`; unknown model → **404** `model_not_found`); a maintenance refusal is **503** `service_unavailable` with code `grimoire_maintenance` (§8.31). Client-tool forwarding surfaces OpenAI codes `unsupported_parameter` / `too_many_tools` / `invalid_schema` while Core codes remain `ClientTools.*`.

The same Core `ErrorCodes.MemoryEvaluation` family owns internal adapter and ledger codes: `MemoryEvaluation.InvalidAdmission` is an executor refusal consumed by the provider as `MemoryEvaluation.ProviderFailure`; `MemoryEvaluation.AmbiguousAccounting` is a recovery refusal. Closed report/ledger reasons additionally include `MemoryEvaluation.MixedOutcomes`, `MemoryEvaluation.Incomplete`, `MemoryEvaluation.InvalidOutput`, `MemoryEvaluation.ModelMismatch`, `MemoryEvaluation.UsageFailure`, `MemoryEvaluation.AccountingFailure`, and `MemoryEvaluation.Erased`. These are not additional HTTP status mappings. A missing failure reason normalizes to `Interrupted` for both the retained incomplete report and operation ledger; successful reports retain `None`.

| Codes (grouped) | HTTP | Semantics |
|-----------------|------|-----------|
| `MemoryDelegation.InvalidGrant` | 400 | Malformed or invalid exact selection, body, scope, purpose, coordinate or bound (§8.10.4). |
| `MemoryDelegation.StaleSource` | 409 | Changed, consumed, expired or revoked proof/authority; process quota, duplicate root or closed mutation admission. |
| `MemoryDelegation.Unavailable` | 503 | Native delegation composition or eligible effective model candidate unavailable, or client forwarding excludes native execution. |
| `MemoryEvaluation.InvalidRequest`; `MemoryEvaluation.InvalidScope`; `MemoryEvaluation.InvalidPrompt`; `MemoryEvaluation.InvalidEvaluation`; `MemoryEvaluation.InvalidMetadata` | 400 | Exact evaluation input, policy or bounded metadata refusal (§8.21). |
| `MemoryEvaluation.NotFound` | 404 | Unknown evaluation evidence/identity, including another operation kind. |
| `MemoryEvaluation.Gone` | 410 | The exact retained evaluation request identity remains after evidence erasure. |
| `MemoryEvaluation.Busy`; `MemoryEvaluation.StaleSource`; `MemoryEvaluation.StaleAuthority`; `MemoryEvaluation.ScopeMismatch`; `MemoryEvaluation.QuotaExceeded`; `MemoryEvaluation.StaleConfiguration`; `MemoryEvaluation.Cancelled`; `MemoryEvaluation.Interrupted`; `MemoryEvaluation.NotPrepared`; `MemoryEvaluation.ProviderFailure` | 409 | Admission/state/ownership conflict before a usable terminal response; quota requires confirmed report-only cleanup. Registered controlled execution failures normally return terminal 200 InsufficientEvidence. |
| `MemoryEvaluation.Unavailable`; `MemoryEvaluation.ProviderUnavailable`; `MemoryEvaluation.InvalidProviderPlan` | 503 | Required metadata/schema or frozen exact provider capability is unavailable. |
| `Validation.InvalidUrl`; `Provider.DnsFailed` | 400 | Provider-test URL syntax or DNS failure, respectively; route-local status with sanitized reason and remedy. Outbound address-policy refusal retains `Security.BlockedOutboundUrl`. |
| `Validation.InvalidPrompt`, `Validation.InvalidBody`, `Validation.InvalidQuery`, `Validation.InvalidProviderType`, `Validation.AttachedFiles`, `Validation.InvalidFields` | 400 | Request shape / bounds validation. `Validation.InvalidFields` is the arm for a well-formed body whose field values are not usable — an empty daemon title or body, or an expected content hash that is not a 64-character hexadecimal digest |
| `Validation.UnsupportedMediaType` | 415 | Missing or non-JSON `Content-Type` on a JSON body route, or a `Content-Type` a bound route does not accept (status set by `ApiRequestJson`, or by the host's exception handler and status-code hook for bound-body routes, not the mapper; §8.1) |
| `Hub.Model` | 404 | Model not in any provider `models` |
| `Hub.Error` | 500 | Generic inference failure (mapper default arm) |
| `Campaign.NotFound`; `Session.NotFound` / `EntryNotFound` / `PinNotFound`; `Attachment.NotFound` / `SourceNotFound`; `Grimoire.LoreNotFound`; `Apprentice.NotFound`; `Workspace.NotFound` / `FileNotFound`; `Spell.NotFound`; `Prompt.NotFound`; `Intelligence.HumanPromptNotFound`; `Mcp.ServerNotFound` / `ToolNotFound`; `Daemon.NotFound`; `Files.NotFound`; `Batches.NotFound` / `InputFileNotFound`; `Saga.NotFound`; `ProvingGrounds.SpellNotFound` / `PromptNotFound`; `Workspace.ReplacementNotFound`; `Execution.NotFound`; `Operation.NotFound`; `Provider.NotFound`; `Lexicon.NotFound` | 404 | Missing resource. `Lexicon.NotFound` is the exact subject of a curation or review request (§8.33) |
| `Mcp.DiagnosticDisabled` | 404 | The diagnostic MCP invocation route exists only on the Development edition (status set at the route; the `POST /api/mcp/tools/invoke` row in §1) |
| `Campaign.InvalidPath` / `MaxReached` / `DuplicateName` / `DuplicatePath`; `Session.Archived` / `InvalidStatus` / `InvalidFormat` / `TooManyEntries` / `EntryTooLarge` / `MemoryManagementDisabled` / `EmptyContent`; `Attachment.InvalidRequest` / `InvalidContent` / `InvalidReference` / `SourceUnavailable`; `Apprentice.Disabled` / `PendingQueueFull` / `InvalidGuidance` / `InvalidPlan` / `InvalidGoal` / `InvalidWorkspace`; `Workspace.NameEmpty` / `SymbolicLinkEscape` / `PathTraversal` / `DirectoryNotEmpty` / `ReplacementAmbiguous` / `PathIsDirectory` / `PathIsFile`; `Spell.NoWorkspace` / `InvalidWorkspace` / `InvalidName` / `NameCollision` / `BuiltinReadOnly` / `DuplicateVersion` / `InvalidVersion`; `Prompt.CodexPathNotContained` / `CodexPathRequired` / `CodexPathInvalid` / `CodexPathNotFound` / `CodexReadFailed` / `CodexPathTooLarge` / `DuplicateVersion` / `InvalidName` / `InvalidVersion` / `InvalidRequest`; `Mcp.AmbiguousServer` / `MissingWorkspace` / `ServerNotRunning` / `AmbiguousTool` / `ToolError`; `Sending.TaskRejected` / `ModalityMismatch` / `SkillNotAdvertised` / `PushNotificationRejected` / `PushNotificationsDisabled`; `Security.BlockedOutboundUrl` / `IdempotencyKeyTooLong`; `Files.InvalidMimeType`; `Batches.InvalidEndpoint`; `Embeddings.ConfirmationRequired`; `ProvingGrounds.InvalidTrial` / `WorkspaceNotAllowed`; `Saga.NotEmpty`; `Scrying.VisionNotSupported` / `TooManyImages` / `UnsupportedMimeType` / `InvalidImageData`; `WebBrowsing.TooLarge` (emitted in the `browse_web` tool result when a page nests HTML past the extraction ceiling; oversized bodies still truncate) / `InvalidUrl` / `RedirectLimitExceeded` (emitted in the `browse_web` tool result when a page exceeds the redirect limit or enters a redirect cycle); `ClientTools.Disabled` / `TooMany` / `InvalidSchema` / `ModelUnsupported` / `ToolChoiceUnavailable`; `Guardrails.PiiDetected` / `Blocked`; `StructuredOutput.ValidationFailed` / `SchemaInvalid`; `Operation.InvalidState`; `Perception.InvalidPath` / `InvalidSnapshot`; `Codex.PathNotContained` / `ContentTooLarge`; `Validation.InvalidLore`; `Validation.InvalidKey` | 400 | Domain validation / policy refusal (non-auth) |
| `Sanctum.InvalidConfig` | 400 | A Sanctum update (`PUT /api/campaigns/{campaignId}/sanctum`) whose `networkPolicy` is `AllowList` with no `allowedDomains`, or one of whose `allowedPaths` entries is not an absolute path (status set at the route) |
| `Campaign.PathNotAllowed`; `Workspace.PathNotAllowed` / `AccessDenied` / `FileWriteDisabled`; `Spell.PathNotAllowed`; `Sending.Disabled` / `AgentNotAllowed`; `Mcp.WorkspaceNotTrusted` / `DiagnosticBlocked`; `Scrying.FeatureDisabled`; `Attachment.Disabled`; `WebBrowsing.SsrfBlocked`; `Perception.PathNotAllowed` | 403 | Path/network/feature deny |
| `Auth.Unauthorized` | 401 | Missing/invalid/ambiguous/oversized API key. **The only 401 the server emits** — written directly by `ApiKeyEndpointFilter` (§11.3), not by `ArcanumErrorMapper`. Clients must switch on this code. Under `/v1` the same refusal is the OpenAI error shape instead (`error.type` `invalid_request_error`, `error.code` `invalid_api_key`, `error.param` null), like every other failure on that surface |
| `Security.MissingApiKey` | 401 | **Client-synthesized only.** The CLI and The Forge produce this locally when no API key is configured, so no request is sent; the server never returns it. The mapper still resolves it to 401 for a `Result` that carries it |
| `Security.CredentialUnreadable` | 401 | **Client-synthesized only.** Secure storage reported that the local API credential exists but is unreadable or corrupted; no authenticated request is sent |
| `Security.UnverifiedLocalApi` | 401 | **Client-synthesized only.** The configured local endpoint answered but failed the nonce-and-authority-bound Arcanum presence proof; no reusable credential is disclosed and no authenticated request is sent |
| `Data.InvalidRequest` / `ConfirmationRequired` | 400 | Invalid data-lifecycle operation, rule, scope, or required factory-reset confirmation |
| `Data.NotFound` | 404 | Explicit data-lifecycle target not found |
| `Data.PlanChanged` / `Blocked` / `Conflict` / `OperationNotFinalized` | 409 | Stale preview, retained dependency/hold, or active-work conflict. `OperationNotFinalized` is the one retention ending where an unconditional retry is safe: the data change applied, nothing is left on disk, and only the durable operation row could not be transitioned |
| `Saga.StaleContent`; `Saga.AlreadyRetired` | 409 | Saga curation state refusals (§8.30). `Saga.StaleContent` says the stored content moved out from under the caller's view of it; `Saga.AlreadyRetired` is emitted by correction alone and says the memory must be reinstated before it can be corrected. Retiring a memory that is already retired and reinstating one that is not retired are **not** refusals and have no code: each answers **200** carrying the outcome kind that says so |
| `LongRest.InvalidRequest` | 400 | Invalid exact declaration, cardinality, content binding, or survivor selection (§8.36) |
| `LongRest.NotFound` | 404 | A receipt does not exist (§8.36) |
| `LongRest.StaleInput`; `LongRest.MissingClaim`; `LongRest.SweepParked`; `LongRest.OperationBusy`; `LongRest.ModelContextTooSmall` | 409 | The inspected exact head/content moved, or a target is missing or has no Annals claim; no history is invented. `LongRest.SweepParked`: the active sweep for the requested scope is parked in a way only an operator can clear; the message names that operation's identifier. `LongRest.OperationBusy`: another executor in this process owns the operation right now; a request that would have to replace or retire that sweep (or a Session resume of it) wrote nothing, and the owner is already advancing it. On the sweep route `LongRest.StaleInput` also means the request could not prove its exact current admission. `LongRest.ModelContextTooSmall`: the configured model's context window cannot take a Long Rest unit (an admitted request or embedding input larger than the provider's window; a summary request states the byte bound the embedding window allows, so an embedding input over the window means the model ignored it). It is a sweep park code that needs an operator, raised before any provider-effect row or accounting run exists for the refused unit; the message names the remedy (raise the provider's `contextWindowLimit` or choose a larger model) and never carries content. A memory too large to be summarized within the request budget is not refused: the sweep leaves it unsummarized and goes on (§8.36) |
| `LongRest.Unavailable`; `LongRest.IntegrityFailure`; `LongRest.ProviderUnavailable`; `LongRest.ProviderUnreachable` | 503 | The transactional substrate or exact protection/history evidence cannot be proved. With Long Rest enabled but its recipe unresolved, a sweep request, and so the Session turn that made it for a protected partition, ends with this code and a message that starts `LongRest: enabled but recipe unresolved:` and names the unresolved part, the one content-free sentence the configuration validator, `arcanum doctor` and `GET /api/health` give (it carries no more provider identity than they do); a disabled feature keeps the generic message. `LongRest.ProviderUnavailable` and `LongRest.ProviderUnreachable` are sweep park codes (a transient provider failure, or a provider that could not be reached before any request byte left the process); they appear as an operation's terminal code, never with a provider response body (§8.36) |
| `LongRest.CredentialUnavailable` | 409 | Local provider credential resolution failed before dispatch. Check the configured credential reference and credential store access; this is an operator configuration blocker, not a network failure. |
| `LongRest.ProviderRejected`; `LongRest.TransportUnknown` | 502 | Sweep park codes that need an operator: the provider rejected a Long Rest call with a non-transient client error, or a work unit has ended its allowed two physical attempts with unknown billing. Neither carries a provider response body (§8.36). Provider failure messages retain a known structured HTTP status and fixed configuration guidance; unknown status stays unknown. Warning logs correlate the configured provider/model and operation ID without response or exception text |
| `Embeddings.DimensionMismatch` | 409 | A semantic search met stored embeddings of another width than the query's and could not score a single row, so the corpus was embedded at a different `Arcanum:Integrations:Embeddings:Dimensions` than the one now configured. The operator resets the embedding tables (`POST /api/embeddings/reset?confirm=true`) and re-indexes (DESIGN §21.2). A corpus where only some rows are of another width, or are unreadable or zero vectors, still answers **200**: those rows are skipped and logged once per scan |
| `Session.CompactionUnavailable`; `Session.TooManyPinned` / `ForkDepthExceeded` / `ForkChanged`; `Attachment.LimitExceeded`; `Apprentice.AlreadyRunning` / `Running` / `NotPaused` / `CannotReweave` / `NotEscalated` / `MaxReached` / `ConclaveDisabled`; `Security.IdempotencyConflict`; `Security.IdempotencyInProgress`; `Ward.AlreadyResolved`; `Operation.StateConflict` | 409 | State or idempotency conflict |
| `Session.PinUnavailable` | 503 | The host cannot apply the pin cap and mutation atomically; no mutation is attempted. |
| `Workspace.FileChanged` | 409 | A read-modify-write `PATCH .../files/contents` found its target no longer holding the bytes the edit was computed from (an edit by someone else, a replaced or deleted file), so nothing was written and the other edit is intact. The caller re-reads and retries; this is not a server fault, so it is not `Workspace.WriteFailed` |
| `Sending.MaxTasksReached`; `RateLimit.TooManyRequests` | 429 | Defensive compatibility/custom-provider mapping or explicit rate limit; the built-in outbound A2A client queues cancellably instead of emitting `Sending.MaxTasksReached` when its concurrency slots are occupied |
| `Workspace.FileTooLarge`; `Files.TooLarge`; `Scrying.ImageTooLarge`; `Attachment.TooLarge`; `Validation.BodyTooLarge` | 413 | Payload too large. `Validation.BodyTooLarge` is written by `ApiRequestJson` (or, for a bound-body route, by the host's status-code hook that puts the envelope on the framework's empty 413, §8.1) for a body larger than this server accepts, and is distinct from `Validation.InvalidBody` because a malformed body is worth resending corrected and an oversized one is not worth resending at all |
| `Validation.BodyReadTimeout` | 408 | Kestrel enforces a minimum request-body data rate and stops waiting for a body that falls under it. Not a **400**: nothing is wrong with the body, and it is worth resending unchanged on a better connection |
| `Validation.RequestHeadersTooLarge` | 431 | The request's headers or trailers exceed the total size this server accepts. Reachable while reading a chunked body, because trailers arrive after it and count against the same ceiling |
| `Sending.AgentUnreachable` / `AgentCardInvalid`; `CommLink.Suppressed` / `WebhookHttpError` / `WebhookException` / `DispatcherException` | 502 | Downstream / webhook failure. The three `CommLink` failure codes are set at `POST /api/commlink/send` (every dispatch failure answers **502**, not through the mapper's default): `WebhookHttpError` is a non-success status from the receiver, `WebhookException` a POST that failed before a status was read, `DispatcherException` a sink that threw unexpectedly. Each carries a fixed message with no URL, status body or exception text |
| `Api.TooManyConnections`; `Connection.Unreachable`; `Embeddings.ProviderUnavailable` / `FeatureDisabled`; `Session.RestQueueFull`; `Saga.EmbeddingUnavailable`; `Workspace.IndexingUnavailable`; `Grimoire.MaintenanceUnavailable` / `WorkDrainTimeout` | 503 | Capacity / provider unavailable, or bounded Campaign Logger queue rejection. `Workspace.IndexingUnavailable` refuses queued reconciliation while workspace indexing is stopping or has not started, and is worth retrying once the host is serving again. `Saga.EmbeddingUnavailable` refuses a curation write that could not produce a vector before it writes anything, because the operator's action is the same as for a provider outage and the request itself was not wrong (§8.30). `Grimoire.MaintenanceUnavailable` is a database transformation holding connection admission on purpose: the window is temporary, nothing is wrong with the request, and the caller is being asked to retry (§8.31). `Grimoire.WorkDrainTimeout` answers the operator who asked for that transformation when ordinary work would not drain in time to start it: nothing was erased, ordinary admission is open again, and asking once the database is quieter is the action |
| `Mcp.DiagnosticTimeout`; `Connection.Timeout`; `WebBrowsing.Timeout` | 504 | Bounded downstream transport/diagnostic operation timeout |
| `Workspace.WriteFailed` / `DeleteFailed`; `Spell.WriteFailed`; `Saga.WriteFailed`; `Lexicon.WriteFailed`; `Covenant.WriteFailed`; `ProvingGrounds.InferenceFailed`; `Saga.SearchFailed` | 500 | Explicit infra/search failures (never downgraded by DefaultBadRequest). `Lexicon.WriteFailed` is a Lexicon curation or memory-review write that could not commit; nothing is partially published (§8.33, §8.34). `Saga.WriteFailed` and `Covenant.WriteFailed` are what a memory-review `apply` answers when its transaction could not commit or a storage read before it failed; nothing was written (§8.34). A Covenant database that stayed busy past the bounded retry is not one of them: it is the retryable 503 `Covenant.Unavailable` |
| `Data.ReconciliationFailed` | 500 | Data-lifecycle apply failed or post-delete reconciliation requires operator review |
| `Data.QuarantineRecoveryRequired` | 500 | The database mutation committed and quarantined bytes still need finalizing. Not "nothing happened": the rows are gone, so durable recovery or an operator finishes the cleanup rather than the caller retrying blind |
| `Hub.Unhandled` | 500 | An exception escaped every endpoint and `ArcanumExceptionHandler` wrote the envelope |
| `Validation.SpellOverride` | 500 | `OverrideSpellName` matched no spell. Client-side input, but the mapper has no entry for it, so the default arm still answers **500** |
