| `Covenant.InvalidScope` / `InvalidKey` / `InvalidContent` / `InvalidCursor` | 400 | Covenant request shape, key grammar, content bounds, or an opaque cursor that failed bounds or binding validation (§8.28) |
| `Covenant.ForbiddenAuthority`; `Covenant.SensitiveEgressRequiresApproval`; `Covenant.PlaintextExportRefused` | 403 | The caller holds no authority for this effect, historical compatibility vocabulary names a missing sensitive-egress approval (no live retirement emits it), or a plaintext export of a Session that carries Covenant-derived content. The export refusal has no approval arm and no retry that succeeds: a plaintext file is nonrevocable the moment it exists |
| `Covenant.NotFound` | 404 | No such scope, key, version, or lane head. Also returned for an artifact owned by another Session, so a distinct code cannot confirm the identity exists, and for an exclusive operation naming a Campaign this installation never registered — which may be a typo or a stale client |
| `Covenant.ArtifactErased` | 410 | The durable receipt proves this existed and was securely erased; the result can be reported but never returned |
| `Covenant.RevisionConflict` / `LifecycleConflict` / `StaleSnapshot` / `StaleCursor` / `CapacityExceeded` / `SensitiveHistoryRequiresContext` / `CampaignBindingConflict`; `Session.CampaignBindingRequired`; `Campaign.PathIdentityRequired`; `Hub.SessionTurnBusy` / `SessionHistoryChanged` / `SessionTurnRestoredInterrupted` | 409 | Optimistic-concurrency, lifecycle, epoch, capacity, binding, path-identity, and Session-turn conflicts. An exclusive operation naming a Campaign the deletion journal has an event for answers `LifecycleConflict` here rather than `Covenant.NotFound`: it was really deleted, and collapsing that into the never-registered case would make the distinction unobservable to every caller. `StaleCursor` is separate from `InvalidCursor`: it authenticated here and its dataset moved on, while an invalid one cannot be trusted to say which query it belonged to. List, Versions and free-text query cursors are all checked against the dataset generation and canonical sequence the continuing page was read under (List and query cursors also against the Campaign deletion sequence; a Versions cursor carries that field as the constant 0, because an entry's version history is not scoped by a Campaign deletion), so a cursor presented after any Covenant write is `StaleCursor` and a first page (no cursor) is never refused for staleness; a cursor sealed under a different envelope key version is `InvalidCursor` |
| `Hub.ContextBudgetExceeded` | 400 | The materialized context exceeds the configured provider window. The message names the provider/model, input/reserved/total token counts, window, and remedies: shorten the prompt, compact or start a Session, select a larger-context model, or correct `providers[].contextWindowLimit` to its actual capacity. `/v1/chat/completions` returns `invalid_request_error` / `context_length_exceeded`; an already-started SSE stream carries those fields in its error chunk and remains HTTP 200 |
| `Hub.InvalidAdmission`; `LongRest.InvalidAdmission` | 500 | Internal provider payload, cache plan/proof, context breakdown, or exact Long Rest admission is invalid or stale; no provider dispatch proceeds under that admission. These are not context-window overflows and `/v1` keeps its sanitized generic inference-failure message |
| `Budget.Exceeded` | 429 | Spend and outstanding reservations meet or would exceed the enforced `Arcanum:Cost:Budget:DailyLimitUsd`. A new UTC accounting day starts at 00:00 UTC; outstanding reservations from prior days can still count, so midnight does not guarantee admission. The message names the setting to review. `/v1/chat/completions` uses `insufficient_quota` for both error type and code; a started SSE stream reports the same error with HTTP 200. On a native stream it arrives as the `error` frame's `data` (§8.5) |
| `Covenant.Unavailable` / `OperatorAuthorityUnavailable` / `HostToolsTransitionRequired` / `MaintenanceFailed` / `ManualArtifactErasureRequired` / `ManualRecoveryRequired` / `ErasureIncomplete` / `IntegrityFailure` | 503 | A Covenant tier, authority, maintenance step, erasure, or integrity contract is not currently able to serve. Each names an operator action rather than a retry, except `Unavailable` from a memory-review `apply` whose database stayed busy past the bounded retry, which says to retry the same request (§8.34) |
| `Covenant.IneligibleTurn` | — | **MCP-only, deliberately unmapped.** No HTTP route can produce it: the operator API arrives with authenticated authority and never asks whether a turn carried a staging capability. Giving it an arm would invite a route to start returning it |
| `MemoryErasure.InvalidPreflight` | 400 | A selective-erase apply whose preflight token is missing, expired, unreadable, or bound to a different request. Prepare again; an erase that already committed replays from its receipt without a token |
| `MemoryErasure.KeyLost`; `MemoryErasure.StalePlan`; `Lexicon.SuppressedNameRefused`; `Lexicon.PinnedMutationRefused` | 409 | Selective-erasure and operator-management state refusals. `MemoryErasure.KeyLost` means erasure evidence exists that the current erasure key cannot verify, so nothing is decided against it until an operator resets the key; `MemoryErasure.StalePlan` means what the erase would remove changed after it was prepared. `Lexicon.SuppressedNameRefused` refuses an agent write to an entry the operator erased in that exact scope, and `Lexicon.PinnedMutationRefused` refuses an agent deletion of a pinned entry; operator writes are never refused by either |
| `MemoryErasure.SubjectErased` | 410 | A durable erasure receipt proves the named row existed and was erased, and no live row has its id. Reported instead of the store's not-found code, because the erase is a fact the caller can act on |
| `MemoryErasure.ErasureIncomplete` | 500 | The erase transaction could not prove every planned row absent, so it rolled back and recorded neither a receipt nor a fingerprint. Never downgraded by `ResolveStatusCodeDefaultBadRequest` |
| `MemoryErasure.Unavailable`; `MemoryErasure.KeyUnavailable` | 503 | Retryable. `MemoryErasure.Unavailable` means the schema the erasure routes need has not been reached yet; `MemoryErasure.KeyUnavailable` means secure storage could not supply the erasure key right now, and nothing was changed |
| `Validation.InvalidTemperature` / `InvalidTopP` / `InvalidPresencePenalty` / `InvalidFrequencyPenalty` / `InvalidMaxOutputTokens` / `InvalidResponseFormat` / `InvalidResponseSchema` | 400 | Explicit inference controls are invalid before inference: finite temperature 0–2, topP 0–1, presence/frequency penalties −2–2 and a positive output-token limit. The trimmed response format must be text, json_object or json_schema; json_schema requires a valid object schema in the supported subset. Native errors identify the native field; OpenAI live and batch validation identify the original wire parameter. No silent clamping or inference call occurs |
| `Validation.InvalidReasoningEffort` / `InvalidReasoningOutput` / `ReasoningEffortAndBudgetMutuallyExclusive` / `InvalidReasoningBudget` / `UnsupportedReasoningControl` / `ReasoningBudgetExceedsModelLimit` / `UnsupportedReasoningOutput` | 400 | Reasoning controls refused before any provider I/O. The first four are about the request itself: an effort or output mode outside its enumeration, an effort supplied together with a numeric budget, or a budget outside 1–2,097,152 tokens. The last three are about the resolved model: a control the model (or `Features.Reasoning`) does not support, a budget above the model's configured maximum, or a summary or full output mode on a model that declares no reasoning capability. Each candidate in a fallback chain is checked, so an explicit control is never silently dropped |
| `Lexicon.InvalidName` / `InvalidFact` / `InvalidScope` / `InvalidCurationTarget` / `InvalidReplacement`; `MemoryReview.InvalidToken` / `InvalidTokenFacts` | 400 | Lexicon curation request or target validation (§8.33), and a review token that is unreadable or whose bound facts do not match the request (§8.34) |
| `WebResearch.InvalidUrl` / `RequestRejected` | 400 | A URL read named something that is not an absolute HTTP or HTTPS address, or a redirect answered with an invalid one; or a search or read request was refused as invalid (an empty or over-long query, an unsupported model, invalid limits) or rejected by the upstream |
| `Lexicon.ProtectedMutationRefused`; `WebResearch.SsrfBlocked` | 403 | A Lexicon correction, lifecycle change, or review action on a protected entry that arrived without the exact-scope write capability it needs (§8.33); a research destination or redirect hop that the outbound URL guard refused, or a redirect hop that the Campaign Sanctum network policy refused (the Sanctum refuses an outside redirect hop in every mode and records the `NetworkEgress` breach, and a breach that cannot be recorded, or a Campaign whose Sanctum cannot be read, is refused whatever the mode; the outbound URL guard refuses in both modes too). It is a `read_url` and research tool-result code and the code `/api/web/browse` answers with for the guard; the legacy `browse_web` alias reports the same hop and guard refusals as `WebBrowsing.SsrfBlocked`. A Sanctum refusal of a tool call's initial target is the Sanctum Guard denial text (breach type `NetworkEgress`), not this code, and an AuditOnly Sanctum lets that one target go on once the breach is recorded. In the research stream a refused citation, whether refused up front or at a redirect hop, is a `source_denied` progress frame and is dropped from the synthesis sources |
| `Lexicon.StaleCurationTarget` / `RetiredMutationRefused` / `CurationGenerationExhausted` / `ArtifactRevisionExhausted`; `MemoryReview.StaleObservation` / `UnseenObservation` / `RequestReuse` | 409 | Curation and review state refusals the caller re-reads and decides on again (§8.33, §8.34): a target that no longer matches the live row, a mutation of a retired entry, a counter that has no further value, an observation superseded or never shown, or a request identity reused for a different decision set |
| `WebResearch.ResponseTooLarge` | 413 | The page or search response exceeded the permitted size |
| `WebResearch.RateLimited` / `BudgetExceeded` | 429 | The search provider rate-limited the request, or research stopped because reported provider cost exceeded the request's limit |
| `Lexicon.CurationIntegrityFailed` / `SearchFailed`; `MemoryReview.IntegrityFailure` | 500 | Curation or review integrity and search failures; nothing is partially published (§8.33, §8.34). The Lexicon curation and memory review routes resolve through `ResolveStatusCode`, which is why these stay **500**; `ResolveStatusCodeDefaultBadRequest` does not protect them and would rewrite any of them to **400** |
| `Lexicon.CurationUnavailable`; `WebResearch.MissingCredential` / `AuthenticationOrCreditsFailed` / `QuotaExhausted` / `ProviderUnavailable` / `UnsupportedOperation` / `JavaScriptRenderingUnavailable` | 503 | `Lexicon.CurationUnavailable`: the committed Core schema is below the version curation needs, so retry after the transition completes (§8.33). The `WebResearch` codes name an operator or provider condition rather than a bad request: no credential configured, a credential or credits the provider refused, an exhausted quota, an unavailable provider or static reader, an operation the configured provider does not support, native web workflows that are switched off (`Arcanum:Features:WebBrowsing`; `UnsupportedOperation` is the code for each of those three), or JavaScript rendering that is not configured (retry with static rendering) |
| `WebResearch.Timeout` | 504 | The page or search connection or response stream exceeded its idle I/O timeout |

Embedding provider failures retain **503** `Embeddings.ProviderUnavailable`; upstream 401/403/404/413 do not become this API's authentication or payload status. Their bounded fixed messages distinguish credential refusal, permission refusal, missing model/endpoint and provider request-size rejection, with supported configuration checks and `arcanum doctor`. Other real HTTP statuses and unknown transport faults remain unavailable. No raw provider/exception/response text or identifier value reaches the public error. A local usage-ledger failure names local storage rather than blaming provider credentials. If a resolved known embedding identity changes before a later send, the call refuses before that send without replay/repricing; its fixed guidance states earlier batches may already be billed. Same-name endpoint/credential rotation is not an identity assurance; legacy unstamped leases remain explicitly unknown (DESIGN §21.1).

**Ollama:** providers use the `OpenAICompatible` contract and surface failures as `Hub.Error`. **Familiars:** `ClaudeCodeCli`/`CodexCli` providers surface a missing binary, a refused spawn, a deadline, or a non-zero exit as `Hub.Error` carrying the CLI's own message. `Provider.NotFound` maps to **404**.

### 8.24 OpenAI embeddings (`POST /v1/embeddings`)

An explicit `dimensions` must be a positive integer equal to the effective configured `Arcanum:Integrations:Embeddings:Dimensions` width. Zero/negative values return **400** `invalid_value`; a different width returns **400** `unsupported_parameter`, both with `param: "dimensions"` before embedding or chunking. Matching the configured width requests that exact returned width, without truncation: if the provider returns a different width, the response is **503** `embedding_provider_unavailable`. This applies to short and mean-pooled long inputs and to float/base64 encodings. Omission retains the provider's full vector width.

Composes `IWeaveService` + tokenizer. `model` must match `Arcanum:Integrations:Embeddings:Model` or be omitted → else **404** `model_not_found`. Long inputs use code-owned chunking + mean-pool/L2. `encoding_format` is `float|base64` (`EmbeddingBlobCodec`). Idempotency-Key is supported, and (like `/v1/chat/completions`) its fingerprint is the raw request bytes. Embedding provider calls have no Arcanum whole-operation deadline; request-abort/caller cancellation propagates directly, while provider failures are sanitized as **503** — including a provider answer whose vectors are not all the same width, which is refused rather than mean-pooled into a silently truncated embedding, and a chunker that answers no chunks for a long input, which is refused rather than failing as an unhandled 500. That width check is a property of the whole response rather than of one round trip: a request issues one provider call for its short inputs and one per long input, so the assembled `data[]` is checked for a single common width before it is written, and a pool that answers each call consistently but the request at two model widths is refused the same way. Errors use the OpenAI envelope (**400** invalid input/chars, **400** `invalid_json` for a body that does not parse, **415** `unsupported_media_type` for a non-JSON `Content-Type`; **503** when The Weave is unavailable). The body is read by the handler rather than bound as a route parameter, so a malformed body never produces a body-less framework 400/415.

### 8.25 HTTP response compression

Brotli+Gzip via ASP.NET ResponseCompression; early pipeline. Excludes `text/event-stream` and `application/x-ndjson`. `EnableForHttps` left false (framework default).

### 8.26 Persisted inference audit log

Opt-in JSONL (`Arcanum:Host:AuditLog:*`); dated files, owner-only, with a soft daily size cap. A row is written only after a turn completes successfully (ping / ping-stream / v1-completion today); errors, cancellations, and interrupted streams are not audit rows. The writer never deletes historical files: age-based removal runs only through the bounded, durable unified data-retention planner/service under `Arcanum:Retention:AuditLogs`. When an audit query omits `from`, it searches every dated file instead of applying a hidden lookback. `GET /api/audit` keeps the existing `ApiResponse<InferenceAuditRecord[]>` body and supports `from`, `to`, `model`, `sessionId`, and `limit` (default 100, maximum 1,000). Records are newest-first. When more retained matches may remain, the response includes `X-Arcanum-Next-Cursor`; repeat the unchanged query with that opaque value as `cursor`. The cursor binds the audit family, configured file family, all filters, the first-page snapshot time, dated-file prefix identity, and reverse byte boundary, so active appends cannot shift the traversal. A malformed, filter-mismatched, replaced, or retained-away cursor returns **400** `Validation.InvalidQuery` with an exact restart-without-cursor action; so does a `from` or `to` that is not a date/time, or a `from` after `to` (the same code `GET /api/guardrails/audit` returns, so the two routes' query contracts match). Readers reverse-scan fixed 64 KiB blocks and stream one JSON record segment at a time; neither the complete file nor skipped records are materialized as a string array. Tool names and counts are metadata; `Arcanum:Host:AuditLog:RedactToolArguments=true` (default) makes `toolArgumentsJson` null, while opting out records the exact raw argument snapshots at operator risk. Tool results, prompt/answer bodies, and reasoning bodies are never fields in this log. Audit failure is warning-only and never changes the already-successful turn. A dated file the reader discovers but cannot open or read — a mode/ACL change, a disk error, a lock held elsewhere — is skipped and the page still returns **200**, so the omission is reported as a **Warning** naming the audit family and the file path; a file that simply vanished between enumeration and open is the retention sweep doing its job and stays at Debug. The same handling applies to `GET /api/guardrails/audit` (§8.27), because both routes share `AuditLogPageReader`.

### 8.27 Content guardrails (PII / toxicity / topics)

Opt-in via `Arcanum:Features:Guardrails` (default false), with policy under `Arcanum:Security:Guardrails`. Input PII (GeneratedRegex plus Luhn for card candidates, each pattern with the same 500 ms match budget as the operator topic patterns) → `Guardrails.PiiDetected`; toxicity/topics → `Guardrails.Blocked`. A PII or blocked-topic pattern that exhausts its match budget is undetermined and fails closed with `Guardrails.Blocked` (audited as `pii-undetermined` / `topic-blocked`). The violation audit record is written to completion even when the request token is already cancelled, because the rejection is decided before the write. Streaming output filtering is code-owned **buffered** mode. Audit JSONL + `GET /api/guardrails/audit` keeps the existing `ApiResponse<GuardrailAuditRecord[]>` body and accepts `from`, `to`, `stage`, `violationType`, `sessionId`, `limit`, and optional `cursor`. Its default/max page, newest-first snapshot semantics, `X-Arcanum-Next-Cursor` continuation, bounded reverse scan, query binding, and **400** `Validation.InvalidQuery` restart contract match `/api/audit`. PII errors contain only a fixed detector category (`pii-email`, `pii-phone`, `pii-ssn`, or `pii-credit-card`) and a category-specific redaction remedy; PII audit records retain fixed masks, never matched content. Native and `/v1` buffered/streaming replies preserve this guidance; `/v1` accepts only the exact code-owned messages and uses a fixed content-free fallback for any other PII error text. Existing HTTP statuses/error codes are unchanged.

An input allowlist miss returns `Guardrails.Blocked` with fixed guidance that no allowed topic matched and names `security.guardrails.allowedTopics` as the policy to review. Configuration validation rejects malformed nonblank `allowedTopics` and `blockedTopics` entries at their exact array indexes, including when the feature is off. Diagnostics never repeat authored patterns or input content. Runtime fallback for invalid legacy patterns remains a skip with one safe warning per field/index and settings snapshot; regex timeouts still refuse and audit, without logging exception objects that retain private input or patterns.

Input violation audit records retain the requested Session id when supplied, so the existing session filter can find a rejected prompt. Their model field uses pure configuration resolution of the requested/default model; if unresolved, it retains the request value. This is preflight metadata, not evidence of a provider call or a later fallback model. A sessionless rejection does not create a Session. Guardrails still run before provider acquisition.

Input policy inspects the latest actual user-message group: locate the last user message, then select user messages back to the preceding assistant message, ignoring system/developer/tool text. Later assistant prefills or tool results do not erase that user group. Role comparison is case-insensitive; message content cannot forge a role boundary. A transcript with no user message has no input group to scan. Stateful turns scan the incoming prompt. Stateless native and `/v1` turns scan both fallback `content` and textual content parts; prior user/assistant/tool history is excluded. Output toxicity and blocked-topic checks still inspect completed generated output; PII is input-only. `security.guardrails.detectPii` remains the master switch, with default-true `detectEmail`, `detectPhone`, `detectSsn`, and `detectCreditCard` controls. Phones require formatted US 3-3-4 groups or an explicit `+` international number (8–15 digits); bare numeric runs do not establish a phone match. Cards require a whole 13–19 ASCII-digit candidate, optional spaces/hyphens, non-identical digits, and Luhn validation. Cards, phones, and SSNs use decimal-digit boundaries, including adjacent Unicode decimal digits. These bounded patterns do not establish identity or guarantee complete PII detection; a valid card-shaped ID can still match.

### 8.28 Covenant public contract (mutation and inspection routed)

The request and response shapes the dedicated Covenant management surfaces use are frozen, and the table below is the whole of them; which of those shapes has a route behind it is what this paragraph names, and no count of them is kept here. **Registered, mutation:** `POST /api/memory/covenant/set/prepare`, `POST /api/memory/covenant/retire/prepare`, `POST /api/memory/covenant/correct/prepare`, `POST /api/memory/covenant/curate/prepare`, `PUT /api/memory/covenant`, `POST /api/memory/covenant/retire`, `POST /api/memory/covenant/correct`, `POST /api/memory/covenant/curate`, the entry erase pair of §8.35, `POST /api/memory/covenant/erase/prepare` and `POST /api/memory/covenant/erase`, and the §8.35 release, `POST /api/memory/covenant/release`. **Registered, inspection:** `POST /api/memory/covenant/list`, `/query`, `/detail`, `/versions`, `/sources`, `/explain` — every one a `POST` with a typed body, so no scope selection, Campaign identity, key, query text, or cursor ever enters a URL. `/query` compiles operator text through the Covenant query compiler, uses the accelerator or its bounded canonical fallback, and returns the same content-free `CovenantPageDto` heads and authenticated continuation model as the other catalogs. Repair, rebuild, Campaign path, and Session binding remain unregistered. The activated `/api/data` Covenant reset/factory-erasure lifecycle in §8.20 registers none of them. This section remains documented because four surfaces build against these shapes, and a contract that four of them each invent separately is four contracts.

> **Boundary status.** The authenticated boundary these routes sit behind is in place — see [§8.29](#829-x-arcanum-context-policy-and-the-covenant-pre-binding-boundary) — and `MemoryStatusDto` has its content-free `covenant` block. The eight set, retire, correct and curate routes declare `CovenantManage` operator authority, and the two erase routes `LifecycleManage`, so the pre-binding middleware refuses an unauthorized request before a single body byte is bound; each of the eight hands its lease to `CovenantProtectedJsonResult<T>`, which revalidates before the first byte and emits the protected header tuple, and the erase pair follows §8.35. Every prepare route takes the **installation** read capability rather than a scoped lease: a Global mutation's effect reaches every Campaign, and making the capability depend on a field in the body would be conditional authority that ends up wrong in one branch. The six mapped inspection routes each sit behind Covenant read authority and hand their lease to the same protected result; the four §8.20 lifecycle routes already require `LifecycleManage`. The maintenance surface is the one that is still absent: `ICovenantMaintenanceService` is registered in no container and reached by no route or command, and its two family-reinitialize methods refuse with `Covenant.ManualRecoveryRequired` pending the authenticated all-installation preflight and the token issuer a signed plan needs. A `search.guidance` of `RebuildRequired` therefore names a state, not a verb any caller can invoke. All six mapped inspection routes declare `ProtectedRead` authority, so the same middleware refuses an unauthorized read before its body is bound; each takes the lease its own body implies — a scoped read lease when the request names one Campaign, the installation read capability for an all-scopes request, because an all-scopes read crosses every Campaign and a scoped lease does not cover that — and hands it to `CovenantProtectedJsonResult<T>` on the same terms the mutation routes do. `/explain` is the exception: the service detaches its own lease, so the handler transfers what it is handed rather than acquiring anything. The four §8.20 lifecycle routes already require `LifecycleManage`.
>
> **Backup and restore status.** Backup, restore, and selective Session transfer are local operations and add no route here. The import side is covered by `IProtectedArtifactTransferStore`, which refuses any Covenant-derived source outright ([DESIGN §10.19.5](Arcanum.DESIGN.md#10195-selective-transfer-and-the-two-ports-it-had-to-build-first)).
>
> **Export status.** The export side is covered too. `GET /api/sessions/{id}/export` returns **403** `Covenant.PlaintextExportRefused` before a single content byte when the Session carries any Covenant-derived artifact, and `POST /api/campaigns/{id}/export` reports typed `exclusions` counts for the Covenant memory and tainted artifacts it did not carry. Both hold one conditional Covenant read lease from before the export graph through the last response byte, and both emit the protected header tuple of [§8.29](#829-x-arcanum-context-policy-and-the-covenant-pre-binding-boundary) while that arm is active. With the feature off neither takes a lease and the Campaign `exclusions` field is absent; the Session route still runs its two content-free ledger reads, so a Session that carries labels or taint written while the feature was on stays refused, and a Session with no ledger rows exports exactly as it did ([DESIGN §10.19.11](Arcanum.DESIGN.md#101911-refusing-tainted-plaintext-export-and-reporting-what-a-campaign-export-left-behind)).

Every shape is a named positional record registered with `ArcanumJsonContext`, every enum is string-only, and every request record owns a `Validate()` that produces the typed refusal below before anything reaches storage. `CovenantPublicContractInventory` is the closed list; `CovenantPublicContractInventoryTests` fails when a public Covenant `*Request`/`*Dto` is missing from it, when a declared entry names a type that no longer exists, when a shape carries an `object`/`JsonElement`/`JsonNode` member, or when a wire enum would accept an integer.

| Planned route | Request | Response |
|---|---|---|
| `POST /api/memory/covenant/list` | `CovenantListRequest` | `CovenantPageDto` |
| `POST /api/memory/covenant/query` | `CovenantQueryRequest` | `CovenantPageDto` |
| `POST /api/memory/covenant/detail` | `CovenantDetailRequest` | `CovenantDetailDto` |
| `POST /api/memory/covenant/versions` | `CovenantVersionsRequest` | `CovenantVersionPageDto` |
| `POST /api/memory/covenant/sources` | `CovenantSourcesRequest` | `CovenantSourcesDto` |
| `POST /api/memory/covenant/explain` | `CovenantExplainRequest` | `CovenantExplainDto` |
| `POST /api/memory/covenant/set/prepare` | `CovenantSetPrepareRequest` | `CovenantMutationPreflightDto` |
| `POST /api/memory/covenant/retire/prepare` | `CovenantRetirePrepareRequest` | `CovenantMutationPreflightDto` |
| `PUT /api/memory/covenant` | `CovenantSetRequest` | `CovenantMutationResultDto` |
| `POST /api/memory/covenant/retire` | `CovenantRetireRequest` | `CovenantMutationResultDto` |
| `POST /api/memory/covenant/correct/prepare` | `CovenantCorrectPrepareRequest` | `CovenantMutationPreflightDto` |
| `POST /api/memory/covenant/correct` | `CovenantCorrectRequest` | `CovenantMutationResultDto` |
| `POST /api/memory/covenant/curate/prepare` | `CovenantCurationPrepareRequest` | `CovenantCurationPreflightDto` |
| `POST /api/memory/covenant/curate` | `CovenantCurationRequest` | `CovenantCurationResultDto` |
| `POST /api/memory/covenant/erase/prepare` | `CovenantErasePrepareRequest` | `MemoryErasurePreflightDto` |
| `POST /api/memory/covenant/erase` | `CovenantEraseRequest` | `MemoryErasureResultDto` |
| `POST /api/memory/covenant/release` | `CovenantErasureReleaseRequest` | `MemoryErasureReleaseResultDto` |
| `POST /api/memory/covenant/schema/repair` | `CovenantSchemaRepairRequest` | `CovenantSchemaRepairResultDto` |
| `POST /api/memory/covenant/index/rebuild` | `CovenantIndexRebuildRequest` | `LongRunningOperationDto` (**202**) |
| `POST /api/memory/covenant/schema/reinitialize/prepare` | `CovenantFamilyReinitializePrepareRequest` | `CovenantFamilyReinitializePlanDto` |
| `POST /api/memory/covenant/schema/reinitialize` | `CovenantFamilyReinitializeApplyRequest` | `LongRunningOperationDto` (**202**) |
| `POST /api/campaigns/path/status` | `CampaignPathIdentityStatusRequest` | `CampaignPathIdentityStatusPageDto` |
| `POST /api/campaigns/{id}/path/prepare` | `CampaignPathPrepareRequest` | `CampaignPathIdentityPlanDto` |
| `POST /api/campaigns/{id}/path/apply` | `CampaignPathApplyRequest` | `CampaignPathIdentityResultDto` |
| `POST /api/sessions/campaign-binding/status` | `SessionCampaignBindingStatusRequest` | `SessionCampaignBindingStatusPageDto` |
| `POST /api/sessions/campaign-binding/prepare` | `SessionCampaignBindingPrepareRequest` | `SessionCampaignBindingPlanDto` |
| `POST /api/sessions/campaign-binding/apply` | `SessionCampaignBindingApplyRequest` | `SessionCampaignBindingResultDto` |

**Bodies, never query strings.** Campaign identities, Covenant keys, filters, search text, and cursors are body fields on every route above, including the read-only ones. A URL is the one part of a request that nothing redacts, and an access log keeps it forever.

**Search matching.** Search text is split into terms on whitespace, and every term must match: each is a **token prefix**, so `spor` finds an entry whose key or text holds the word `sport` and `ort` does not, because a term matches only where a word begins. Case does not matter in either mode (`привет` finds `Привет`, though the two modes fold it by different rules, below), and the characters `.`, `_` and `-` belong to a word (`global.sport` is one word, which `global` and `global.sp` find and `sport` does not). A word is made of letters, digits and those three characters; every other character separates words, including the vowel signs and other combining marks of scripts such as Devanagari, so `ताब` finds `किताब` just as `ort` finds nothing in `sport`. The one exception is the Latin combining accents (U+0300 to U+0331), which stay inside the word they follow. Both execution modes the page's `executionMode` reports answer by this rule, the full-text index and the bounded canonical fallback, so a hit does not appear while the accelerator is behind and vanish once it catches up. Four differences remain: the full-text index removes diacritics (`emile` finds `Émile`) where the fallback keeps them, and folds case by SQLite's Unicode rules where the fallback folds it by .NET's ordinal case-insensitive comparison; the full-text index reads a term that contains a separator (`a,b`, or a word with a vowel sign inside it such as `किताब`) as the phrase `a b` where the fallback matches the term as written; the fallback classifies characters with the Unicode tables of the .NET runtime where the full-text index uses SQLite's, so a character that Unicode added or reclassified after SQLite's tables were built (a rare script or a recent symbol) can be a word character in one mode and a separator in the other; and the fallback examines at most 2,048 heads and reports `truncated` when it could not examine them all.

**Frozen request bounds.** Search text ≤ 512 UTF-8 bytes and ≤ 32 terms (`Validation.InvalidQuery`); authored content 1–2,048 UTF-8 bytes (`Covenant.InvalidContent`); keys match `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9._-]{0,127}` (`Covenant.InvalidKey`); cursors and tokens ≤ 4,096 encoded characters, checked before decoding (`Covenant.InvalidCursor`); apply-request digests are exactly 64 hexadecimal characters (`Validation.InvalidBody`). `limit` is **clamped** to 1–200 with a default of 50 rather than refused, and the clamped value is exposed as `EffectiveLimit`. Byte bounds are UTF-8 bytes, not characters.

**Typed operational defaults.** The existing operator `set`/`correct` contracts accept the reserved keys `defaults.inference.temperature` (finite float in `[0, 2]`) and `defaults.inference.top-p` (finite float in `[0, 1]`). Each value is the entire authored content: one unsigned invariant decimal integer digit, optionally followed by `.` and one or more decimal digits. No sign, exponent, whitespace, or leading integer zeroes is accepted; fractional digits at the upper bound must all be zero. The existing 2,048-byte authored-content limit applies. Every other key beginning `defaults.` is refused as `Covenant.InvalidContent`, including unsupported consumers and security-policy targets. Only eligible, admitted Confirmed Global/Campaign versions can fill an absent inference `temperature` or `topP`; explicit request values take precedence. The catalog grants no authority over security settings, tools, egress, retention, or resource ceilings. A malformed persisted active Confirmed typed artifact refuses inference with `Covenant.IntegrityFailure`; ordinary successful disabled/unavailable/empty absences still proceed without defaults.

`CovenantExplainDto.operationalDefaults` is an array of eligible planned defaults. Each `CovenantExplainOperationalDefaultDto` contains `entryId`, exact immutable `versionId`, `revision`, `scope`, nullable `campaignId`, `kind` (`Temperature` or `TopP`), `consumer` (`InferenceSampling`), `policyVersion`, `decision` (`EligibleConfirmed`), and nullable `value`. Empty plans return `[]`. Values remain null unless `showContent:true`; the existing protected-read authority, lease, and response headers apply in both cases. Explain has no inference request or provider budget, so eligibility does not claim actual application: a later explicit request may override a default, or Confirmed admission may fail to fit. Plans and admission receipts bind exact version and typed policy provenance; the provider envelope binds the final effective options.

**Erasure fingerprints on a set.** A write of a key's Confirmed content (`set`, and `correct`, which commits as one) re-creates that exact scoped key, so it releases any erasure fingerprint recorded for it (§8.35). The preflight's `effect.releasesErasureFingerprint` discloses it before the operator confirms, and the result's `releasedErasureFingerprint` reports what the commit did, in the mutation's own transaction. Both are trailing, optional and tri-state: `true` when a fingerprint is (or would be) deleted, which lets agents author the key in that scope again; `false` when there is nothing to release, which is also what a retirement and every replay report; and `null` when the installation holds Covenant fingerprints this host cannot check, because no erasure key is latched or the rows were recorded under another key, so nothing is released and the operator runs `memory erasure status`. The flag is not bound into the token, and an operator write is never refused over a fingerprint: a commit the kernel refuses rolls the release back with it.

**A malformed target hash is a malformed body.** A `correct` or `correct/prepare` whose `targetRenderedHash` is not a 64-character hexadecimal digest is **400** `Validation.InvalidBody`, not `Covenant.InvalidScope`: the hash is a body field and the request named no scope wrongly. A well-formed hash that is not the live head's is **409** `Covenant.StaleSnapshot`.

**Both revisions on the preflight.** `CovenantMutationPreflightDto` reports `currentLaneRevision` — read from the head, never echoed — beside `expectedLaneRevision`, which is the number the request carried. The commit compares exactly those two and refuses a mismatch with `Covenant.RevisionConflict`, so a preview that reported only the head described a write no caller could tell would fail: every line of it true, and the refusal arriving after the operator had approved it. A client shows the disagreement instead of the confirmation.

**Capacity and lifecycle before confirmation.** `set/prepare`, `correct/prepare`, and `retire/prepare` evaluate the publication demand against the current scope and Section in the same admitted read snapshot. They use the commit's code-owned capacity calculations and exclude the head being replaced, so an ordinary replacement is not charged twice. A certain capacity refusal returns `Covenant.CapacityExceeded` before a token is issued; Section refusals explain that the operator can retire or shorten an entry, or shorten the replacement, then prepare again. Setting a retired Confirmed head without `reactivate:true` returns `Covenant.LifecycleConflict` with the reactivation remedy. Retiring an absent head is refused; retiring an existing tombstone remains a valid no-op. A successful preflight does not reserve room or authorize stale state: commit still checks capacity, lifecycle, authority, revisions, and epochs, while exact committed retries retain receipt-first behavior.

**Explicit scope, always.** `CovenantCursorScopeSelection` (`Global`, `Campaign`, `AllScopes`) has no default; an omitted value is `Covenant.InvalidScope`, so an installation-wide read is never the result of a missing field. `Global`/`AllScopes` must not carry `campaignId`; `Campaign` must. Detail carries `CovenantScope`, which has no all-scopes member at all, because the same key can exist in Global and in every Campaign. A `Set` carries no lane — Confirmed is the only lane an operator authors — and a Global Proposed retirement is `Covenant.InvalidScope`.

**Effective versus local state.** `CovenantHeadDto` reports `lifecycle` (local) and `shadow`/`materialization` (effective for the evaluated Campaign) separately. Both effective fields are `NotEvaluated` when the request supplied no `effectiveForCampaignId`. With an evaluation Campaign, Campaign heads report `shadow: NotShadowed` (only Global heads can be shadowed); Global heads report `NotEvaluated`. Retired heads and heads belonging to another Campaign report `materialization: Ineligible`; all other materialization is `NotEvaluated`. List/search are bounded metadata snapshots: their rows do not carry the complete eligible Campaign-head set, curation masks, fragment integrity, supported policy versions or turn admission budget. They therefore never claim `Eligible` or `ReviewOnly` from lane alone, and do not hydrate protected fragments or add reads to guess. The evaluation Campaign remains part of the filter digest a cursor binds.

**Detail is content-free by rule.** `CovenantDetailDto` carries `scope`, `campaignId`, `key`, `entryId`, the `confirmed` and `proposed` heads (`CovenantHeadDto`: version identity, lane revision, lifecycle, origin, authored and rendered hashes, byte cost, provenance count and digest, and timestamps, never the authored text), the key's dependency `keyEpoch`, `confirmedSources` and `proposedSources` (always `null`: provenance leaves are the `/sources` route's bounded read), and, as trailing members, `confirmedCuration` and `proposedCuration`. Each is a `CovenantCurationStateDto` (`isPinned`, `isMasked`, `revision`) for that lane in the requested scope, read at the key's binding epoch inside the same read transaction as the heads, so the heads and the curation describe one snapshot. A lane nobody curated reads `false`, `false`, `0`, the same state and revision a curation preflight reports, and a lane can be curated with no head: a Campaign mask over a Global key is curation in a Campaign that holds no entry for that key. A key with no entry in the scope answers **200** with a null `entryId` and null heads, and the curation members carry nothing an erasure could be read from: an erase removes the erased subject's curation, except a retained Campaign mask, which reads exactly as a mask recorded where no entry ever existed.

**Protected responses.** Every content-bearing Covenant read is written through `CovenantProtectedJsonResult<T>` or `CovenantProtectedStreamResult`: the operation lease is revalidated immediately before the first byte, the response carries the exact tuple in [§8.29](#829-x-arcanum-context-policy-and-the-covenant-pre-binding-boundary) with any `ETag` and `Last-Modified` removed, and the lease is released after serialization completes. A conditional revalidation of protected content is a 304 on data the installation may since have erased.

**Idempotency.** Covenant set and retirement use their canonical `mutationId` and the durable receipt ledger as the sole replay mechanism; a supplied HTTP `Idempotency-Key` has no semantic effect on these routes. Long-running Covenant operations bind a caller-generated `operationId` plus a stable `applyRequestDigest`: the same pair replays the durable operation, a different digest under the same id is `Security.IdempotencyConflict`, and neither depends on the process that issued the original **202** still being alive.

`MemorySearchScope.All` continues to exclude Covenant. Covenant content search, sources, and explain use the typed routes above and always require Covenant read authority, so no existing default `All` request silently gains privilege.

### 8.29 `X-Arcanum-Context-Policy` and the Covenant pre-binding boundary

The authenticated boundary every dedicated Covenant management route sits behind applies today to every `/api` and `/v1` route, including the existing `/api/data` Covenant reset and factory lifecycle. The mutation and inspection routes of §8.28 are registered behind it, including protected free-text query; repair, rebuild, Campaign path, and Session binding are not registered at all.

**Decision order.** API-key authentication → Grimoire request admission (§8.31) → installation-reset recovery admission → `X-Arcanum-Context-Policy` validation → Covenant authority issuance → body-size enforcement → source-generated binding. Everything before "body-size enforcement" happens with **zero bytes of the request body read**. Authentication is strictly first: a wrong key presented with a malformed policy header returns **401**, never 400, because a 400 would tell an unauthenticated caller that they reached a real route and that only their header spelling was wrong.

**The header.**

| Sent | Result |
|---|---|
| absent | `Default` — durable context may be injected |
| exactly `none` (one header, lowercase ASCII) | `None` — durable context is suppressed for this request; the server echoes `X-Arcanum-Context-Policy: none` |
| `NONE`, `None`, ` none`, `none `, `""`, `none,none`, any other value | **400** `Covenant.InvalidScope`, before binding |
| the header repeated | **400** `Covenant.InvalidScope` — refused, never merged |
| any value on a route that cannot inject Covenant content | **400** `Covenant.InvalidScope` |

Repetition is refused rather than merged because every merge rule — first wins, last wins, comma-join — is a guess about which of two disagreeing senders meant it. A route that cannot inject context refuses the header rather than ignoring it, because a caller that sent `none` to a route that silently discarded it believes it suppressed content it in fact sent. `X-Arcanum-Context-Policy` is in the CORS exposed-header set so a browser client can read back the decision instead of assuming it.

**Idempotency.** The parsed policy is part of an `Idempotency-Key` request's fingerprint (DESIGN §11.17): the same key and body sent once without the header and once with `none` is **409** `Security.IdempotencyConflict`, never a replay of the first answer, because the first answer may carry durable memory the retry asked to exclude (or lack memory the retry asked for). The fingerprint carries the policy for every request, the default included, so a claim recorded before this was folded in reads as a conflict across an upgrade rather than as a replay. A response marked protected is never stored for replay: its claim is abandoned, so a retry with the same key runs again and receives its own protected headers rather than a cached body without them.

Routes that accept the header today: `PostIntelligencePing`, `PostIntelligencePingStream`, `PostIntelligenceContextInspect`, `TestPrompt`, `Prompt_Execute`, `Prompt_ExecuteStream`, `Spell_Execute`, `Spell_ExecuteStream`, `Spell_Cast`. Every one of those nine builds its invocation context from a resolved Campaign, which is what makes the header mean something on it, and `CovenantContextRouteInventoryTests` fails in both directions when the set changes. `PostOpenAiChatCompletions` accepted the header until it was withdrawn: both of its handlers build a stateless context with no Campaign, so the context provider answers `Absent(NoCampaign)` before reading any Covenant state and nothing was ever going to be injected there. It now refuses the header under the row above, which is the honest answer — a caller that sent `none` to it was being told it had suppressed something.

**The protected response tuple.** Every protected success *and* failure emits exactly:

```text
Cache-Control: no-store, private
Pragma: no-cache
Expires: 0
```

with `ETag` and `Last-Modified` removed. `private` survives intermediaries that treat an unqualified `no-store` as advisory; `Pragma` and `Expires` cover HTTP/1.0-era caches that ignore `Cache-Control`. It is applied on response start, so it also covers framework-generated refusals no handler wrote. Streaming responses keep their `Cache-Control: no-cache` default when they are *not* protected and are never downgraded when they are — headers cannot be corrected after the first byte.

**Authority.** A route declares exactly one `CovenantAuthorityRequirement`; the boundary issues a context bound to it and to the current clean authority epoch, and an endpoint filter rechecks — never reissues — that epoch after binding. A missing context is **403** `Covenant.ForbiddenAuthority`; an epoch that moved (host-tools taint, key rotation) is **503** `Covenant.OperatorAuthorityUnavailable`. `ProtectedRead` cannot be declared as an operator requirement, so a context minted for an inspection page can never authorize a mutation.

### 8.30 Saga curation (`/api/memory/saga/`)

Six routes over one Saga memory at a time, mapped onto the same authenticated `/api` group as everything else in this chapter and deliberately not onto `/v1`, which maps its own routes and knows nothing about Saga curation. They sit beside the Covenant's curation surface (§8.28) and the Lexicon's delete rather than under `/api/saga`: `/api/saga` is the store's own read-and-delete surface and answers what is in there, while these answer what one memory is and what the operator has decided about it (DESIGN §21.13).

The detail route answers `ApiResponse<SagaMemoryDetail>`; the five write routes answer `ApiResponse<SagaCurationResult>`, which carries the outcome beside the memory the call left behind. That extra field is load-bearing, because three outcomes write nothing and are still successes.

**`eligibility` is written as its name.** `SagaRetrievalEligibility` is `Eligible`, `Retired`, `OwnershipUnresolved`, `EmbeddingMissing`, `Consolidated`, `Dormant`, or `OutsideValidity` (§8.36). It was previously written as its number (`1` to `4`, in that order); it is now written as its name (`"eligibility":"Retired"`) on `GET /api/memory/saga/{id}`, inside every write route's `detail`, and on the Saga review items' current snapshot (§8.34). That changes the shape of `arcanum memory saga show --json`, of the Saga curation verbs' `--json`, and of `arcanum memory saga review list --json` the same way: a client that matched the number matches the name instead. No request body carries this value.

**One classifier, under the policy retrieval runs.** Eligibility is decided in one place for the detail route, the review queue, and `POST /api/memory/search` (§1): retired first, then unresolved ownership, then exact-current-source Long Rest suppression (`Consolidated`), then Long Rest valid-time exclusion (`OutsideValidity`) and read-time dormancy (`Dormant`) while its gate is enabled, then a missing embedding. Unresolved ownership is a rung only while Campaign scoping is on (`Arcanum:Features:CampaignScopedMemory`). With it off, a turn ranks every embedded memory whoever owns it, so a memory whose owning Session's binding never resolved reads `Eligible` (or `EmbeddingMissing`), and `GET /api/memory/explain` counts it the same way. With it on, that memory reads `OwnershipUnresolved` on the detail route and in the review queue; search and explain, which narrow by the ownership a turn ranks by, neither return it nor count it.

| Route | Body | Refusals |
|---|---|---|
| `GET /api/memory/saga/{id}` | — | **404** `Saga.NotFound` |
| `POST /api/memory/saga/{id}/correct` | `SagaCorrectRequest` — `expectedContentHash`, `content` | **404** `Saga.NotFound`; **409** `Saga.StaleContent`; **409** `Saga.AlreadyRetired`; **503** `Saga.EmbeddingUnavailable`; **400** `Validation.InvalidFields` for a hash that is not a 64-character hexadecimal digest; **400** `Validation.InvalidBody` for a missing hash or absent content |
| `POST /api/memory/saga/{id}/retire` | `SagaRetireRequest` — `expectedContentHash` | **404** `Saga.NotFound`; **409** `Saga.StaleContent`; **400** `Validation.InvalidFields` / `Validation.InvalidBody` as above |
| `POST /api/memory/saga/{id}/reinstate` | `SagaReinstateRequest` — `expectedContentHash` | **404** `Saga.NotFound`; **409** `Saga.StaleContent`; **503** `Saga.EmbeddingUnavailable`; **400** `Validation.InvalidFields` / `Validation.InvalidBody` as above |
| `POST /api/memory/saga/{id}/pin` | none | **404** `Saga.NotFound` |
| `POST /api/memory/saga/{id}/unpin` | none | **404** `Saga.NotFound` |

**`Saga.EmbeddingUnavailable` also covers a wrong-width vector.** `correct` and `reinstate` embed before the store is called, and a provider that answers a vector whose length is not the clamped `Arcanum:Integrations:Embeddings:Dimensions` (a model swapped without `embeddings reset`, or a provider that ignores the requested width) is refused with **503** `Saga.EmbeddingUnavailable`, whose message names the two widths, rather than reaching the store's dimension guard and surfacing as an unhandled **500**. **A cancelled request does not undo the verb.** The projection the response carries is composed after the store has committed, on `CancellationToken.None`, so a caller that disconnects between the commit and the read still gets (or, once gone, would have got) the outcome the store reported, never a cancellation that says a durable change did not happen.

**Three successes write nothing, and each says which it was.** Retiring a memory that is already retired, reinstating one that is not retired, and correcting one to the text it already holds all answer **200** with the outcome kind that names what happened — the operator asked for a state and the memory is in it. Only correction meets `Saga.AlreadyRetired`, and only because a retired memory is reinstated before it is corrected rather than corrected in place; that check runs before any content comparison, so it is the answer whatever text the correction carried. `ArcanumErrorMapper` owns the status codes and `ISagaCurationService` owns which outcomes are errors, per verb; neither decision is restated at the route.

**A correction can release an erasure fingerprint.** `SagaCurationResult` carries a trailing optional `releasedErasureFingerprint`. A correction whose new content is content the operator erased in the memory's own scope makes that identity live again and deletes its fingerprint in the same transaction, so extraction may write that content there again: `true`. On a host-tools-tainted installation, which issues no release authority, the correction still lands but releases nothing and reports `false` (§8.34). `false` means there was nothing to release, and every other verb and outcome reports it. `null` means the store holds Saga fingerprints this host could not check, because no erasure key is latched, the rows were recorded under another key, or the memory's stored scope names no identity a fingerprint can describe; the correction still succeeds and the operator runs `memory erasure status`. A correction is never refused over a fingerprint (§8.35).

**`expectedContentHash` is proof, not a courtesy.** It is compared inside the write transaction, against the same digest function `SagaMemoryDetail.ContentHash` publishes, so a caller quotes a value the host computed rather than reproducing the function. Pin and unpin take no hash and no body at all: a pin is not a statement about what a memory says, and requiring proof of the text would make pinning fail after an unrelated correction.

Bodies are read through `ApiRequestJson` rather than as bound parameters, so the route itself answers malformed JSON and a wrong media type with the envelope and a code (§8.1). A bound-body route is answered the same way by the host's exception handler (§8.1), so the empty **400** and **415** minimal-API binding writes no longer reach a client on either read path. A body that never reached the curation service is refused as `Validation.InvalidBody`, which names the envelope rather than the verb: none of these routes can report a memory, an outcome, or a store from a body it could not read.

---

### 8.31 Grimoire request admission and the maintenance `503`

**Connection faults are separate.** A factory acquisition that fails because the native runtime, admission machinery, or storage cannot open or initialize the Grimoire returns **503** `Covenant.Unavailable` on `/api`, or an OpenAI error with type `service_unavailable` and code `grimoire_unavailable` on `/v1`. Its message contains only a fixed stage and exception classification, numeric SQLite codes when available, and the fixed `Grimoire.NativeRuntimeUnavailable` code for native loading failure. Without typed diagnostic metadata it says “Grimoire connection unavailable.” It never repeats arbitrary error or exception text, paths, connection strings, passphrases, or SQL. `GET /api/health` reports the Grimoire component `Unhealthy` with that safe detail. These errors do not claim maintenance ownership or authorize maintenance-only deferral. A response already started is not rewritten.

An installation can take its own database offline — a Covenant erasure, a factory reset — and for the length of that transformation nothing ordinary may hold the Grimoire open. This section is what a caller sees while that is true.

**Where it decides.** Immediately after the authentication stage and before installation-reset admission, Covenant pre-binding, body-size enforcement, binding, and the endpoint. It runs in both branches of the pre-binding gate, so an anonymous or peer-authenticated route under `/api` is decided by path authority exactly like an authenticated one. In the anonymous branch the recovery-mode 404-hide still decides first: a route that must stay indistinguishably unavailable while a factory reset is recovering cannot begin announcing that this host exists and its database is busy. "After authentication" means after the authentication *stage*, not after a successful key check: a wrong key is still **401**, never **503**, because a **503** would confirm to an unauthenticated caller that they reached a real route.

**What it selects.** Whole path segments, case-insensitively, under `/api` and `/v1` — never API-key metadata. That is the entire exclusion rule and it has no list to drift: `/metrics` is outside both prefixes, `/apiary` is not inside `/api`, and `/v10` is not inside `/v1`. A request that matched no route this host mapped is left alone, so `404`, `405`, and `415` stay reachable without a key.

**What it exempts.** Exactly `GET /api/presence`, `GET /api/health`, and `POST /api/server/quit`. Presence is the bodyless local proof used before a client reads or sends a credential; closing it would force the launcher to choose between disclosing the key to an unverified listener and spawning a second host onto an occupied port. Health already answers a closed gate better than a refusal could — its probe reports an Unhealthy component naming only an exception type, inside the **503**-with-`IsSuccess: true` envelope §1 documents. Quit is the shutdown step of the factory-reset sequence and opens nothing. None is a way past the gate: health and quit are refused by the database itself if they touch it, while presence contains no database operation or state. The factory-reset route is deliberately **not** exempt — it is the request maintenance runs for, and it holds an admission lease so the transformation can release it from its own drain.

**What it answers.**

| Surface | Status | Body |
|---|---|---|
| `/api/**` | **503** | source-generated `ApiResponse<string>` with `IsSuccess: false` and code `Grimoire.MaintenanceUnavailable` |
| `/v1/**` | **503** | source-generated OpenAI error envelope with type `service_unavailable` and code `grimoire_maintenance` |

Both carry the same fixed sentence — "The Grimoire is temporarily unavailable while maintenance owns connection admission." — and nothing else. No path, owner, operation id, phase, generation, or native detail appears in the body or in any log line, and the refusal is never logged at `Error`: it is expected control flow, not a fault. Both refusals carry the `no-store, private` / `Pragma: no-cache` / `Expires: 0` tuple with `ETag` and `Last-Modified` removed, because a cached maintenance answer outlives the window that produced it. No `Retry-After` accompanies either: a transformation may compact a large catalog or park for manual recovery, and a delay a client treats as a promise and the host cannot keep is worse than none.

The same pair is written for a request that was already running when admission closed under it, so an operator sees one answer for one condition rather than two. That second answer is produced by the admission stage itself, wrapping the rest of the pipeline, rather than left to the framework's exception handling: that middleware clears cache headers through a response-start callback registered before any handler runs, and those callbacks run last-registered-first, so nothing downstream can put back the exact tuple above. Wrapping there also covers the surfaces admission deliberately does not gate — `/metrics` queries the database on every scrape, and would otherwise answer **500** and be logged at Error for the length of the window. A response whose first byte has already left is never rewritten, and the refusal is not reported as a fault when it cannot be written.

**What it guarantees.** Every matched, non-exempt `/api` or `/v1` request either owns an admission lease for the whole of its request scope — held past the pooled database context and past every response-completion writer — or receives the refusal above before any endpoint work happens, with **zero bytes of the request body read**. Both bodies serialize through explicit source-generated `JsonTypeInfo`, so neither adds a reflection path.

No route, wire DTO, CLI verb or option, configuration key, schema/DDL, or migration changed to deliver this.

---

### 8.32 Streaming routes in a maintenance window

§8.31 is what a request that has not started receives. This section is what a response that has
already started does, because a stream whose first byte has left cannot be given a `503`: its status
and headers are settled, and a half-written body cannot become an envelope. Every streaming route is
therefore classified, and the class decides what a maintenance window does to it.

| Route | Class | Grimoire authority | Framing |
|---|---|---|---|
| `GET /api/events/daemon` | quiesceable | none | SSE |
| `GET /api/events/mcp` | quiesceable | none | SSE |
| `GET /api/events/logs` | quiesceable | none | SSE |
| `GET /api/sessions/{id}/stream` | quiesceable | live | SSE |
| `GET /api/apprentices/{id}/chronicle` | quiesceable | live | SSE |
| `POST /api/intelligence/ping-stream` | billable drain | live | NDJSON |
| `POST /api/prompts/{id}/execute-stream` | billable drain | live | NDJSON |
| `POST /api/spells/{name}/execute-stream` | billable drain | live | NDJSON |
| `POST /api/web/research` | billable drain | live | NDJSON |
| `POST /v1/chat/completions` (`stream: true`) | billable drain | live | SSE |
| `GET /api/sessions/{id}/attachments/{attachmentId}/content` | finite drain | live | bytes |
| `GET /v1/files/{id}/content` | finite drain | live | bytes |
| The A2A JSON-RPC server surface | billable drain | live | package-owned SSE |

**The five quiesceable routes stop at a complete frame boundary.** The frame already being written is
finished, no further data frame or `: keep-alive` comment is written, the producer is cancelled and
observed, enumerators and scopes are disposed, and the body ends with the terminal `data: [DONE]`
frame. HTTP status stays **200**, because it left with the first byte. The two routes that replay
before going live stop between replayed frames as well: the session stream stops between Entries and
skips its `data: {"type":"live"}` sentinel, and the Chronicle stops between its plan, escalation, and
step-start frames. Both then skip their buffered drain. `/api/events/logs` writes no `: connected`
comment if the window began before it got there. Those per-route guards fall through to the shared
writer, so a stream that stopped early still ends on the one terminal frame the writer owns; the
frame-boundary behaviour itself is pinned at that writer, and driving each route's replay phase
against a live transition belongs to the full-host race coverage that closes the epic.

A client cannot distinguish this ending from any other deliberate one, and that is intentional:
`[DONE]` already means "the server ended this stream" on all five, so `arcanum watch` exits cleanly
instead of reporting a disconnect that did not happen. A reconnect attempt made while the window is
still open receives the §8.31 **503**.

**Every other stream is drained, never cut.** A billable stream has already spent money on the
caller's behalf by the time a transition begins, so cutting it would charge for an answer nobody
receives; a finite one ends on its own in bounded time and holds no producer to stop. Both hold an
ordinary request lease, which the transition waits out — so a transition begun while one is open can
still reach its work-drain timeout and refuse. That refusal is the intended answer rather than a
regression: nothing is erased, ordinary admission stays open, and the operator retries once the
stream has finished.

No route, request or response shape, media type, frame vocabulary, sentinel, CLI verb, configuration
key, or database schema changed to deliver this.

---

### 8.33 Lexicon curation (`/api/memory/lexicon/`)

The six curation POSTs require the normal API authentication and JSON request media type. They remain mapped with prompt-time Lexicon or ordinary Annals capture disabled. Responses use `ApiResponse<T>` with source-generated camelCase fields. The three Lexicon enums — `LexiconScopeKind`, `LexiconRetrievalEligibility`, and `LexiconCurationOutcomeKind` — accept recognized string names, never numeric spellings. Inherited Annals enums retain numeric JSON values: a format-2 assertion head contains `"operation":1` and `"contentHashFormat":2`, not symbolic strings such as `"Assert"` or `"LexiconStructuredSnapshot"`; those strings fail deserialization. Existing `GET /api/memory/lexicon/{name}` remains effective active lookup. `DELETE` remains an exact-scope delete, including retired rows. It records no erasure fingerprint, so extraction or an agent may write the name again; only `erase` (§8.35) suppresses and verifies. POST `show` is exact inspection: absence in a Campaign is 404 even when Global has the same name.

| POST path | Request body | Successful data |
|---|---|---|
| `/api/memory/lexicon/show` | `LexiconShowRequest`: `name`, `scope` | `LexiconEntryDetail` |
| `/api/memory/lexicon/correct` | `LexiconCorrectRequest`: `target`, `content` containing `type` and complete `facts` array | `LexiconCurationResult` |
| `/api/memory/lexicon/retire` | `LexiconRetireRequest`: `target` | `LexiconCurationResult` |
| `/api/memory/lexicon/reinstate` | `LexiconReinstateRequest`: `target` | `LexiconCurationResult` |
| `/api/memory/lexicon/pin` | `LexiconPinRequest`: `target` | `LexiconCurationResult` |
| `/api/memory/lexicon/unpin` | `LexiconUnpinRequest`: `target` | `LexiconCurationResult` |
| `/api/memory/lexicon/erase/prepare` | `LexiconErasePrepareRequest`: `target`, `mutationId`. Requires `SensitivityRetentionPurge`; §8.35 | `MemoryErasurePreflightDto` |
| `/api/memory/lexicon/erase` | `LexiconEraseRequest`: `target`, `mutationId`, `preflightToken`. Requires `SensitivityRetentionPurge`; §8.35 | `MemoryErasureResultDto` |

Exact Global show:

```json
{"name":"Operator","scope":{"kind":"Global","campaignId":null}}
```

For Campaign scope use `{"kind":"Campaign","campaignId":"a nonempty Campaign GUID"}`. Global forbids a Campaign id; Campaign requires one. Names are trimmed and case-normalized by the server. Correction preserves the entry's id, name, normalized name, and scope. Its `content` shape is `{"type":"Person","facts":["Uses dark mode.","Prefers concise replies."]}`; this replaces the entire ordered fact set. Type and facts are trimmed, empty facts dropped, exact duplicates removed retaining their first position, and at least one fact must remain. A retired entry must be reinstated before correction.

`LexiconEntryDetail` contains `entry`, `scope`, nullable `currentOrigin`, `lifecycle`, `eligibility` (`Eligible` or `Retired`), `curationGeneration`, `snapshotDigest`, `target`, `annalHistory`, and `historicalFactProvenance`. `entry` contains id/name/type/facts/updatedAt, current fact provenance and scope, and lifecycle/generation/eligibility fields. `lifecycle` contains nullable `retiredAtUtc` and `pinnedAtUtc`. History is immutable claim evidence, not old fact text. Historical source records contain `annalVersionId`, `factOrdinal`, source Session/attachment identity, logical key, attachment version/content hash, materialization time, and source type; no superseded fact or per-fact hash is retained.

Every mutation submits the `target` exactly as returned by show. It binds `scope`, `normalizedName`, `entryId`, `curationGeneration`, `snapshotDigest`, `lifecycle`, `annalHead`, and `sensitivityLabel`. The server compares every field against one live write snapshot and independently verifies row/head/label consistency. Clients must not recompute or omit evidence. A successful mutation returns `{"outcome":"Applied","entry":{...}}` where `entry` is the resulting full `LexiconEntryDetail`. Outcomes are `Applied`, `Unchanged`, `AlreadyRetired`, `NotRetired`, `AlreadyPinned`, and `NotPinned`, all HTTP 200. The latter five write nothing. They still require a current exact target: retrying an already applied request with its predecessor target returns 409, not a successful replay.

The two evidence arms are explicit and presence-sensitive:

| Target arm | `isPresent: false` | `isPresent: true` |
|---|---|---|
| `annalHead` | All other fields null or omitted. | Requires `claimId`, `versionId`, positive `revision`, numeric `operation` (1 = Assert, 2 = Correct, 3 = Retire), numeric `contentHashFormat` (1 = LegacyStoreDigest, 2 = LexiconStructuredSnapshot), and a SHA-256 hex `contentHash` for Assert/Correct. Retire requires a null content hash. |
| `sensitivityLabel` | All other fields null or omitted. | Requires nonempty `labelId`, positive `artifactRevision`, SHA-256 hex `artifactContentDigest`, and complete `generationProvenance` under the existing Covenant format. |

Both arm objects and their `isPresent` properties are required. Omission is not explicit false. Missing or null arm objects, incomplete present-arm values, and contradictory absent-arm values that deserialize successfully produce `Lexicon.InvalidCurationTarget`. An omitted `isPresent` property or another deserialization failure produces `Validation.InvalidBody`. A claimless row is valid, with `annalHead.isPresent: false`, null origin, and empty history. All such invalid evidence is refused before mutation. Format-2 snapshot bytes and the distinct derived-artifact label digest are specified in DESIGN §10.6.3.

| Status | Typed error or meaning |
|---|---|
| 400 | `Lexicon.InvalidName`, `Lexicon.InvalidFact`, `Lexicon.InvalidScope`, `Lexicon.InvalidCurationTarget`, `Lexicon.InvalidReplacement`. Missing/null evidence arms and deserialized incomplete or contradictory evidence produce `Lexicon.InvalidCurationTarget`; omitted `isPresent`, malformed JSON, and other deserialization failures produce `Validation.InvalidBody`. |
| 401 | Missing or invalid API authentication. |
| 403 | `Lexicon.ProtectedMutationRefused`; existing Covenant authorization failures retain their established mapping. |
| 404 | `Lexicon.NotFound` for the exact subject. |
| 409 | `Lexicon.StaleCurationTarget`, `Lexicon.RetiredMutationRefused`, `Lexicon.CurationGenerationExhausted`, `Lexicon.ArtifactRevisionExhausted`. |
| 415 | Missing or unsupported Content-Type: `Validation.UnsupportedMediaType`. |
| 500 | `Lexicon.CurationIntegrityFailed`, `Lexicon.WriteFailed`, `Lexicon.SearchFailed`; no partial durable publication. |
| 503 | `Lexicon.CurationUnavailable` for the five mutation routes while committed Core schema is below v11. Retry after the transition completes; the error message is `Lexicon curation is temporarily unavailable while the Core schema transition completes.` |

During a resumable Core transition, ordinary Lexicon lookup, list/search, scribe, hard deletion, Annals inspection, and retention continue using the legacy schema. Exact show of an unlabeled legacy row reports null retirement/pin instants, generation 1, format-1 Annals history, and empty historical fact provenance. Its exact target remains usable after v11 completes if the row and evidence have not changed. Protected mutation authority is checked first: denied or stale Covenant authority keeps its existing refusal, while an authorized request reaches the retryable curation-unavailable response. There is no endpoint capability preflight that bypasses that ordering. A manually introduced legacy protected label grants no content access without authority, and even valid authority cannot make an unverifiable legacy digest acceptable; inspection fails closed rather than guessing a digest grammar.

Shared body-size/time and Covenant availability/maintenance refusals keep their existing statuses (§8.23). Protected exact show acquires the matching Global or Campaign read lease; mutations carrying present sensitivity evidence acquire that owner's write lease before service invocation. Effective Campaign GET and arbitrary-scope list/search/sources/explain use installation read authority. The row and label are verified in one snapshot, authority is revalidated before serialization, and the result owns the lease until the last byte. Protected responses send `Cache-Control: no-store, private`, `Pragma: no-cache`, `Expires: 0`, and no `ETag` or `Last-Modified`. Unlabeled results use ordinary response behavior. Every curation route declares conditional-read metadata; exactly the five mutation routes also declare the conditional exact-write marker. Metadata alone grants no authority.

Operator list and generic lexical search may include retired entries with lifecycle/eligibility markers. Status and sources count retained rows. Explain counts only `Eligible` Lexicon entries as possible next-turn sources; it does not promise a prompt match. Pinning affects automatic retention, not explicit erasure, retirement, correction, or reset. In retention plans (§8.20), `lexiconCuration` is `{"pinnedRows":N,"pinnedRowsExemptFromPlan":N}`: all retained pins and the pins otherwise old enough for an enabled Lexicon pruning rule. It exists only on `DataRetentionPlan`, not `DataRetentionApplyResult`. Apply rechecks inside the deletion transaction; a later pin preserves that candidate and appears in the apply result's conflicts as `Data.PinnedAfterPlanning`. Independent candidates may continue, while reconciliation and recovery retain the preserved boundary. No new configuration key is required.

---

### 8.34 Exact-version memory review (`/api/memory/{store}/review/`)

Each durable memory store exposes exactly three authenticated static POSTs: `list`, `prepare`, and `apply`. Scope selectors, Campaign ids, Covenant lanes, observations, and cursors are typed body fields and never URL segments. Saga uses `SagaMemoryScopeKind` plus nullable `campaignId`; Lexicon uses `LexiconCurationScope`; Covenant uses `CovenantScope`, nullable `campaignId`, and one required `CovenantLane`. Global forbids a Campaign id, Campaign requires one, and Saga alone admits the exact `LegacyUnresolved` scope. Page and bulk lengths are refused outside 1–50 rather than clamped.

| Suffix | Store-specific request | Successful data |
|---|---|---|
| `list` | `*ReviewListRequest` with exact scope, `limit`, and optional authenticated `cursor` | `*ReviewPageDto` with current-head unreviewed items, durable marker, frozen upper frontier, and optional continuation |
| `prepare` | `*ReviewBulkPrepareRequest` with nonempty `requestId`, one `action`, and 1–50 store-specific decisions | `MemoryReviewBulkPlanDto` with the exact ordered targets and five-minute `preparedPlanToken` |
| `apply` | `*ReviewBulkApplyRequest` repeating the complete prepare request plus its `preparedPlanToken` | `MemoryReviewBulkResultDto`, including per-item outcomes, reviewed-through marker, and `replayed` |

The first list page freezes the exact queue frontier; its continuation and every observation token authenticate the store, canonical scope, marker generation/revision, lower and upper event frontiers, and exact event/version identity. Pages are newest first. A continuation from another store, scope, lane, marker generation, or request is invalid. Queue items report assertion origin, source, scope, and immutable version, plus a bounded current snapshot with the content and lifecycle needed to review it. Saga claim history and Lexicon Annals/fact-provenance histories stay on their exact `show` surfaces rather than multiplying a 50-item queue page by an unbounded nested history. A version superseded after observation is stale: every action, including `Confirm`, refuses it, and a refreshed queue presents the actionable current head instead.

Observation, continuation cursor, and prepared-plan tokens each expire five minutes after issuance and are signed with an unexported per-process key. Restarting the host process invalidates them; constructing another codec or restarting a host inside the same process does not rotate that key. Preparing a plan does not renew its observation tokens. `CovenantReviewPageDto.expiresAtUtc` is an additive nullable UTC field: the earliest actual expiry among the observations and continuation issued on that page, or `null` when the page issued no tokens. A Covenant `MemoryReviewBulkPlanDto.expiresAtUtc` is the earliest deadline among its plan token and every observation it needs, while `issuedAtUtc` is the actual plan-token issuance time. These UTC displays describe the monotonic lifetime against the clock at issuance or authenticated read; changing the wall clock cannot extend token validity.

Every newly committed Covenant review apply advances the marker revision for its exact scope and lane, invalidating other observations, cursors, and prepared plans from that queue, even for untouched items. Refresh between batches with different actions; do not accumulate observation tokens for later use. Token, stale, and unseen refusals direct the operator to `Run arcanum memory covenant review list again`. Per-decision refusals name the one-based decision ordinal and, only after successful token authentication and validation, its event sequence. Invalid tokens never disclose an inferred event, raw token, replacement content, or exception text. Batch rejection remains all-or-nothing.

Actions are `Confirm`, `Correct`, `Retire`, `Pin`, and `Unpin`. All decisions in a request share that one action and exact store/scope/lane. `Correct` requires replacement content for every item; all other actions forbid it. Preparation rejects duplicate, tampered, expired, unseen, wrong-scope, mixed-frontier, or non-current observations before issuing a plan. Apply revalidates the ordered request digest, marker state, exact versions, and authority inside the owning store's transaction. The complete batch commits or none does. A committed exact replay returns the durable result even after the process plan token expired; reuse of the request identity for a different decision set is **409**.

**Per-item outcomes are one closed set.** `MemoryReviewBulkItemResultDto.outcome` is, for every store, exactly one of `Confirmed`, `Corrected`, `Retired`, `Pinned` or `Unpinned` (the action did what it was asked), or, when the memory was already in the state the action asked for and nothing was written, the one spelling that action has in every store: `Unchanged` (a `Correct` carried the content already held), `AlreadyRetired` (`Retire`), `AlreadyPinned` (`Pin`) or `NotPinned` (`Unpin`). A `Confirm` is always recorded, so it has no such outcome. Always in this PascalCase spelling. `AutoAcknowledged` is the outcome a Lexicon receipt records for the replacement version a correction acknowledges in the same transaction; it never appears in a response. Earlier builds reported the Lexicon's applied outcomes in lowercase (`acknowledged`, `corrected`, `retired`, `pinned`, `unpinned`) and Covenant's no-op outcomes as one word, `NoChange`, whatever the action; a receipt persisted either way still replays and reports the closed spelling for its action.

Each `MemoryReviewBulkItemResultDto` also carries a trailing optional `releasedErasureFingerprint`. A Saga or Covenant `Correct` writes its replacement as the operator, so when an erasure fingerprint is recorded for the replacement's content (Saga) or the item's key (Covenant) in the item's own scope it is deleted in the review's transaction and the item reports `true`; `null` means the store holds fingerprints this host could not check, so nothing was released and the operator runs `memory erasure status`.
